Outcomes

70 names carry a lower review score after the pass than before, and 7 worked names sit below the 0.85 floor; every one of them is held rather than accepted, as the verify phase asserts, and the lowest score after the pass is 0.8500. A revised definition scores lower whenever the reviewer weighs the stored sentences differently, so a drop is recorded rather than treated as a defect; the floor is what holds a name back.

Score coverage: 129 of 173 worked names carry a score before the edit and 158 carry one after. The remainder are names whose earlier record carried no score, or whose edit never reached a scored candidate; both are counted here rather than left implicit in the worked table.

argon_density

description before

Charge-state-summed number density of argon ions, including Ar+ through Ar18+ and excluding neutral argon; local and averaged forms share this definition.

description after

Number density of argon ions across all charged ionization states, excluding neutral argon. It is the general argon-ion concentration shared by its local and spatially averaged forms.

documentation before

Argon density is the number of argon ions per unit volume, summed over all ionization stages from singly charged Ar+ through fully stripped Ar18+. Neutral argon is excluded. $$ n_{\mathrm{Ar}} = \sum_{Z=1}^{18} n_{\mathrm{Ar}^{Z+}} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Ar}}$ is the total argon-ion number density, $Z$ is the integer argon charge state, and $n_{\mathrm{Ar}^{Z+}}$ is the number density of argon ions in that charge state. The definition applies to a local value and to averages over a specified chord or confined plasma volume, including the [line-averaged argon density](name:line_averaged_argon_density) and [volume-averaged argon density](name:volume_averaged_argon_density). It is distinct from argon count, which represents an equivalent-electron inventory rather than particles per volume.

documentation after

Number density of argon. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded. The charge-state sum is $$ n_{\mathrm{Ar}} = \sum_{q=1}^{18} n_{\mathrm{Ar}^{q+}} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Ar}}$ is the argon-ion number density, $q$ is the positive integer charge-state index, $n_{\mathrm{Ar}^{q+}}$ is the number density of argon ions in charge state $q$, and 18 is the atomic number of argon. The quantity may be specified locally or as a spatial average, with the averaging domain set by the specific form. The [line-averaged argon density](name:line_averaged_argon_density) is the average along a specified plasma path, and the [volume-averaged argon density](name:volume_averaged_argon_density) is the average over the confined plasma volume. Both are averages of the local density, not unnormalized line or volume integrals, and both keep the same charge-state-summed ion population. This quantity is not the argon inventory or count, which is an amount rather than a concentration per unit volume, and it is not an electron density or a density of any other species.

beryllium_density

description before

Local number density of beryllium impurity ions per unit plasma volume, summed over all ionization states from singly ionized to fully stripped (Be1+ through Be4+), excluding neutral beryllium atoms.

description after

Number density of beryllium ions across all positive charge states, excluding neutral beryllium, expressing their concentration per physical plasma volume.

documentation before

Beryllium (atomic number $Z = 4$) enters the plasma primarily through sputtering or evaporation from beryllium first-wall surfaces. The local beryllium ion number density $n_\mathrm{Be}$ aggregates contributions from all four charged ionization stages: $$ n_\mathrm{Be} = \sum_{z=1}^{4} n_{\mathrm{Be}^{z+}} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Be}^{z+}}$ is the number density of beryllium ions in charge state $z+$, with $z$ running from 1 (singly ionized) to 4 (fully stripped). Neutral beryllium atoms are excluded from this sum. Beryllium impurity content is inferred from passive spectroscopy of beryllium emission lines or from active charge-exchange recombination spectroscopy, with spatially resolved profiles reconstructed by tomographic inversion of chord-integrated measurements. Spatially averaged representations include [line_averaged_beryllium_density](name:line_averaged_beryllium_density) along a diagnostic chord and [volume_averaged_beryllium_density](name:volume_averaged_beryllium_density) integrated over the confined plasma volume. The relative impurity abundance with respect to the electron population is characterized by the ratio_of_beryllium_density_to_electron_density.

documentation after

Number density of beryllium. The underlying local density is defined by $$ n_{\mathrm{Be}} = \frac{d\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{Be}}}{dV} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Be}}$ is the beryllium-ion number density, $\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{Be}}$ is the beryllium-ion count, and $V$ is physical volume. Spatial averaging is not intrinsic to the unqualified density. The [line-averaged beryllium density](name:line_averaged_beryllium_density) is its path integral divided by the chord length, whereas the [volume-averaged beryllium density](name:volume_averaged_beryllium_density) is its volume integral divided by the confined plasma volume. In contrast, [beryllium density at the magnetic axis](name:beryllium_density_at_magnetic_axis) specifies a local evaluation rather than a spatial average. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

deuterium_density

description before

Deuterium density is the number density of ionized deuterium in the fuel-ion population. It is the local density of deuterons, excluding neutral atoms, molecules, and line- or volume-averaged deuterium densities.

description after

Number density of deuterium ions gives the deuteron concentration per physical volume in the plasma fuel population. The ions of the species are counted over all charge states, with neutral atoms excluded.

documentation before

Deuterium density is the number density of ionized deuterium in the fuel-ion population. As a species-level density it is charge-state-summed over ionized atomic charge states; for deuterium this reduces to the singly ionized deuteron population. The local deuterium density is defined by the charge-state sum $$ n_D = \sum_{q>0} n_{\mathrm{D}^{q+}} $$ where $n_D$ is the deuterium density, $q$ is the positive atomic charge state, and $n_{\mathrm{D}^{q+}}$ is the number density of deuterium ions in charge state $q$. Neutral deuterium atoms, molecular deuterium, and non-deuterium species are excluded. This quantity is local; spatial aggregations such as [line-averaged deuterium density](name:line_averaged_deuterium_density) and [volume-averaged deuterium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_density) are distinct quantities.

documentation after

Number density of deuterium. The underlying local concentration is the zeroth velocity-space moment: $$ n_{\mathrm D} = \int f_{\mathrm D}(\mathbf v)\,\mathrm d^3v $$ where $n_{\mathrm D}$ is the deuterium-ion number density, $f_{\mathrm D}$ is the local deuteron velocity distribution, $\mathbf v$ is particle velocity, and $\mathrm d^3v$ is the velocity-space volume element. The integral covers the full velocity space. No particular evaluation location or spatial averaging operation is prescribed. The [line-averaged deuterium density](name:line_averaged_deuterium_density) is the chord-length mean of this concentration, whereas the [volume-averaged deuterium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_density) is its mean over the plasma volume. Molecular deuterium and other ion species do not contribute. Unlike [deuterium–tritium density](name:deuterium_tritium_density), this quantity does not combine the two fuel isotopes. It supplies the denominator of the tritium-to-deuterium density ratio, which expresses isotope composition rather than an absolute concentration. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

deuterium_tritium_density

description before

Deuterium-tritium density is the local ion number density of the deuterium-tritium fuel-ion pseudo-species, treating deuterium and tritium as one effective species.

description after

Number density of deuterium–tritium fuel ions treated as one effective species, including all ionic charge states but excluding neutral atoms.

documentation before

Deuterium-tritium density is the local ion number density of the deuterium-tritium fuel-ion pseudo-species, treating deuterium and tritium ions as one effective species. It is a species-level per-volume particle count summed over all ion charge states. The defining relation is the sum of the component fuel-ion number densities: $$ n_{DT} = n_D + n_T $$ where $n_{DT}$ is the deuterium-tritium density, $n_D$ is the [deuterium density](name:deuterium_density), and $n_T$ is the [tritium density](name:tritium_density). Each component density is charge-state-summed over the corresponding ion charge states; the dimensionless tritium fraction, where used, is $f_T = n_T/n_{DT}$. This quantity includes only deuterium and tritium fuel ions represented by the effective deuterium-tritium species. Neutral deuterium or tritium atoms, neutral molecules such as D$_2$, T$_2$, and DT, reaction products, and impurity ions are excluded. As a number density, its value domain is $n_{DT} \ge 0$.

documentation after

Number density of deuterium tritium. The effective-species density is defined by $$ n_{\mathrm{DT}} = n_{\mathrm{D}} + n_{\mathrm{T}} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{DT}}$ is the combined deuterium–tritium ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{D}}$ is the [deuterium-ion number density](name:deuterium_density), and $n_{\mathrm{T}}$ is the tritium-ion number density. This is a fuel-population density, not a fusion-reaction rate. It implies no fixed deuterium-to-tritium mixing ratio and prescribes neither a particular location nor a spatial averaging measure. The [line-averaged form](name:line_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density) averages this concentration along a specified chord, whereas the [volume-averaged form](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density) averages it over a specified volume. Both retain the same effective fuel-species definition. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

deuterium_tritium_density_flux_surface_averaged_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface-averaged number density of deuterium-tritium pseudo-species ions at the plasma boundary (separatrix).

description after

Flux-surface-averaged number density of deuterium-tritium pseudo-species ions at the plasma boundary (separatrix).

documentation before

Flux-surface-averaged number density of deuterium-tritium pseudo-species ions at the plasma boundary (separatrix) is the particle density of the effective deuterium-tritium fuel species averaged over the last closed magnetic flux surface. The pseudo-species represents the deuterium-tritium fuel mixture as a single ion constituent with effective mass number 2.5; as a species-level density, it is summed over all charge states. Using the poloidal-contour representation of a flux-surface average in an axisymmetric equilibrium, the boundary value is defined by: $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{DT}} \rangle_b = \frac{\oint_{\psi=\psi_b} n_{\mathrm{DT}}(l)\, dl / B_p(l)} {\oint_{\psi=\psi_b} dl / B_p(l)} $$ where $\langle n_{\mathrm{DT}} \rangle_b$ is this boundary average, $\psi_b$ labels the plasma-boundary flux surface, $l$ is poloidal arc length along that surface, and $B_p$ is the poloidal magnetic-field magnitude. The local pseudo-species density is $n_{\mathrm{DT}} = n_{\mathrm{D}} + n_{\mathrm{T}}$, where $n_{\mathrm{D}}$ and $n_{\mathrm{T}}$ are the corresponding charge-state-summed deuterium and tritium ion densities. This quantity is the plasma-boundary member of [flux-surface-averaged deuterium-tritium density](name:flux_surface_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density_at_plasma_boundary) and the flux-surface-averaged counterpart of [deuterium-tritium density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density_at_plasma_boundary), which retains variation around the same boundary surface. The single-isotope boundary analog [flux-surface-averaged deuterium density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_deuterium_density_at_plasma_boundary) separates one isotope contribution, while [volume-averaged deuterium-tritium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density) averages the same effective fuel population over the confined-plasma volume. It excludes scrape-off-layer and material-limiter densities, including [deuterium-tritium density at a limiter](name:deuterium_tritium_density_at_limiter).

documentation after

Flux-surface-averaged number density of deuterium-tritium pseudo-species ions at the plasma boundary (separatrix) is the particle density of the effective deuterium-tritium fuel species averaged over the last closed magnetic flux surface. The pseudo-species represents the deuterium-tritium fuel mixture as a single ion constituent with effective mass number 2.5; as a species-level density, it is summed over all charge states. Using the poloidal-contour representation of a flux-surface average in an axisymmetric equilibrium, the boundary value is defined by: $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{DT}} \rangle_b = \frac{\oint_{\psi=\psi_b} n_{\mathrm{DT}}(l)\, dl / B_p(l)} {\oint_{\psi=\psi_b} dl / B_p(l)} $$ where $\langle n_{\mathrm{DT}} \rangle_b$ is this boundary average, $\psi_b$ labels the plasma-boundary flux surface, $l$ is poloidal arc length along that surface, and $B_p$ is the poloidal magnetic-field magnitude. The local pseudo-species density is $n_{\mathrm{DT}} = n_{\mathrm{D}} + n_{\mathrm{T}}$, where $n_{\mathrm{D}}$ and $n_{\mathrm{T}}$ are the corresponding charge-state-summed deuterium and tritium ion densities. This quantity is the plasma-boundary member of [flux-surface-averaged deuterium-tritium density](name:flux_surface_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density_at_plasma_boundary) and the flux-surface-averaged counterpart of [deuterium-tritium density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density_at_plasma_boundary), which retains variation around the same boundary surface. The single-isotope boundary analog [flux-surface-averaged deuterium density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_deuterium_density_at_plasma_boundary) separates one isotope contribution, while [volume-averaged deuterium-tritium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density) averages the same effective fuel population over the confined-plasma volume. It excludes scrape-off-layer and material-limiter densities, including [deuterium-tritium density at a limiter](name:deuterium_tritium_density_at_limiter).

difference_of_total_plasma_heating_power_and_time_derivative_of_plasma_stored_energy

description before

Instantaneous plasma power available for confinement losses after correcting total coupled heating for the rate of change of stored plasma energy.

description after

Difference of total plasma heating power and the time derivative of plasma stored energy, giving the instantaneous power available for confinement losses.

documentation before

This quantity is the instantaneous global power-balance residual available to account for energy leaving the plasma confinement system. It corrects the total plasma heating power for transient changes in the plasma’s stored energy. It is defined by $$ P_{\mathrm{loss}} = P_{\mathrm{heat,total}} - \frac{dW_{\mathrm{plasma}}}{dt} $$ where $P_{\mathrm{loss}}$ is the power-balance residual represented by this quantity, $P_{\mathrm{heat,total}}$ is the total plasma heating power, $W_{\mathrm{plasma}}$ is the total stored energy of the plasma, and $t$ is time. The quantity is global and aggregated over all heating contributions included in total plasma heating power and over the complete stored plasma-energy reservoir. It is not the heating input itself, a single-source heating contribution, or a regional radiation measurement; [radiated power over the core region](name:radiated_power_over_core_region) is a specific loss channel rather than this balance residual. A positive value means heating exceeds the instantaneous increase in stored energy, while a negative value means stored energy is increasing faster than the heating input. This residual is the loss-power term in the conventional energy-confinement relation $\tau_E = W_{\mathrm{plasma}}/P_{\mathrm{loss}}$, where $\tau_E$ is the energy confinement time; transient storage changes are therefore excluded from the inferred loss power rather than counted as losses.

documentation after

Difference of total plasma heating power and the time derivative of plasma stored energy. This is the global loss-power term remaining after accounting for changes in the plasma energy inventory. The defining power balance is $$\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}$$ where $P_{\mathrm{loss}}$ is the instantaneous power available for confinement losses, $P_{\mathrm{heat,tot}}$ is the total heating power coupled to the plasma, $W_p$ is the plasma stored energy, and $t$ is time. Both operands must refer to the same whole-plasma region and included energy populations. Total coupled heating is not generator power or launched power before coupling. The stored-energy inventory is not restricted to thermal energy by this definition, and the resulting loss power is not restricted to radiation alone. No steady-state assumption is required. For constant stored energy, the loss power equals the total coupled heating power. For the same energy inventory, it supplies the denominator in the energy confinement time relation $\tau_E = W_p/P_{\mathrm{loss}}$, where $\tau_E$ is the energy confinement time and the loss power must be positive. $$a - b$$ The difference is one quantity minus another. The time derivative is the rate of change of a quantity with time at a fixed point or label.

flux_surface_averaged_argon_density_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Number density of argon ions averaged over the last closed magnetic flux surface, summed over all argon ion charge states and excluding neutral argon.

description after

Number density of argon ions averaged over the plasma boundary, summed over all argon ion charge states and excluding neutral argon.

documentation before

Number density of argon The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{Ar}}\rangle = \oint n_{\mathrm{Ar}}\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \, / \, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $\langle n_{\mathrm{Ar}}\rangle$ is the averaged argon-ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{Ar}}$ is the local argon-ion number density, $S$ is the plasma-boundary flux surface, $dS$ is its surface element, and $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux; $|\nabla\psi|$ is the magnitude of its spatial gradient. This quantity is summed over all charged argon ion states and excludes neutral argon. It is a flux-surface average rather than a local point value, line average, or volume average. It is the averaged counterpart of the local [argon ion density](name:argon_density) and differs from the [volume-averaged argon density](name:volume_averaged_argon_density), which uses a plasma-volume average instead of an inverse-poloidal-flux-gradient-weighted surface average. Its species-specific structure parallels the [flux-surface-averaged xenon density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_xenon_density_at_plasma_boundary). The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface.

documentation after

Number density of argon. Charge-state summation commutes with flux-surface averaging: $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $n_{\mathrm{Ar}}$ is the local [argon-ion number density](name:argon_density), $n_{\mathrm{Ar}^{z+}}$ is the local number density of argon ions with positive charge number $z$, and angle brackets denote the flux-surface average at the plasma boundary. The sum includes all positive charge states of argon. Each ion is counted once, without charge weighting; neutral argon is excluded. This quantity is a flux-surface average, not a local point value, volume average, or chord average. It specializes [density at the plasma boundary](name:density_at_plasma_boundary) by selecting the argon-ion population and the flux-surface-averaged rather than local value. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_beryllium_density_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Number density of beryllium ions averaged over the plasma boundary, summed over all ionization states and excluding neutral beryllium.

description after

Number density of beryllium ions averaged over the plasma boundary, summed over all beryllium ion charge states and excluding neutral beryllium.

documentation before

Number density of beryllium The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{Be}}\rangle = \oint n_{\mathrm{Be}}\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \, /\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $\langle n_{\mathrm{Be}}\rangle$ is the averaged beryllium-ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{Be}}$ is the local beryllium-ion number density, $S$ is the plasma-boundary flux surface, $dS$ is its surface element, and $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux whose gradient magnitude is $|\nabla\psi|$. This quantity is summed over all beryllium ion charge states and excludes neutral beryllium. It is a flux-surface average, not a local point value, volume average, or chord average. It is the flux-surface-averaged counterpart of the local [beryllium ion density at the plasma boundary](name:beryllium_density_at_plasma_boundary). It differs from the [volume-averaged beryllium density](name:volume_averaged_beryllium_density), which uses a volume rather than a flux-surface domain. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface.

documentation after

Number density of beryllium. flux_surface_averaged_current_density_due_to_wave_driven_current_drive

description before

Vector average on a magnetic flux surface of conventional plasma current density generated non-inductively by electromagnetic-wave–particle interactions, excluding inductive and non-wave current drive.

description after

Vector average over a closed magnetic flux surface of conventional plasma current density driven non-inductively by electromagnetic wave–particle interactions.

documentation before

Flux-surface-averaged wave-driven current density is the vector average, on a magnetic flux surface, of the conventional plasma current density generated non-inductively by electromagnetic-wave–particle interactions. It retains the vector character of the local current-density contribution while averaging its resolved components over the surface. The flux-surface average is defined componentwise by $$ \left\langle \mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{wd}} \right\rangle_{\psi} = \frac{\displaystyle\int_{S_{\psi}} \mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{wd}}\,\frac{dS}{\lvert\nabla\psi\rvert}} {\displaystyle\int_{S_{\psi}}\frac{dS}{\lvert\nabla\psi\rvert}} $$ where $\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{wd}}$ is the local conventional current-density vector generated by wave–particle interactions, $\psi$ is the scalar magnetic-flux label, $S_{\psi}$ is the corresponding magnetic flux surface, $dS$ is its surface-area element, and $\nabla\psi$ is the spatial gradient of the flux label. The quantity applies to the plasma current on one magnetic flux surface and includes the wave-driven contributions of the charged-particle populations represented in the conventional current. Its aggregation convention is a complete flux-surface average, not a local value, volume average, toroidal current integral, or Fourier-mode-resolved contribution. It excludes inductive current density and current contributions generated by bootstrap, ohmic, or other non-wave current-drive mechanisms. No field-aligned or other directional projection is imposed at this parent level. This quantity is obtained by applying flux-surface averaging to the local [wave-driven current density](name:current_density_due_to_wave_driven_current_drive). The parallel flux-surface-averaged wave-driven current density is a specialization that retains only the component projected along the magnetic-field direction before or within the averaging operation. Sign convention: Positive when a resolved current-density component points in the corresponding positive direction of the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ basis, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height, consistent with conventional positive-charge transport.

documentation after

Flux-surface-averaged wave-driven current density represents the net vector contribution generated non-inductively by electromagnetic wave–particle interactions. It retains the vector character of conventional plasma [current density](name:current_density), rather than selecting a directional projection. The defining relation is $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\overline{\mathbf{j}}_{\mathrm{w}}$ is the averaged wave-driven current-density vector, $\mathbf{j}_{\mathrm{w}}$ is the local [wave-driven current-density vector](name:current_density_due_to_wave_driven_current_drive), and angle brackets denote the [flux-surface average](concept:flux_surface_averaged). Only the wave-driven contribution under consideration is included; inductive current, bootstrap current, and other non-wave contributions are excluded. The result is an averaged current density, not an enclosed current or an average of the current-density magnitude. Unlike [flux-surface-averaged parallel current density due to wave-driven current drive](name:flux_surface_averaged_parallel_current_density_due_to_wave_driven_current_drive), this quantity averages the vector itself without applying a magnetic-field projection or dividing by a reference vacuum toroidal field. Sign convention: Positive when a vector component represents net conventional positive-charge transport along that component's specified positive direction. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_effective_charge_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface average of the ion-mixture effective charge over the entire plasma-boundary surface, distinct from the local boundary value.

description after

Effective charge of the ion mixture at the plasma boundary, obtained by flux-surface averaging the local charge-squared ion-density sum divided by electron density.

documentation before

This quantity is the flux-surface average of the ion-mixture effective charge over the plasma boundary, identified with the last closed flux surface. It summarizes the charge composition across the complete boundary surface rather than at a single location. $$ \left\langle Z_{\mathrm{eff}} \right\rangle_{\psi_b} = \frac{\oint_{\psi_b} Z_{\mathrm{eff}}\,d\ell/B_p}{\oint_{\psi_b} d\ell/B_p}, \qquad Z_{\mathrm{eff}} = \frac{\sum_i n_i Z_i^2}{n_e} $$ Here $\psi_b$ labels the plasma-boundary flux surface, $d\ell$ is poloidal arc length, $B_p$ is the poloidal magnetic-field magnitude, $n_i$ and $Z_i$ are the number density and charge number of ion population $i$, and $n_e$ is the electron number density. The linked effective charge at the plasma boundary denotes the corresponding local boundary quantity; this entry denotes its flux-surface average.

documentation after

Effective charge of the ion mixture, flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary, measures the charge-squared ionic content relative to the electron population. The local quantity being averaged is defined by $$ Z_{\mathrm{eff}} = \frac{\sum_a n_a Z_a^2}{n_e} $$ where $Z_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the local [effective charge at the plasma boundary](name:effective_charge_at_plasma_boundary), $n_e$ is the local electron number density, and $a$ indexes ionic populations distinguished by species and charge state. For each population, $n_a$ is its number density and $Z_a$ is its ionic charge number. The sum includes all ion species and all positive charge states; neutral particles do not contribute. This quantity averages the local effective charge over the entire plasma-boundary surface rather than specifying a pointwise boundary value. It is the average of the local density ratio, not the ratio of separately averaged densities, and differs from the [volume-averaged effective charge](name:volume_averaged_effective_charge), which averages over a plasma volume. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_electron_density_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface-averaged electron number density at the plasma boundary, using inverse-magnitude poloidal-flux-gradient weighting rather than a pointwise value.

description after

Number density of free electrons averaged over the entire plasma boundary, excluding electrons bound in atoms, ions, or molecules.

documentation before

Number density of electron The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \langle n_e\rangle = \oint n_e\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \, / \, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $\langle n_e\rangle$ is the averaged electron number density, $n_e$ is the local electron number density, the subscript $e$ identifies electrons, $S$ is the plasma-boundary flux surface, $dS$ is its surface element, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux, and $\nabla$ is the spatial gradient operator. This quantity applies only to the electron population at the plasma boundary. Its aggregation is over the flux surface with inverse-gradient weighting, not over particle species; it is neither an ion density, a pointwise density, a volume average, nor an unweighted surface average. It is the averaged counterpart of the local [electron number density at the plasma boundary](name:electron_density_at_plasma_boundary): the parent quantity supplies $n_e$ at each surface point, while this quantity applies the normalized flux-surface integral. It differs from [flux-surface-averaged total ion density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_total_ion_density_at_plasma_boundary), which represents charged ions rather than electrons. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface.

documentation after

Number density of electron. The local density underlying this average is defined by $$ n_e(\mathbf{x}) = \int f_e(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{v})\,d^3v $$ where $n_e$ is the local [electron density at the plasma boundary](name:electron_density_at_plasma_boundary), $\mathbf{x}$ is spatial position on that boundary, $\mathbf{v}$ is electron velocity, $f_e$ is the free-electron phase-space number density, and $d^3v$ is the velocity-space volume element. The integral includes the full electron velocity distribution. This quantity applies the flux-surface-average operator to that local density. It is not a local point value, volume average, or chord average. Electrons bound in atoms, ions, or molecules are excluded. The corresponding [flux-surface-averaged ion density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_ion_density_at_plasma_boundary) counts ions of a specified species instead of free electrons. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

documentation after

Number density of hydrogen. The population is protium ions, with number densities summed over all ionic charge states and each ion counted once; neutral hydrogen and other hydrogen isotopes are excluded. The quantity is averaged over the entire plasma-boundary surface; it is not a local point value, volume average, or chord average. It is the flux-surface-averaged counterpart of the local [hydrogen density at the plasma boundary](name:hydrogen_density_at_plasma_boundary): this quantity is the surface average of that local hydrogen-ion number density. The corresponding deuterium population has a separate average, [flux-surface-averaged deuterium density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_deuterium_density_at_plasma_boundary). The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_major_radius

description before

Flux-surface average of reciprocal major radius, providing a geometric equilibrium coefficient for toroidal geometry in flux-coordinate equations.

description after

The flux-surface average of reciprocal distance from the toroidal symmetry axis defines a geometric coefficient on each closed equilibrium magnetic flux surface.

documentation before

This quantity is the geometric average of the reciprocal major radius over a complete magnetic flux surface. It is an equilibrium metric coefficient independent of plasma species, population, magnetic-field component, and physical transport process. For a magnetic flux surface identified by the label $\psi$, the defining flux-surface average is: $$ g_{1/R}(\psi) = \left\langle \frac{1}{R} \right\rangle_\psi = \frac{\displaystyle\oint_{\mathcal{S}(\psi)} \frac{dS}{|\nabla\psi|}\,\frac{1}{R}} {\displaystyle\oint_{\mathcal{S}(\psi)} \frac{dS}{|\nabla\psi|}} $$ Here $g_{1/R}$ denotes the metric coefficient, $R$ is the major-radius coordinate, $\psi$ is the magnetic flux-surface label, $\mathcal{S}(\psi)$ is the corresponding closed magnetic flux surface, $dS$ is its surface-area element, and $\nabla\psi$ is the spatial gradient of the flux-surface label. The coordinate $R$ belongs to the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $\phi$ is the toroidal angle and $Z$ is the vertical coordinate. The aggregation is over the entire closed flux surface using the flux-surface weighting shown in the defining relation; this is not the local reciprocal radius at a point. The quantity is distinct from the flux-surface average of reciprocal squared major radius, [flux-surface-averaged inverse square of major radius](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_square_of_major_radius). It is the flux-surface-averaged specialization of [inverse major radius](name:inverse_of_major_radius): the averaging operator is applied to the local reciprocal-major-radius quantity without introducing a species, field-component, or process restriction.

documentation after

Major radius is the [radius](name:radius) measured perpendicular to the toroidal symmetry axis. This quantity averages reciprocal major radius over each complete closed magnetic flux surface of a nested equilibrium. Its defining operator composition is $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $g(\psi)$ is this geometric equilibrium coefficient, $R$ is major radius, $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux labeling the surface, and angle brackets denote the flux-surface average. This is the flux-surface-averaged form of [inverse major radius](name:inverse_of_major_radius), rather than a pointwise value or an average over the enclosed volume. It requires positive major radius throughout the surface and a well-defined averaging operation. It differs from [flux-surface-averaged major radius](name:flux_surface_averaged_major_radius): averaging the reciprocal is not generally equivalent to taking the reciprocal after averaging. It also differs from [flux-surface-averaged inverse squared major radius](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_square_of_major_radius), whose averaged quantity is the reciprocal square rather than the reciprocal. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. The inverse is the reciprocal of a quantity.

flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude

description before

Flux-surface average of the reciprocal square of total magnetic-field strength, a geometric coefficient in neoclassical and flux-surface-averaged transport relations.

description after

Flux-surface average of the inverse square of total equilibrium magnetic-field magnitude, a geometric coefficient in neoclassical and flux-surface-averaged transport relations.

documentation before

This quantity is the flux-surface average of the reciprocal square of the total magnetic-field strength on a nested magnetic surface. It captures field-strength geometry independently of the magnetic-field direction. The defining relation is $$ \left\langle \frac{1}{|\mathbf{B}|^2} \right\rangle_\psi = \frac{\displaystyle \int_{S(\psi)} |\mathbf{B}|^{-2}\,\frac{dS}{|\nabla\psi|}} {\displaystyle \int_{S(\psi)} \frac{dS}{|\nabla\psi|}} $$ where $\mathbf{B}$ is the total magnetic-field vector, $|\mathbf{B}|$ is its magnitude, $\psi$ is the flux-surface label, $S(\psi)$ is the magnetic surface labeled by $\psi$, $dS$ is its surface-area element, and $\nabla\psi$ is the spatial gradient of the flux-surface label. The quantity applies to the complete magnetic flux surface and uses the inverse-$|\nabla\psi|$ weighting shown in the defining relation. It is not a pointwise inverse-square field value and has no selection by magnetic-field direction, component, species, or source. It is the flux-surface-averaged transformation of the parent [inverse square of magnetic-field magnitude](name:inverse_of_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude), with the reciprocal square formed locally before averaging. It is distinct from [flux-surface-averaged magnetic-field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_magnetic_field_magnitude) and [flux-surface-averaged square of magnetic-field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude), because nonlinear inversion and squaring do not commute with surface averaging.

documentation after

Flux-surface-averaged inverse-square magnetic-field magnitude is a magnetic-geometry coefficient for equilibrium and neoclassical transport relations. It applies to the total equilibrium field on each closed nested magnetic flux surface. The coefficient is defined by $$ \langle B^{-2}\rangle = \oint B^{-2}\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $B$ is the total equilibrium magnetic-field magnitude, $B^{-2}$ is its local inverse square, $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic-flux label, $dS$ is the surface-area element, and $\nabla\psi$ is the gradient of the flux label. Both integrals extend over the closed magnetic flux surface labeled by $\psi$, and angle brackets denote the flux-surface average on that surface. All magnetic-field components contribute through the total magnitude; no individual component is selected. The definition requires nonzero field magnitude and finite surface integrals. This is the surface-averaged form of the [local inverse-square magnetic-field magnitude](name:inverse_of_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude), rather than its pointwise value. Squaring and inversion precede averaging: the result is generally neither the inverse square of the [surface-averaged field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_magnetic_field_magnitude) nor the reciprocal of the [surface-averaged squared field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude). The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. The inverse is the reciprocal of a quantity. The magnitude is the Euclidean norm of a vector quantity, or the modulus of a complex one. The square is a scalar quantity multiplied by itself, or a vector's dot product with itself.

flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_square_of_major_radius

description before

Flux-surface average of reciprocal squared major radius, providing a geometric metric coefficient for parallel-gradient and neoclassical transport calculations.

description after

Geometric equilibrium coefficient obtained by averaging the reciprocal squared distance from the toroidal symmetry axis over a complete closed magnetic flux surface.

documentation before

This quantity is the flux-surface average of the reciprocal square of the major radius on a nested equilibrium magnetic flux surface. It is a positive geometric metric coefficient used in parallel-gradient operators and neoclassical and gyrokinetic transport equations. It is defined by $$ g_{m1}(\psi)=\left\langle \frac{1}{R^2}\right\rangle_{\psi} $$ where $g_{m1}$ denotes this metric coefficient, $R$ is the major radius measured from the toroidal symmetry axis, $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux labeling the surface, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\psi}$ denotes the flux-surface average on that surface. The quantity applies to the entire magnetic flux surface and has no species, field-component, or source-process restriction. Its aggregation convention is a flux-surface average of the local reciprocal-square geometric factor; it is not the reciprocal square of a flux-surface-averaged major radius. It is the flux-surface-averaged form of the pointwise [inverse of square of major radius](name:inverse_of_square_of_major_radius). It differs from the related [flux-surface-averaged inverse of major radius](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_major_radius), which averages the first reciprocal power of $R$ rather than its reciprocal square.

documentation after

Major radius is the [radius](name:radius) measured perpendicular to the toroidal symmetry axis. This geometric equilibrium coefficient averages reciprocal squared major radius over each complete closed magnetic flux surface of a nested equilibrium. Its defining operator composition is $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $K(\psi)$ is this geometric coefficient, $R$ is major radius, $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux labeling the surface, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_\psi$ denotes the flux-surface average on that surface. This quantity is the flux-surface-averaged counterpart of the pointwise [inverse square of major radius](name:inverse_of_square_of_major_radius), not a volume average or an evaluation at a selected point. It requires positive major radius throughout the surface and a finite flux-surface average. The reciprocal-square operation precedes averaging. Consequently, this coefficient is generally neither the inverse square of [flux-surface-averaged major radius](name:flux_surface_averaged_major_radius) nor the square of [flux-surface-averaged inverse of major radius](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_major_radius). The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. The inverse is the reciprocal of a quantity. The square is a scalar quantity multiplied by itself, or a vector's dot product with itself.

flux_surface_averaged_ion_density_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Number density of ions of a specified species at the plasma boundary, flux-surface averaged with inverse poloidal-flux-gradient weighting, summed over all charge states, and excluding neutral atoms and molecules.

description after

Number density of ions of a specified species averaged over the plasma boundary, summed over all positive charge states and excluding neutral atoms and molecules.

documentation before

Number density of ion The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \langle n_i\rangle = \oint n_i\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $\langle n_i\rangle$ is the averaged ion number density, $n_i$ is the local [ion density at the plasma boundary](name:ion_density_at_plasma_boundary) of the represented ion species, $i$ is the ion-species label, $S$ is the plasma-boundary flux surface, $dS$ is its surface element, and $\psi$ is the [poloidal magnetic flux](name:poloidal_magnetic_flux) whose gradient magnitude is $|\nabla\psi|$. This quantity is evaluated on the confined-plasma side of the plasma boundary. It is summed over all charge states of the represented ion species and excludes neutral atoms and molecules; distinct ion species are not implicitly combined. It is a flux-surface average, not a local point value, volume average, or chord average. The average is formed from the local [ion density at the plasma boundary](name:ion_density_at_plasma_boundary) by applying inverse-poloidal-flux-gradient weighting over the surface. It differs from the [flux-surface-averaged total ion density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_total_ion_density_at_plasma_boundary), which additionally aggregates charged-ion densities across ion species. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface.

documentation after

Number density of ion. For species $i$, the averaged density is $$ \langle n_i\rangle = \oint n_i\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \, / \, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $\langle n_i\rangle$ is the averaged number density, $n_i$ is the local [ion density at the plasma boundary](name:ion_density_at_plasma_boundary) for species $i$, $i$ labels the selected ion species, $S$ is the plasma-boundary flux surface, and $dS$ is its surface element. The symbol $\psi$ is [poloidal magnetic flux](name:poloidal_magnetic_flux), and $|\nabla\psi|$ is the magnitude of its gradient. The average covers the complete surface $S$ and applies to the selected ion species on the confined-plasma side of the plasma boundary. At each point, $n_i=\sum_{q>0}n_{i,q}$, where $n_{i,q}$ is the local number density of species $i$ in positive charge state $q$, and $q$ is the charge number. All positive charge states are included, with each ion counted once regardless of charge; neutral atoms and molecules and ions of other species are excluded. This is a flux-surface average, not a local point value, volume average, or chord average. This is the flux-surface-averaged counterpart of [ion density at the plasma boundary](name:ion_density_at_plasma_boundary), which denotes the local boundary density for the same species. Unlike [flux-surface-averaged total ion density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_total_ion_density_at_plasma_boundary), it does not aggregate densities across distinct ion species. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_iron_density_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Number density of iron ions averaged over the plasma-boundary flux surface, summed over all iron ion charge states and excluding neutral iron.

description after

Number density of iron ions averaged over the plasma boundary, summed over all ionic charge states and excluding neutral iron.

documentation before

Number density of iron The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{Fe}}\rangle = \oint n_{\mathrm{Fe}}\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \, / \, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $\langle n_{\mathrm{Fe}}\rangle$ is the averaged iron-ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{Fe}}$ is the local iron-ion number density, $S$ is the plasma-boundary flux surface, $dS$ is its surface element, and $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux whose gradient magnitude provides the averaging weight. This quantity applies to iron ions on the plasma-boundary flux surface and is summed over all positive iron ion charge states, from singly ionized through fully stripped iron. Neutral iron is excluded. The result is a flux-surface average, not a local point value, volume average, or total iron inventory. It is the flux-surface-averaged counterpart of the local [iron density at the plasma boundary](name:iron_density_at_plasma_boundary): the same charge-state-summed ion density is converted from a pointwise value to an inverse-poloidal-flux-gradient-weighted surface mean. It is distinct from [iron density at the limiter](name:iron_density_at_limiter), which refers to a different evaluation locus. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface.

documentation after

Number density of iron. The local ionic density underlying this average satisfies $$ n_{\mathrm{Fe}} = \sum_{q=1}^{26} n_{\mathrm{Fe}^{q+}} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Fe}}$ is the local [iron density at the plasma boundary](name:iron_density_at_plasma_boundary), $n_{\mathrm{Fe}^{q+}}$ is the local number density of iron ions with positive charge number $q$, and $q$ is the integer ionic charge number. The sum includes all iron ion charge states, from singly ionized through fully stripped iron, with each ion counted once and neutral iron excluded. It counts particles rather than weighting them by ionic charge. Applying flux-surface averaging to the local parent density gives this quantity; charge-state summation commutes with that averaging operation. The result is a flux-surface average, not a local point value, volume average, or chord average. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. flux_surface_averaged_magnetic_field_magnitude

description before

Flux-surface average of the local magnetic-field strength, retaining the pointwise modulus before averaging over each magnetic surface.

description after

Flux-surface-averaged magnetic-field magnitude is the mean strength of the total equilibrium field, with the local magnitude taken before averaging.

documentation before

This quantity is the flux-surface average of the magnitude of the total equilibrium magnetic-field vector. It represents the mean field strength on a magnetic surface, rather than a component or a vector average. The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \left\langle \lvert\mathbf{B}\rvert \right\rangle_{\psi} = \frac{\oint_{\psi} \lvert\mathbf{B}\rvert\,\mathrm{d}S/\lvert\nabla\psi\rvert}{\oint_{\psi} \mathrm{d}S/\lvert\nabla\psi\rvert} $$ where $\mathbf{B}$ is the total equilibrium magnetic-field vector, $\psi$ is the scalar label of the magnetic flux surface, $\mathrm{d}S$ is the surface-area element, and $\nabla\psi$ is the spatial gradient of that surface label. The aggregation is the normalized surface integral with weighting $\mathrm{d}S/\lvert\nabla\psi\rvert$. The quantity applies to all components of the equilibrium field on the selected magnetic flux surface and is non-negative. It excludes any signed field component, any species or source decomposition, and the magnitude of a vector obtained by averaging $\mathbf{B}$ before taking its modulus. It is the flux-surface-averaged transformation of [magnetic-field magnitude](name:magnetic_field_magnitude). It differs from [flux-surface-averaged square of magnetic-field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude), which averages the squared field strength rather than the field strength itself.

documentation after

Flux-surface-averaged magnetic-field magnitude is the mean strength of the total equilibrium [magnetic field](name:magnetic_field) on each closed nested magnetic flux surface. Its defining relation is $$ \langle B\rangle = \oint B\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $B$ is the local total [magnetic-field magnitude](name:magnetic_field_magnitude), $\langle B\rangle$ is its flux-surface average, $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic-flux label, $\nabla\psi$ is the gradient of that label, and $dS$ is the area element of the closed surface over which both integrals are taken. All equilibrium magnetic-field components contribute to the local magnitude before averaging. This quantity is not a selected field component, a pointwise field strength, or the magnitude of a surface-averaged field vector. It is positive for a nonvanishing equilibrium field. The [flux-surface-averaged squared magnetic-field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude) instead averages the square of the local field strength. That second moment is not the square of this mean unless the field strength is constant over the surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. The magnitude is the Euclidean norm of a vector quantity, or the modulus of a complex one.

flux_surface_averaged_major_radius

description before

Flux-surface-averaged nonnegative perpendicular distance from the toroidal symmetry axis to points on a nested magnetic flux surface, giving the mean major-radius coordinate in the right-handed cylindrical (R, φ, Z) frame.

description after

Major radius averaged over a complete closed magnetic flux surface gives the mean perpendicular distance from the toroidal symmetry axis.

documentation before

Major radius is the nonnegative perpendicular distance from the toroidal symmetry axis to a geometric point or specified location. For this flux-surface-averaged quantity, that distance is averaged over each nested magnetic flux surface in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The flux-surface average of major radius is defined by $$ \langle R\rangle_\psi = \frac{\oint_\psi R\,dl/B_p}{\oint_\psi dl/B_p} $$ where $\langle R\rangle_\psi$ is the averaged major-radius coordinate, $\psi$ is the magnetic-flux label identifying the surface, $l$ is poloidal arc length along that surface, and $B_p$ is the magnitude of the poloidal magnetic field. The contour integral extends around the complete poloidal intersection of the nested magnetic flux surface. This quantity applies to a complete magnetic flux surface and aggregates the local [major radius](name:major_radius) using the flux-surface weighting defined by the inverse poloidal-field magnitude. It is therefore distinct from pointwise major radius and contains no point-specific or localized evaluation. The quantity is the flux-surface-averaged counterpart of [major radius](name:major_radius). It must not be confused with [flux-surface-averaged inverse of major radius](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_major_radius), which averages the reciprocal $1/R$ rather than averaging $R$ itself; consequently, it is not generally the reciprocal of that neighboring average.

documentation after

Major radius is the [radius](name:radius) measured perpendicular to the toroidal symmetry axis. Its flux-surface-averaged value is a geometric equilibrium coefficient for each complete closed magnetic flux surface of a nested equilibrium. Its defining operator composition is $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\overline{R}(\psi)$ is this coefficient, $R$ is the pointwise [major radius](name:major_radius), $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux labeling the surface, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_\psi$ denotes the flux-surface average on that surface. This quantity is the averaged counterpart of pointwise major radius, not a volume average or the major radius evaluated at a selected point. It applies wherever the flux-surface average is well defined and finite. The reciprocal of this mean generally differs from the [flux-surface-averaged inverse of major radius](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_major_radius), because that sibling applies inversion before averaging. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

Number density of neon. Charge-state summation commutes with flux-surface averaging: $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $n_{\mathrm{Ne}}$ is the local [neon density at the plasma boundary](name:neon_density_at_plasma_boundary), $n_{\mathrm{Ne}^{q+}}$ is the local number density of neon ions with positive charge number $q$, and angle brackets denote flux-surface averaging over the plasma boundary. The population is summed over all neon ion charge states, from singly ionized to fully stripped neon, with each ion counted once regardless of charge. Neutral neon is excluded. Applying the flux-surface-average operator to the local neon density gives this quantity; it is not a local point value, volume average, or chord average. The corresponding [flux-surface-averaged tungsten density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_tungsten_density_at_plasma_boundary) applies the same averaging operation to tungsten ions rather than neon ions. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_nitrogen_density_at_plasma_boundary

Number density of nitrogen ions averaged over the last closed magnetic flux surface bounding the confined plasma, summed over all ion charge states and excluding neutral nitrogen.

description after

Number density of nitrogen ions averaged over the plasma boundary, summed over all ionic charge states and excluding neutral nitrogen.

documentation before

Number density of nitrogen The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{N}}\rangle = \oint n_{\mathrm{N}}\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \, /\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $\langle n_{\mathrm{N}}\rangle$ is the averaged nitrogen-ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{N}}$ is the local nitrogen-ion number density, $S$ is the plasma-boundary flux surface, $dS$ is its surface element, and $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux. The quantity $|\nabla\psi|$ is the magnitude of the poloidal magnetic-flux gradient, and the closed-integral symbols denote integration over the complete surface. This quantity is summed over all nitrogen ion charge states and excludes neutral nitrogen. It is a flux-surface average rather than the local value given by [nitrogen ion density at the plasma boundary](name:nitrogen_density_at_plasma_boundary), and it is not a volume or line average. flux_surface_averaged_oxygen_density_at_plasma_boundary

Number density of oxygen ions averaged over the plasma boundary, summed over all ionic charge states and excluding neutral and molecular oxygen.

Number density of oxygen The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{O}}\rangle = \oint n_{\mathrm{O}}\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \, /\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $\langle n_{\mathrm{O}}\rangle$ is the averaged oxygen-ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{O}}$ is the local oxygen-ion number density, $S$ is the plasma-boundary flux surface, $dS$ is its surface element, and $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux. This quantity is not a local point value, a volume average, an individual charge-state density, or a density that includes neutral oxygen. It is the flux-surface-averaged counterpart of the local [oxygen density at the plasma boundary](name:oxygen_density_at_plasma_boundary), using inverse-poloidal-flux-gradient weighting to replace the pointwise value with a surface mean. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma.
