flux_surface_averaged_parallel_current_density_at_constraint_position

description before

Flux-surface-averaged parallel current density at a specified constraint position is the field-aligned conventional current contribution averaged over the selected closed magnetic surface.

description after

Flux-surface-averaged parallel current density at the constraint position is the mean current–magnetic-field dot product divided by a signed reference toroidal magnetic field.

documentation before

Flux-surface-averaged parallel current density at a specified constraint position is the signed plasma current density associated with the component of conventional current flowing along the local magnetic field, averaged over the corresponding closed magnetic flux surface. The defining field-aligned flux-surface average is $$ j_{parallel}^{\mathrm{FS}}(\psi_c)=\frac{\left\langle\mathbf{j}\cdot\mathbf{B}\right\rangle_{\psi_c}}{B_0(\psi_c)} $$ where $j_{\parallel}^{\mathrm{FS}}(\psi_c)$ is this averaged parallel current density, $\mathbf{j}$ is the conventional plasma current-density vector, $\mathbf{B}$ is the local magnetic-field vector, $\psi_c$ is the flux-surface label selected by the constraint position, $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\psi_c}$ denotes averaging over that magnetic flux surface, and $B_0(\psi_c)$ is the reference magnetic-field magnitude used to normalize the field-aligned projection. The quantity applies to the total plasma current-density field on the selected closed magnetic surface, with all current-carrying populations and mechanisms included unless a narrower physical qualifier is supplied. Its aggregation is over the complete flux surface, so it is not a pointwise parallel current density. It is not restricted to a particular current-drive mechanism and is therefore distinct from the [parallel current density due to ohmic current drive](name:parallel_current_density_due_to_ohmic_current_drive) and the parallel flux-surface-averaged current density due to wave-driven current drive. The underlying vector quantity is [current density](name:current_density); this name applies its magnetic-field-aligned projection and then performs a flux-surface average. The toroidal flux-surface-averaged current density at a constraint position instead uses the geometric toroidal projection before averaging. Sign convention: Positive when the conventional current-density vector has a component along the local magnetic-field vector.

documentation after

Flux-surface-averaged parallel current density at the constraint position represents net conventional plasma current along the magnetic field, with magnetic-field weighting and a signed reference-field normalization. The defining relation is: $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $j_\parallel^{\mathrm{FS}}$ is this quantity, $\mathbf{j}$ is the conventional [current density](name:current_density), $\mathbf{B}$ is the magnetic-field vector, $|\mathbf{B}|$ is its magnitude, and $j_\parallel^{\mathrm{loc}}$ is the local [parallel current density at the constraint position](name:parallel_current_density_at_constraint_position). Angle brackets denote the flux-surface average, and $B_0$ is the nonzero signed reference toroidal magnetic field. The reference field is positive along increasing toroidal angle $\phi$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is distance from the symmetry axis and $Z$ is vertical height. This is the constraint-position specialization of [flux-surface-averaged parallel current density](name:flux_surface_averaged_parallel_current_density). It includes the signed contributions of all charged-particle populations and is not restricted to inductively driven current. It is not generally the simple flux-surface average of the local parallel component: the magnetic-field magnitude enters the numerator before division by the reference field. Sign convention: Positive when the surface-averaged conventional current–magnetic-field dot product has the same sign as the reference toroidal magnetic field $B_0$. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_parallel_current_density_due_to_wave_driven_current_drive

description before

Parallel current density driven by a coherent wave, defined by a flux-surface-averaged magnetic-field projection divided by the reference vacuum toroidal field.

description after

Parallel current density driven non-inductively by a coherent wave, defined by the flux-surface-averaged current–magnetic-field dot product divided by the reference vacuum toroidal field.

documentation before

Parallel current density on a closed magnetic flux surface represents the signed net plasma current contribution driven non-inductively by a specified coherent electromagnetic wave. Its definition averages a magnetic-field projection rather than simply averaging the local field-aligned current component. The defining relation is $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\overline{j}_{\parallel}$ is this averaged parallel current density, $\mathbf{j}_{\mathrm{w}}$ is the conventional plasma-current-density vector attributed to the specified wave, $\mathbf{B}$ is the magnetic-field vector, and $B_0$ is the signed reference vacuum toroidal magnetic field, which must be nonzero. The brackets denote the flux-surface average, $\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$, where $f$ is the quantity being averaged, $S$ is the closed magnetic flux surface, $dS$ is its surface-area element, $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux, and $\nabla\psi$ is its spatial gradient. Here the averaged quantity is the wave-driven current-density vector dotted with the magnetic field. This quantity is the flux-surface-averaged specialization of [parallel current density due to wave-driven current drive](name:parallel_current_density_due_to_wave_driven_current_drive). It includes only the net contribution attributed to the specified coherent wave, excluding inductive current, bootstrap current, and contributions attributed to other waves or non-wave mechanisms. It is therefore not the aggregate [parallel current density due to all non-inductive drive](name:parallel_current_density_due_to_non_inductive_current_drive). Unlike the [flux-surface-averaged wave-driven current-density vector](name:flux_surface_averaged_current_density_due_to_wave_driven_current_drive), this quantity retains a signed magnetic-field projection rather than vector direction. The projection is formed before averaging and cannot generally be recovered by projecting the averaged vector. Sign convention: Positive when the flux-surface-averaged wave-driven current projection onto the magnetic field has the same sign as the reference vacuum toroidal field $B_0$.

documentation after

Parallel current density due to wave-driven current drive is a signed measure of the field-aligned conventional plasma current generated non-inductively by a coherent wave through wave–particle interactions. The defining relation is $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $J_{\parallel,\mathrm{w}}$ is the quantity defined here, $\mathbf{j}_{\mathrm{w}}$ is the coherent-wave-driven conventional current-density vector, $\mathbf{B}$ is the total magnetic field, $|\mathbf{B}|$ is its magnitude, and $B_0$ is the nonzero signed reference vacuum toroidal magnetic field. The local [parallel wave-driven current density](name:parallel_current_density_due_to_wave_driven_current_drive), $j_{\parallel,\mathrm{w}}$, is the projection of that current-density vector along the local magnetic field. Angle brackets denote the [flux-surface average](concept:flux_surface_averaged). This is the coherent-wave contribution to the [flux-surface-averaged parallel current density](name:flux_surface_averaged_parallel_current_density). The magnetic-field factor and reference-field divisor distinguish it from the flux-surface average of the local parallel projection alone. It excludes bootstrap current, the [inductive contribution](name:flux_surface_averaged_parallel_inductive_current_density), and contributions driven by other independent sources; it is a current density, not an enclosed current. Sign convention: Positive when the surface-averaged current–magnetic-field dot product has the same sign as $B_0$. Positive $B_0$ points toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius and $Z$ is vertical height. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_parallel_electric_field_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface average of the signed electric-field projection along the magnetic field, taken over the separatrix rather than at a single point.

description after

Signed flux-surface average of the electric–magnetic field dot product at the plasma boundary, divided by a reference magnetic-field value.

documentation before

The flux-surface-averaged parallel electric field is the signed average of the electric-field projection along the local magnetic-field direction over the separatrix magnetic surface. The defining flux-surface average is $$ \left\langle E_\parallel \right\rangle_{\rm sep} = \frac{\displaystyle\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{\rm sep}} E_\parallel\,\frac{d\ell}{B}}{\displaystyle\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{\rm sep}}\frac{d\ell}{B}} $$ where $\langle E_\parallel \rangle_{\rm sep}$ is the averaged quantity, $E_\parallel$ is the local projection of the electric field along the local magnetic field, $\mathcal{C}_{\rm sep}$ is the poloidal contour of the separatrix, $d\ell$ is the differential poloidal arc length, and $B$ is the magnitude of the local magnetic field. The subscript $\rm sep$ identifies the separatrix surface. The quantity applies to the complete separatrix surface and uses the inverse-magnetic-field weighting intrinsic to a flux-surface average; it is not a pointwise value. It is the averaged counterpart of the local [parallel electric field at the separatrix](name:parallel_electric_field_at_separatrix), so local variation around the separatrix is combined rather than retained as a separate spatial value. Sign convention: Positive when the electric field points along the local magnetic-field direction.

documentation after

The flux-surface-averaged parallel electric field at the plasma boundary represents signed field-aligned electric forcing with magnetic-field weighting. It is a surface-aggregated quantity rather than a point-local boundary value. The defining relation is $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\overline{E}_{\parallel,b}$ is this averaged quantity, $\mathbf{E}$ is the [electric field](name:electric_field), $\mathbf{B}$ is the local magnetic-field vector, and $B_0$ is the specified nonzero reference magnetic-field value, constant over the averaging surface. The brackets denote flux-surface averaging, and the subscript $b$ identifies evaluation at the plasma boundary. The point-local [parallel electric field at the plasma boundary](name:parallel_electric_field_at_plasma_boundary) is $E_\parallel=\mathbf{E}\cdot\mathbf{B}/|\mathbf{B}|$, where $E_\parallel$ denotes that local projection and $|\mathbf{B}|$ is the local magnetic-field magnitude. Consequently, this quantity averages $|\mathbf{B}|E_\parallel$ and divides by $B_0$; it is not generally the direct surface average of $E_\parallel$. Unlike the [flux-surface-averaged parallel wave electric-field amplitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_parallel_wave_electric_field_amplitude), it retains signed contributions, allowing cancelation rather than averaging their amplitudes. Sign convention: Positive when the flux-surface-averaged electric–magnetic field dot product and the reference magnetic-field value $B_0$ have the same sign. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_parallel_inductive_current_density

description before

Flux-surface-averaged parallel current density driven by an inductive electric field.

description after

Flux-surface-averaged parallel inductive current density is the mean inductive current–magnetic-field dot product divided by a signed reference toroidal magnetic field.

documentation before

documentation after

Flux-surface-averaged parallel inductive current density is the resistively driven contribution to conventional plasma [current density](name:current_density), assigned to a closed magnetic flux surface. It represents the plasma response to the inductive electric field along the magnetic field. The defining relation is: $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $j_{\parallel,\mathrm{ind}}^{\mathrm{FS}}$ is this surface-assigned quantity, $\mathbf{j}_{\mathrm{ind}}$ is the inductively driven conventional current-density vector, $\mathbf{B}$ is the local magnetic-field vector, $|\mathbf{B}|$ is its magnitude, and $B_0$ is the nonzero signed reference toroidal magnetic field. Angle brackets denote the flux-surface average. The local [parallel inductive current density](name:parallel_inductive_current_density), $j_{\parallel,\mathrm{ind}}$, is the projection of $\mathbf{j}_{\mathrm{ind}}$ along $\mathbf{B}$. This definition applies to closed magnetic flux surfaces. Because of the magnetic-field factor and reference-field divisor, it is not simply the flux-surface average of the local parallel projection. It excludes bootstrap and other non-inductively driven contributions. With the same averaging and reference-field convention, adding the [non-inductive contribution](name:parallel_current_density_due_to_non_inductive_current_drive) gives the [total flux-surface-averaged parallel current density](name:flux_surface_averaged_parallel_current_density). The reference field is signed along the toroidal direction in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is upward vertical height. Sign convention: Positive when the surface-averaged inductive current–magnetic-field dot product has the same sign as $B_0$, with $B_0$ positive in the direction of increasing $\phi$. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_parallel_wave_electric_field_amplitude

description before

Peak strength of the wave electric field along the equilibrium magnetic field, averaged over a closed magnetic flux surface with complex phase discarded.

description after

Strength of the field-aligned coherent-wave electric field after power-deposition-weighted complex averaging over a closed magnetic flux surface.

documentation before

The flux-surface-averaged parallel wave electric-field amplitude is a surface mean of the local peak strength of the oscillating electric field along the equilibrium magnetic field. It retains amplitude information rather than the complex phase of the wave electric-field phasor. The geometric flux-surface average of the local amplitude is $$ \langle A_\parallel\rangle = \oint A_\parallel\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $A_\parallel$ is the local parallel wave electric-field amplitude, $\langle A_\parallel\rangle$ is its geometric flux-surface average, $S$ is the closed magnetic flux surface, $dS$ is its differential area, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux, and $\nabla\psi$ is its spatial gradient. The integrals cover the complete surface. The [toroidal-mode-resolved member](name:per_toroidal_mode_flux_surface_averaged_parallel_wave_electric_field_amplitude) retains each toroidal Fourier mode separately and specifies additional power-deposition weighting. Its surface mean is $\langle w A_\parallel\rangle/\langle w\rangle$, where $w$ is the local power-deposition weight and $\langle w\rangle$ is its geometric flux-surface average, which must be nonzero. Toroidal-mode resolution is not required at this parent scope. This quantity applies surface averaging to the local parallel wave electric-field amplitude; it is neither a volume integral nor a root-mean-square field. Averaging local amplitudes is distinct from averaging complex phasors before taking their magnitude, because phase cancelation is discarded. Unlike the [flux-surface-averaged parallel electric field at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_parallel_electric_field_at_plasma_boundary), it averages a nonnegative peak wave amplitude rather than a signed electric-field projection and is not restricted to the plasma boundary.

documentation after

This quantity is the amplitude obtained after averaging the complex, magnetic-field-parallel component of a coherent-wave electric field over a closed magnetic flux surface. Local wave-power deposition supplies an additional weighting within the surface average. The surface-representative complex field is defined by $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\overline{E}_{\parallel}$ is the deposition-weighted complex mean, $E_{\parallel}$ is the coherent-wave electric-field phasor projected along the local equilibrium magnetic field, $P_{\mathrm{dep}}$ is the [total absorbed wave power density](name:total_absorbed_wave_power_density), and angle brackets denote the flux-surface average. The requested amplitude is the modulus of this complex mean. The definition requires a nonzero denominator and a common phase reference for the phasors being averaged. Complex averaging precedes removal of phase, so spatial phase differences can reduce the resulting amplitude. This differs from averaging the local parallel-wave electric-field amplitude, which removes relative phase information before aggregation. It describes only the field-aligned component, not the total electric-field strength. The [toroidal-mode-resolved member](name:per_toroidal_mode_flux_surface_averaged_parallel_wave_electric_field_amplitude) applies this definition separately to each toroidal Fourier harmonic. The amplitude is the peak magnitude of a sinusoidally varying quantity. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_perpendicular_wave_vector

description before

Power-deposition-weighted mean of the wave number perpendicular to the local background magnetic field over a closed magnetic flux surface.

description after

Power-deposition-weighted mean wave-number magnitude perpendicular to the local background magnetic field over a closed magnetic flux surface.

documentation before

This quantity is the power-deposition-weighted mean of the perpendicular wave-number magnitude of a coherent plasma wave over a closed magnetic flux surface. The weighting emphasizes spatial phase variation where the wave transfers energy to the plasma. It is defined by $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $k_{\perp,\mathrm{dep}}$ is the weighted mean, $k_\perp$ is the local [perpendicular wave-number magnitude](name:perpendicular_wave_vector), and $P_{\mathrm{dep}}$ is the [total absorbed wave power density](name:total_absorbed_wave_power_density), the volumetric rate of energy transfer from the coherent wave to plasma particles. Angle brackets denote the flux-surface average $\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$, where $f$ is the quantity being averaged, $S$ is the closed magnetic flux surface, $dS$ is its area element, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux, and $\nabla$ is the spatial gradient operator. The denominator is therefore the [flux-surface-averaged total absorbed wave power density](name:flux_surface_averaged_total_absorbed_wave_power_density). The result is a deposition-weighted surface mean, not an unweighted surface average or a volume integral, and is undefined when the denominator vanishes. Perpendicular refers to the local background magnetic field, not specifically to the flux-surface normal; the local quantity is a magnitude rather than a signed directional projection. The declared unit is inconsistent with a wave number.

documentation after

This quantity is the power-deposition-weighted mean of the local perpendicular wave-vector magnitude of a coherent plasma wave on a closed magnetic flux surface. Perpendicular is relative to the local background magnetic field; the local quantity is a magnitude, not a signed component or a projection along the flux-surface normal. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. For each closed flux surface and each toroidal mode number, the average of the product of the local perpendicular wave vector and the local deposition density is $$\langle k_\perp P_{\mathrm{dep}}\rangle = \oint k_\perp P_{\mathrm{dep}}\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ and the quantity is the ratio $k_{\perp,\mathrm{dep}} = \langle k_\perp P_{\mathrm{dep}}\rangle / \langle P_{\mathrm{dep}}\rangle$. Here $k_{\perp,\mathrm{dep}}$ is this deposition-weighted mean, $k_\perp$ is the local [perpendicular wave vector](name:perpendicular_wave_vector), $P_{\mathrm{dep}}$ is the local [total absorbed wave power density](name:total_absorbed_wave_power_density), $S$ is the closed magnetic flux surface, $dS$ is its area element, $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux, and $\nabla$ is the spatial gradient operator. The denominator $\langle P_{\mathrm{dep}}\rangle$ is the [flux-surface-averaged total absorbed wave power density](name:flux_surface_averaged_total_absorbed_wave_power_density), and the mean is defined only where it is nonzero. This is a deposition-weighted surface mean, not an unweighted flux-surface average of $k_\perp$ and not a volume integral.

flux_surface_averaged_poloidal_magnetic_field_at_pedestal_top

description before

Poloidal magnetic field at the pressure pedestal top is the mean poloidal-plane field strength, weighted over the flux surface by the inverse poloidal-flux-gradient magnitude.

description after

Flux-surface-averaged poloidal magnetic-field magnitude at the pedestal top measures the equilibrium field strength excluding its toroidal component.

documentation before

Poloidal magnetic field at the [pressure pedestal top](name:normalized_poloidal_flux_coordinate_of_pedestal) is the average magnitude of the equilibrium magnetic-field projection onto the poloidal plane over the corresponding closed magnetic flux surface. The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \langle B_\mathrm{p}\rangle = \oint B_\mathrm{p}\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $B_\mathrm{p}$ is the local [poloidal magnetic-field strength at the pedestal top](name:poloidal_magnetic_field_at_pedestal_top), $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux, $\nabla\psi$ is its spatial gradient, $dS$ is the surface-area element, and the angle brackets denote the flux-surface average. Both integrals extend over the entire closed pedestal-top flux surface, with inverse gradient-magnitude weighting. In the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, $B_\mathrm{p}=\sqrt{B_R^2+B_Z^2}$, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, $Z$ is vertical position, and $B_R$ and $B_Z$ are the radial and vertical components of the equilibrium [magnetic field](name:magnetic_field). The poloidal-plane magnitude is formed locally before averaging; the result is neither a vector average nor a pointwise low-field-side or high-field-side value. Unlike the [flux-surface-averaged total magnetic-field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_magnetic_field_magnitude), this quantity excludes the toroidal component before taking the magnitude and averaging.

documentation after

Poloidal magnetic field at the [pedestal top](name:normalized_poloidal_flux_coordinate_of_pedestal) is the [flux-surface average](concept:flux_surface_averaged) of the magnitude of the equilibrium [magnetic field](name:magnetic_field) projected onto the poloidal plane. The local magnitude entering the average is $$ B_\mathrm{p} = \sqrt{B_R^2+B_Z^2} $$ where $B_\mathrm{p}$ is the poloidal magnetic-field magnitude, and $B_R$ and $B_Z$ are the radial and vertical magnetic-field components in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame. Here $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. This quantity is the flux-surface-averaged form of [poloidal_magnetic_field_at_pedestal_top](name:poloidal_magnetic_field_at_pedestal_top), aggregated over the closed magnetic flux surface at that locus rather than evaluated at a single point on its high-field or low-field side. The local magnitude is taken before averaging; it is not the magnitude of an averaged vector. Unlike [flux_surface_averaged_magnetic_field_magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_magnetic_field_magnitude), which includes all equilibrium field components, this quantity excludes the toroidal contribution at every point. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_ratio_of_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude_to_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude

description before

Flux-surface average of the squared toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient magnitude divided by the squared local magnetic-field magnitude, a metric coefficient for equilibrium geometry.

description after

Ratio of the squared gradient magnitude of the toroidal-flux radial coordinate to the squared total magnetic-field magnitude, averaged over a closed magnetic flux surface as an equilibrium metric coefficient.

documentation before

This coefficient describes the flux-surface-averaged geometric weighting of a toroidal-flux radial coordinate by the inverse square of the local magnetic-field magnitude. The defining relation is $$ \mathcal{G}_B = \left\langle \frac{|\nabla \rho_{\mathrm{tor}}|^2}{|\mathbf{B}|^2} \right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}} $$ where $\mathcal{G}_B$ is the averaged metric coefficient, $\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$ is the toroidal-flux radial coordinate, $\nabla$ is the spatial gradient, $\mathbf{B}$ is the magnetic-field vector, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}$ denotes the average over a magnetic flux surface. The quantity applies to nested equilibrium magnetic flux surfaces and uses the average of the pointwise ratio, not the ratio of separately averaged numerator and denominator. It is nonnegative because both the gradient magnitude and magnetic-field magnitude are squared. It is the flux-surface-averaged form of the local [ratio of squared toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient magnitude to squared magnetic-field magnitude](name:ratio_of_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude_to_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude). It shares the gradient-squared geometric factor with the [flux-surface-averaged squared toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude), but additionally applies the pointwise inverse-square magnetic-field weighting before averaging. It is distinct from the corresponding major-radius denominator variant, [flux-surface-averaged ratio of squared toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient magnitude to squared major radius](name:flux_surface_averaged_ratio_of_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude_to_square_of_major_radius).

documentation after

Ratio of the squared magnitude of the toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient to the squared magnitude of the total magnetic field, averaged over a closed equilibrium magnetic flux surface, defines this equilibrium metric coefficient. The coefficient is defined by $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\mathcal{G}_B$ is the metric coefficient, $\rho_\mathrm{tor}$ is the toroidal-flux radial coordinate, a radial flux label constant on each magnetic flux surface, and $\nabla\rho_\mathrm{tor}$ is its [spatial gradient](name:toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient). The vector $\mathbf{B}$ is the total magnetic field, $|\mathbf{B}|$ is its magnitude, and the subscript $\mathrm{FS}$ identifies the flux-surface average. The quantity applies to each closed equilibrium magnetic flux surface and aggregates over that complete surface; it is not a pointwise value or an average across distinct surfaces. It has no species or particle-population restriction. The denominator uses the total magnetic-field magnitude, not only its toroidal or poloidal component. The averaged integrand is the [local ratio of squared toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient magnitude to squared magnetic-field magnitude](name:ratio_of_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude_to_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude), so division occurs before averaging. Accordingly, this quantity is generally not the ratio of the [flux-surface-averaged squared gradient magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude) to the [flux-surface-averaged squared magnetic-field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude), nor the product of the averaged squared gradient magnitude and the [flux-surface-averaged inverse squared magnetic-field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude). Unlike the [major-radius-denominator variant](name:flux_surface_averaged_ratio_of_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude_to_square_of_major_radius), it weights the squared gradient by the inverse squared total magnetic-field magnitude. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. The magnitude is the Euclidean norm of a vector quantity, or the modulus of a complex one. The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The square is a scalar quantity multiplied by itself, or a vector's dot product with itself.

flux_surface_averaged_ratio_of_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude_to_square_of_major_radius

description before

Flux-surface-averaged magnetic-geometry coefficient measuring the squared toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient relative to the squared major radius.

description after

Ratio of the squared toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient magnitude to squared major radius, averaged over a closed magnetic flux surface as an equilibrium metric coefficient.

documentation before

This quantity is a magnetic-geometry metric coefficient that characterizes how a toroidal-flux radial coordinate varies across nested magnetic surfaces, with the variation weighted by inverse squared major radius. It is defined by $$ g_2 = \left\langle \frac{|\nabla \rho|^2}{R^2} \right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}} $$ where $g_2$ is the flux-surface-averaged metric coefficient, $\rho$ is the toroidal-flux radial coordinate, $\nabla\rho$ is its spatial gradient, $|\nabla\rho|$ is the gradient magnitude, $R$ is the major radius, and the subscript $\mathrm{FS}$ denotes the prescribed average over a complete magnetic flux surface. The quantity applies to an equilibrium magnetic flux surface and represents an aggregate over that surface rather than a pointwise local value. It has no species, population, or transport-channel restriction. It is the flux-surface average of the local ratio defined by [ratio of square of toroidal flux coordinate gradient magnitude to square of major radius](name:ratio_of_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude_to_square_of_major_radius). Unlike [flux-surface-averaged square of toroidal flux coordinate gradient magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude), it retains the inverse-square-major-radius weighting; the numerator is constructed from the [toroidal flux coordinate gradient magnitude](name:toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude).

documentation after

Ratio of the squared magnitude of the toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient to squared major radius, averaged over a closed equilibrium magnetic flux surface, defines this geometric metric coefficient. The coefficient is defined by $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\mathcal{G}_R$ is the metric coefficient, $\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$ is the toroidal-flux radial coordinate, and $\nabla\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$ is its [spatial gradient](name:toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient). The coordinate is constant on each magnetic flux surface. The quantity $R$ is the major radius, measured from the toroidal symmetry axis, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}$ denotes the flux-surface average. The quantity applies to each closed equilibrium magnetic flux surface and aggregates over that complete surface; it is neither a pointwise value nor an average across distinct surfaces. It describes magnetic geometry without species or particle-population weighting. The denominator is squared major radius, not squared magnetic-field magnitude. The averaged integrand is the [local ratio of squared toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient magnitude to squared major radius](name:ratio_of_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude_to_square_of_major_radius), so division occurs before averaging. Consequently, this coefficient is generally not the product of the [flux-surface-averaged squared gradient magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude) and the [flux-surface-averaged inverse squared major radius](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_square_of_major_radius). The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. The magnitude is the Euclidean norm of a vector quantity, or the modulus of a complex one. The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The square is a scalar quantity multiplied by itself, or a vector's dot product with itself.

flux_surface_averaged_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude

description before

Flux-surface average of the squared magnitude of the total equilibrium magnetic field, providing a geometric coefficient for equilibrium and neoclassical transport relations.

description after

Flux-surface-averaged squared magnetic-field magnitude is a geometric coefficient of the total equilibrium field in magnetohydrodynamic equilibrium and neoclassical transport relations.

documentation before

This quantity is the surface-averaged squared strength of the total magnetic field on a nested magnetic flux surface. It is a geometric metric coefficient used in equilibrium descriptions and in bounce-averaged and neoclassical transport relations. The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \left\langle B^2 \right\rangle_\psi = \frac{\displaystyle\oint_{S(\psi)} \frac{B^2}{\lvert\nabla\psi\rvert}\,dS}{\displaystyle\oint_{S(\psi)} \frac{1}{\lvert\nabla\psi\rvert}\,dS} $$ where $B^2$ is the [square of the magnetic-field magnitude](name:square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude), $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic-flux label of the surface, $S(\psi)$ is the closed magnetic flux surface with that label, $\nabla\psi$ is the gradient of the flux label, and $dS$ is the surface-area element. The quantity applies to the total equilibrium magnetic field, including all of its components, and is aggregated over each closed flux surface with the inverse-$\lvert\nabla\psi\rvert$ weighting shown above. It is not the pointwise value of $B^2$, the square of a flux-surface-averaged magnetic-field magnitude, or a single magnetic-field component. It is the flux-surface-averaged form of [square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude](name:square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude). It differs from [flux_surface_averaged_magnetic_field_magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_magnetic_field_magnitude), which averages the field magnitude before squaring, and from [flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude), which averages its reciprocal.

documentation after

Flux-surface-averaged squared magnetic-field magnitude is a magnetic-geometry coefficient for equilibrium and neoclassical transport relations, including bounce-averaged transport equations. It applies to the total equilibrium field on each closed nested magnetic flux surface. The coefficient is defined by $$ \langle B^2\rangle = \oint B^2\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $B$ is the total equilibrium magnetic-field magnitude, $B^2$ is its local [square](concept:square), $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic-flux label, $dS$ is the surface-area element, and $\nabla\psi$ is the gradient of the flux label. Both integrals extend over the closed magnetic flux surface labeled by $\psi$, and angle brackets denote the [flux-surface average](concept:flux_surface_averaged) on that surface. All magnetic-field components contribute through the total [magnitude](concept:magnitude); no individual component is selected. The definition requires finite surface integrals and a nonzero denominator. This is the surface-averaged form of the [local squared magnetic-field magnitude](name:square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude), rather than its pointwise value. Squaring precedes averaging: the result is generally neither the square of the [surface-averaged field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_magnetic_field_magnitude) nor the reciprocal of the [surface-averaged inverse-square field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_square_of_magnetic_field_magnitude). The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. The magnitude is the Euclidean norm of a vector quantity, or the modulus of a complex one. The square is a scalar quantity multiplied by itself, or a vector's dot product with itself.

flux_surface_averaged_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude

description before

Flux-surface average of the squared magnitude of the gradient of the normalized toroidal-flux radius. It is the averaged contravariant radial metric coefficient for the flux coordinate rho_tor.

description after

Mean-square toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient over a closed equilibrium magnetic surface, describing the geometric spacing of neighboring magnetic surfaces.

documentation before

Flux-surface average of the squared magnitude of the gradient of the toroidal flux coordinate is the average, over one magnetic flux surface, of the squared norm of $\nabla \rho_\mathrm{tor}$. It is the flux-surface-averaged contravariant radial metric coefficient associated with the radial coordinate in the flux-coordinate system $(\rho_\mathrm{tor}, \theta, \phi)$, where $\theta$ is a poloidal angle label and $\phi$ is the toroidal angle. The quantity is defined by the flux-surface average of the local radial metric component: $$ G_{\rho\rho}(\rho_\mathrm{tor}) = \left\langle |\nabla \rho_\mathrm{tor}|^2 \right\rangle_{\rho_\mathrm{tor}} = \frac{\int_{S(\rho_\mathrm{tor})} |\nabla \rho_\mathrm{tor}|^2\, dA / |\nabla \rho_\mathrm{tor}|}{\int_{S(\rho_\mathrm{tor})} dA / |\nabla \rho_\mathrm{tor}|} $$ where $G_{\rho\rho}$ denotes this flux-surface-averaged metric coefficient, $\rho_\mathrm{tor}$ is the normalized toroidal-flux radius, $S(\rho_\mathrm{tor})$ is the magnetic flux surface with that label, $\nabla$ is the spatial gradient operator, $dA$ is the surface-area element, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\rho_\mathrm{tor}}$ denotes averaging over that flux surface. The coordinate $\rho_\mathrm{tor}$ is constituted by $\rho_\mathrm{tor}=\sqrt{\Phi/\Phi_b}$, where $\Phi$ is the toroidal magnetic flux enclosed by the flux surface and $\Phi_b$ is its value at the plasma boundary. The scope is closed nested equilibrium flux surfaces; the aggregation convention is an average over the entire surface at fixed $\rho_\mathrm{tor}$. This is not the pointwise quantity $|\nabla \rho_\mathrm{tor}|^2$, not the flux-surface average of $|\nabla \rho_\mathrm{tor}|$, and not a volume or time average. It belongs to the same flux-surface-averaged geometric-metric family as the [flux-surface-averaged inverse major radius](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_major_radius), but it characterizes the radial metric of the normalized toroidal-flux radius rather than a major-radius factor.

documentation after

Flux-surface-averaged squared toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient is a geometric metric coefficient describing the spatial spacing of neighboring equilibrium magnetic surfaces. The coefficient is defined by $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\mathcal{G}$ is this metric coefficient, $\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$ is the toroidal-flux radial coordinate, $\nabla\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$ is its [spatial gradient](name:toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient), and angle brackets denote the flux-surface average. The coordinate is constant on each magnetic flux surface. The quantity applies to each closed equilibrium magnetic flux surface and aggregates over that complete surface, not across distinct surfaces. It is nonnegative, contains no directional projection, and has no species or particle-population weighting. No division by magnetic-field magnitude or major radius is included. Its integrand is the [local squared toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient magnitude](name:square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude), so squaring occurs before averaging. Consequently, this coefficient generally differs from the square of the [flux-surface-averaged gradient magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude). The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. The magnitude is the Euclidean norm of a vector quantity, or the modulus of a complex one. The square is a scalar quantity multiplied by itself, or a vector's dot product with itself.

flux_surface_averaged_time_averaged_normalized_energy_flux_due_to_e_cross_b_drift

description before

Normalized electrostatic energy flux across magnetic flux surfaces in a rotating frame, averaged over time and each surface and summed over perpendicular wavenumbers in nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence.

description after

Electrostatic energy flux across magnetic flux surfaces in a rotating frame, normalized, time- and surface-averaged, and summed over perpendicular wavenumbers in nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence.

documentation before

Electrostatic energy flux across magnetic flux surfaces represents transport of particle energy by the E×B drift generated by the perturbed electrostatic potential. This quantity is evaluated in the rotating frame for nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence, normalized, averaged over time and each closed flux surface, and summed over both perpendicular wavenumber components. Flux-surface averaging applies inverse-poloidal-flux-gradient weighting to the [time-averaged normalized energy flux due to E×B drift](name:time_averaged_normalized_energy_flux_due_to_e_cross_b_drift): $$ \langle \overline{\widehat{\Gamma}_W}\rangle = \oint \overline{\widehat{\Gamma}_W}\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ Here $\Gamma_W$ is the electrostatic energy flux summed over perpendicular wavenumbers, with $W$ denoting particle energy in the rotating frame. Its normalized value is $\widehat{\Gamma}_W=\Gamma_W/\Gamma_{W,\mathrm{ref}}$, where $\Gamma_{W,\mathrm{ref}}$ is a positive reference energy flux whose specific scaling is convention-dependent. The overbar denotes the time average, angle brackets denote the flux-surface average, $S$ is the closed magnetic flux surface, $dS$ is its area element, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux, and $\nabla$ is the spatial gradient operator. The result remains associated with each magnetic flux surface; it is neither a volume-integrated energy transfer nor a time-accumulated transfer. The perpendicular spectral contributions are summed rather than retained individually. Only the electrostatic contribution is included, excluding electromagnetic contributions such as the [energy flux associated with the perturbed parallel vector potential](name:energy_flux_normalized_due_to_perturbed_parallel_vector_potential).

documentation after

Electrostatic energy flux across magnetic flux surfaces is the energy-transport contribution carried by the E×B drift associated with the perturbed electrostatic potential. This quantity applies to nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence in a rotating frame and combines time averaging, flux-surface averaging, and summation over both perpendicular wavenumbers. The combined aggregation is $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\Gamma_W^{\mathrm{av}}$ is the resulting normalized rotating-frame energy flux, $\Gamma_{W,k_x,k_y}(t)$ is the unaveraged normalized electrostatic energy-flux contribution at perpendicular wavenumber components $k_x$ and $k_y$, and $t$ is time. The bounds $t_1$ and $t_2$ delimit the averaging interval, the subscript $W$ denotes particle energy, and angle brackets denote the [flux-surface average](concept:flux_surface_averaged). This is the flux-surface-averaged, perpendicular-wavenumber-summed rotating-frame member of [time-averaged normalized energy flux due to E×B drift](name:time_averaged_normalized_energy_flux_due_to_e_cross_b_drift). It retains the normalization of the [unaveraged normalized electrostatic energy flux](name:energy_flux_normalized_due_to_e_cross_b_drift). The wavenumber contributions are summed rather than averaged, while dependence on the magnetic flux surface is retained. Contributions attributed to the perturbed parallel vector potential or perturbed parallel magnetic field are excluded; this is not the total electromagnetic energy flux. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_time_averaged_normalized_perturbed_parallel_magnetic_field_magnitude

description before

Normalized parallel magnetic-fluctuation magnitude in nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence, averaged over time and flux surfaces, summed over kx, and resolved in ky.

description after

Flux-surface- and time-averaged normalized perturbed parallel magnetic-field magnitude is the amplitude of magnetic fluctuations along the equilibrium field, normalized by a reference field strength, in nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence.

documentation before

Flux-surface- and time-averaged normalized parallel magnetic-field perturbation magnitude measures compressional fluctuations along the local equilibrium magnetic field in nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence. It retains dependence on one perpendicular wavenumber after summation over the other. Let $a_\parallel(k_y)$ denote the time-averaged normalized parallel perturbation magnitude, summed over the perpendicular wavenumber $k_x$ at fixed perpendicular wavenumber $k_y$. The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \langle a_\parallel\rangle = \oint a_\parallel\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where angle brackets denote the flux-surface average, $S$ is the closed magnetic flux surface, $dS$ is its surface-area element, $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic-flux label, and $\nabla\psi$ is its spatial gradient. The surface integrals are evaluated at fixed $k_y$. The normalized magnitude entering the averages is $|\delta B_\parallel|/B_{\mathrm{ref}}$, where $\delta B_\parallel$ is the magnetic perturbation projected along the local equilibrium field and $B_{\mathrm{ref}}$ is the positive reference magnetic-field magnitude specified by the normalization convention. Time averaging applies over the specified averaging interval. The modulus is taken before averaging; this quantity is not the modulus of the averaged signed perturbation, a squared amplitude, or a turbulent transport flux. Summation over $k_x$ does not imply summation over $k_y$. This is the flux-surface-averaged specialization of the [time-averaged normalized parallel magnetic-perturbation magnitude](name:time_averaged_normalized_perturbed_parallel_magnetic_field_magnitude), with the wavenumber aggregation stated above. Unlike the [flux-surface-averaged magnetic-field magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_magnetic_field_magnitude), it describes only the normalized parallel perturbation, not the total equilibrium field strength.

documentation after

Flux-surface- and time-averaged normalized perturbed parallel magnetic-field magnitude is the amplitude of the perturbed magnetic-field component along the local equilibrium field, normalized by a reference field magnitude. It characterizes parallel magnetic-compression fluctuations in nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence. For each resolved perpendicular spectral wavevector pair, the averaged normalized amplitude is $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ Here $A$ is the averaged normalized amplitude, $k_x$ and $k_y$ are perpendicular spectral wavevector components, $t$ is time, and $B_{\mathrm{ref}}$ is the reference magnetic-field magnitude used for normalization. The parallel perturbation $\delta B_\parallel$ is the projection of the perturbed [magnetic field](name:magnetic_field) along the local equilibrium-field direction. The overbar denotes a time average, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_\psi$ denotes the [flux-surface average](concept:flux_surface_averaged) on the closed magnetic flux surface labeled by the [poloidal magnetic flux](name:poloidal_magnetic_flux) $\psi$. The spectral dependence is associated with $k_x$ and $k_y$: it may be resolved in both components or summed over $k_x$ at fixed $k_y$, retaining the dependence on $k_y$. The modulus is taken before the averages, so this is an average amplitude of the parallel perturbation, not a mean-square or root-mean-square amplitude. It excludes the equilibrium field and perpendicular perturbations. This is the flux-surface-averaged form of the [time-averaged normalized perturbed parallel magnetic-field magnitude](name:time_averaged_normalized_perturbed_parallel_magnetic_field_magnitude). The [normalized perturbed parallel magnetic-field magnitude](name:normalized_perturbed_parallel_magnetic_field_magnitude) is the corresponding local normalized amplitude without these time and flux-surface averages. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. The magnitude is the Euclidean norm of a vector quantity, or the modulus of a complex one.

flux_surface_averaged_time_averaged_normalized_perturbed_parallel_vector_potential_magnitude

description before

Electromagnetic fluctuation amplitude of the normalized parallel vector-potential perturbation in nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence, averaged over time and a closed magnetic flux surface at each wavevector.

description after

Time- and flux-surface-averaged amplitude of normalized magnetic-vector-potential fluctuations parallel to the equilibrium magnetic field in nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence.

documentation before

This quantity is the flux-surface average of the time-averaged magnitude of the normalized magnetic-vector-potential perturbation in nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence. The parallel component is the projection along the equilibrium magnetic field, and the magnitude is the modulus of the complex perturbation. The defining flux-surface average is $$ \langle \overline{|\widetilde A_\parallel|}\rangle = \oint \overline{|\widetilde A_\parallel|}\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $\widetilde A_\parallel$ is the normalized parallel vector-potential perturbation, the overbar denotes time averaging, and angle brackets denote flux-surface averaging. The integrals extend over the closed magnetic flux surface $S$, $dS$ is its area element, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux, and $\nabla$ is the spatial gradient operator. The result retains wavevector dependence: the surface and time averages do not imply summation over perpendicular wavevector components. It includes only the perturbation, not the equilibrium vector potential, and averages the magnitude rather than its square or the complex perturbation itself. It is the flux-surface average of the [time-averaged normalized parallel vector-potential perturbation magnitude](name:time_averaged_normalized_perturbed_parallel_vector_potential_magnitude). Its amplitude normalization is inherited from the underlying [normalized perturbation magnitude](name:normalized_perturbed_parallel_vector_potential_magnitude), rather than defined by the averaging operations.

documentation after

This quantity describes the electromagnetic fluctuation amplitude associated with the perturbed magnetic vector potential parallel to the equilibrium magnetic field in nonlinear gyrokinetic turbulence. It is the flux-surface average of the [time-averaged normalized parallel-vector-potential magnitude](name:time_averaged_normalized_perturbed_parallel_vector_potential_magnitude). For a resolved perpendicular wavevector, the defining composition is $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\mathcal{A}_{\parallel}$ is the averaged normalized fluctuation amplitude, $\delta\widehat{A}_{\parallel}$ is the normalized perturbation of the magnetic vector potential projected along the equilibrium magnetic field, $\mathbf{k}$ is the perpendicular wavevector, and $t$ is time. Vertical bars denote the perturbation magnitude, the overbar denotes averaging over the specified time interval, and angle brackets denote the flux-surface-average operator. The wavevector-resolved form retains both perpendicular wavenumbers. The reduced form additionally sums these nonnegative amplitudes over $k_x$, the first perpendicular wavenumber, while retaining dependence on the other perpendicular wavenumber; this sum is not a wavenumber average. The magnitude is taken before either average, so phase cancelation does not reduce the amplitude as it would for an averaged signed or complex perturbation. This is not a mean-square amplitude or magnetic energy. Only the perturbed vector potential is included, excluding the equilibrium contribution. The normalization is inherited from the underlying [normalized perturbation magnitude](name:normalized_perturbed_parallel_vector_potential_magnitude); neither average introduces an additional normalization. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. The magnitude is the Euclidean norm of a vector quantity, or the modulus of a complex one.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_argon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal bulk-flow velocity of elemental argon ions at the plasma boundary, combining charge states through a density-weighted mean.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of argon ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal bulk-flow velocity of elemental argon ions describes the toroidal component of their collective motion over the plasma-boundary flux surface. It includes the argon ion population rather than a single charge state. The boundary flux-surface average is defined by: $$ \left\langle v_{\phi,\mathrm{Ar}}\right\rangle_{\psi_b} = \frac{\displaystyle\int_{S_{\psi_b}} v_{\phi,\mathrm{Ar}}\,dS/|\nabla\psi|}{\displaystyle\int_{S_{\psi_b}} dS/|\nabla\psi|} $$ where $\psi$ is a scalar magnetic-flux label, $\psi_b$ is its value on the plasma boundary, $S_{\psi_b}$ is the complete boundary flux surface, $dS$ is its surface-area element, and $\nabla\psi$ is the spatial gradient of the flux label. The component $v_{\phi,\mathrm{Ar}}$ is defined in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, with $\hat{R}\times\hat{\phi}=\hat{Z}$; $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. For charge-state index $z$, with density $n_{\mathrm{Ar},z}$ and bulk velocity $\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{Ar},z}$, the local elemental-argon velocity is the density-weighted mean $\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{Ar}}=\sum_z n_{\mathrm{Ar},z}\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{Ar},z}/\sum_z n_{\mathrm{Ar},z}$. Thus, this quantity differs from the local, unaveraged [toroidal argon velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_argon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) and parallels the charge-state aggregation used for toroidal flux-surface-averaged deuterium velocity at the plasma boundary. Sign convention: Positive when the argon bulk-flow component points in the direction of increasing toroidal angle $\phi$.

documentation after

This quantity is the toroidal component of the argon-ion bulk-flow velocity, flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary. The projection is onto the toroidal unit vector $\hat{\phi}$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The local density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states is averaged as follows: $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $v_{\phi,\mathrm{Ar}}$ is the local charge-state-aggregated toroidal argon-ion velocity, $q$ is the positive ion charge number, $n_{\mathrm{Ar}^{q+}}$ is the number density of that charge state, and $v_{\phi,\mathrm{Ar}^{q+}}$ is its toroidal bulk-flow velocity. The brackets with subscript $b$ denote flux-surface averaging at the plasma boundary. Neutral argon is excluded. Charge-state velocities are combined through a local density-weighted mean, not summed; the subsequent spatial operation is the flux-surface average of the [pointwise toroidal argon velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_argon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), rather than a value at one boundary point. The [beryllium counterpart](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_beryllium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) uses the same projection, averaging operation, and locus for beryllium ions. Sign convention: Positive when the flux-surface-averaged toroidal argon-ion velocity is directed toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_beryllium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Density-weighted toroidal bulk-flow velocity of beryllium ions, flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary over all populated charge states.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of beryllium ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

This quantity is the toroidal component of the beryllium-ion bulk-flow velocity, averaged over the plasma-boundary flux surface. The toroidal projection is taken in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame onto $\hat{\phi}$. Let $\mathcal{S}_b$ denote the plasma-boundary, or separatrix, flux surface. The density-weighted combination of beryllium ion charge states is defined by: $$ \left\langle v_{\phi,\mathrm{Be}} \right\rangle_{\mathcal{S}_b} = \frac{\sum_q \left\langle n_{\mathrm{Be}^{q+}} v_{\phi,\mathrm{Be}^{q+}} \right\rangle_{\mathcal{S}_b}}{\sum_q \left\langle n_{\mathrm{Be}^{q+}} \right\rangle_{\mathcal{S}_b}} $$ where angle brackets denote flux-surface averaging over $\mathcal{S}_b$, $n_{\mathrm{Be}^{q+}}$ is the number density in m$^{-3}$, $v_{\phi,\mathrm{Be}^{q+}}$ is the toroidal bulk-flow velocity in m s$^{-1}$, and $q$ indexes all populated beryllium ion charge states. This intensive mean is distinct from the local quantity [toroidal beryllium velocity at the magnetic axis](name:toroidal_beryllium_velocity_at_magnetic_axis).

documentation after

This quantity is the toroidal component of the [beryllium-ion bulk-flow velocity](name:beryllium_velocity), flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary. The projection is onto the toroidal unit vector $\hat{\phi}$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The local density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states enters the average as follows: $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $V_{\phi,\mathrm{Be},b}$ is this boundary-averaged toroidal beryllium-ion velocity, $q$ is the positive ion charge number, $n_{\mathrm{Be}^{q+}}$ is the local number density of beryllium ions in that charge state, and $v_{\phi,\mathrm{Be}^{q+}}$ is their local toroidal bulk-flow velocity. The brackets denote flux-surface averaging, and the subscript $b$ specifies the plasma boundary. Neutral beryllium is excluded. Charge-state velocities are combined through a local density-weighted mean, not summed; the subsequent spatial operation averages that local velocity rather than selecting its value at one boundary point. The [hydrogen counterpart](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_hydrogen_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) uses the same projection, averaging operation, and locus for hydrogen ions. Sign convention: Positive when the flux-surface-averaged toroidal beryllium-ion velocity is directed toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_carbon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Density-weighted toroidal bulk-flow velocity of carbon impurity ions, averaged over the last closed magnetic flux surface at the plasma boundary.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of carbon ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal carbon velocity is the density-weighted mean toroidal bulk-flow velocity of carbon impurity ions over the last closed magnetic flux surface. All carbon ion charge states are included in the mean. The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \langle V_\phi \rangle_{\mathrm{FS}} = \frac{\oint V_\phi\,dl/B_p}{\oint dl/B_p} $$ where $V_\phi$ is the toroidal velocity component, $dl$ is poloidal arc length along the flux surface, and $B_p$ is the magnitude of the poloidal magnetic field. The quantity is distinct from the local [toroidal carbon velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_carbon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) and from velocities of other impurity species. Sign convention: Positive when the carbon-ion flow points in the direction of increasing toroidal angle $\phi$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R, \phi, Z)$ frame.

documentation after

This quantity is the toroidal component of the carbon-ion bulk-flow velocity, flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary. The projection is onto the toroidal unit vector $\hat{\phi}$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The local density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states enters the average as follows: $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $V_{\phi,\mathrm{C},b}$ is this boundary-averaged toroidal carbon-ion velocity, $q$ is the positive ion charge number, $n_{\mathrm{C}^{q+}}$ is the local number density of carbon ions in that charge state, and $v_{\phi,\mathrm{C}^{q+}}$ is their local toroidal bulk-flow velocity. The brackets denote flux-surface averaging, and the subscript $b$ specifies the plasma boundary. Neutral carbon is excluded. Charge-state velocities are combined through a local density-weighted mean, not summed; the subsequent spatial operation averages the [local toroidal carbon velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_carbon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) rather than selecting its value at one boundary point. The [oxygen counterpart](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_oxygen_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) uses the same projection, averaging operation, and locus for oxygen ions. Sign convention: Positive when the flux-surface-averaged toroidal carbon-ion velocity is directed toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_deuterium_tritium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Density-weighted toroidal bulk-flow velocity of an effective deuterium–tritium fuel species, averaged over the last closed flux surface at the plasma boundary.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of an effective single-species deuterium–tritium ion fuel, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

This quantity is the density-weighted toroidal bulk-flow velocity of deuterium and tritium treated as one effective fuel species, averaged over the last closed magnetic flux surface at the plasma boundary. $$ \left\langle v_{\phi,\mathrm{DT}} \right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}} = \frac{\left\langle n_{\mathrm{DT}} v_{\phi,\mathrm{DT}} \right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}}{\left\langle n_{\mathrm{DT}} \right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}} $$ Here, $v_{\phi,\mathrm{DT}}$ is the local toroidal bulk-flow velocity of the effective deuterium–tritium species, $n_{\mathrm{DT}}$ is its number density, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}$ denotes a flux-surface average over the plasma-boundary surface. This averaged quantity is distinct from the local value represented by [toroidal deuterium–tritium velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_deuterium_tritium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary).

documentation after

This quantity is the toroidal component of the effective deuterium–tritium ion bulk-flow velocity, flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary. The projection is onto the toroidal unit vector $\hat{\phi}$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The local velocity entering the average is $$ v_{\phi,\mathrm{DT}} = \mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{DT}}\cdot\hat{\phi} $$ where $v_{\phi,\mathrm{DT}}$ is the local toroidal bulk-flow velocity and $\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{DT}}$ is the effective fuel-ion bulk-velocity vector, defined as the density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states. The fuel mixture is represented by one fictitious species with mass $m_{\mathrm{DT}}=2.5m_u$, where $m_{\mathrm{DT}}$ is the effective species mass and $m_u$ is the atomic mass constant. This denotes an effective fuel species, not the deuterium–tritium fusion reaction channel. Neutral particles are excluded. This quantity applies flux-surface averaging to the [toroidal deuterium–tritium velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_deuterium_tritium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), rather than selecting its value at one boundary point.
