The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_deuterium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface-averaged deuterium-ion bulk-flow velocity along increasing toroidal angle on the last closed magnetic surface in the right-handed cylindrical (R, φ, Z) frame, with ionization states density-weighted.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of deuterium ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

This quantity is the flux-surface average of the toroidal component of the deuterium-ion bulk velocity on the plasma boundary, defined as the last closed magnetic flux surface. The deuterium velocity is the density-weighted mean over all deuterium ionization states. $$ \left\langle V_{\phi,D} \right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}} = \frac{\displaystyle \oint_{\mathcal{S}_b} V_{\phi,D}\,\frac{dS}{|\mathbf{B}|}}{\displaystyle \oint_{\mathcal{S}_b} \frac{dS}{|\mathbf{B}|}} $$ Here, $V_{\phi,D}=\mathbf{V}_D\cdot\hat{\phi}$ is the deuterium bulk-velocity projection onto the toroidal unit vector, $\mathcal{S}_b$ is the boundary flux surface, $dS$ is its surface-area element, and $|\mathbf{B}|$ is the magnetic-field magnitude. The projection is defined in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. Unlike [toroidal deuterium velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_deuterium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), this quantity is averaged over the entire boundary surface rather than evaluated at a local point. Sign convention: Positive when the deuterium bulk velocity has a component in the direction of increasing toroidal angle $\phi$.

documentation after

This quantity is the toroidal component of the deuterium-ion bulk-flow velocity, flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary. The projection is onto the toroidal unit vector $\hat{\phi}$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The local velocity entering the average is $$ v_{\phi,\mathrm{D}} = \mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{D}}\cdot\hat{\phi} $$ where $v_{\phi,\mathrm{D}}$ is the local toroidal deuterium-ion bulk-flow velocity and $\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{D}}$ is the deuterium-ion bulk-velocity vector, defined as the density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states. Only deuterium ions are included; neutral deuterium and other ion species are excluded. Because deuterium has only one positive charge state, its charge-state mean reduces to the singly ionized deuterium bulk velocity. This quantity applies flux-surface averaging to the [toroidal deuterium velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_deuterium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), rather than selecting its value at one boundary point. Charge-state velocities are combined through a local density-weighted mean, not summed, before spatial averaging. The [hydrogen-ion counterpart](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_hydrogen_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) uses the same projection, spatial averaging, and locus but describes hydrogen ions rather than deuterium ions. Sign convention: Positive when the averaged deuterium-ion toroidal flow is toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude

description before

Geometric metric coefficient formed by averaging the local magnitude of the toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient over each nested magnetic flux surface.

description after

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient magnitude measures how rapidly the flux coordinate changes with distance across neighboring equilibrium magnetic surfaces.

documentation before

This quantity is the flux-surface average of the local geometric rate at which the toroidal flux coordinate changes through physical space. It is a scalar metric coefficient associated with nested magnetic surfaces. The defining relation is $$ \mathcal{G}(\mathcal{S}) = \left\langle \left|\nabla \rho_{\mathrm{tor}}\right| \right\rangle_{\mathcal{S}} $$ where $\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{S})$ is the averaged metric coefficient, $\mathcal{S}$ is a magnetic flux surface, $\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$ is the toroidal flux coordinate labeling nested magnetic surfaces, $\nabla$ is the spatial gradient, and the angle brackets with subscript $\mathcal{S}$ denote averaging over that surface. The quantity applies to the complete nested magnetic flux surface and is aggregated over the surface rather than evaluated at a point, along a line, or over a volume. It represents the average of the gradient magnitude, not a directional component and not the local unaveraged value. It is obtained by applying flux-surface averaging to the local [toroidal flux coordinate gradient magnitude](name:toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude). Unlike [flux-surface-averaged square of toroidal flux coordinate gradient magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude), it averages the magnitude itself rather than its square.

documentation after

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient magnitude is a geometric metric coefficient describing the spatial spacing of neighboring equilibrium magnetic surfaces for a specified flux-coordinate scale. The coefficient is defined by $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\mathcal{G}$ is this metric coefficient, $\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$ is the toroidal-flux radial coordinate, and $\nabla\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$ is its [spatial gradient](name:toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient). The coordinate is constant on each magnetic flux surface, vertical bars denote magnitude, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}$ denotes the flux-surface average. The quantity applies separately to each closed equilibrium magnetic flux surface and aggregates over that complete surface, without species or particle-population weighting. It is neither a pointwise value nor an average across distinct surfaces. The averaged quantity is the [local toroidal-flux-coordinate gradient magnitude](name:toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude), so the magnitude is taken before averaging. This differs from taking the magnitude of an averaged gradient vector. It also differs from the root-mean-square gradient magnitude, which is the square root of the [flux-surface-averaged squared gradient magnitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_square_of_toroidal_flux_coordinate_gradient_magnitude). The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient. The magnitude is the Euclidean norm of a vector quantity, or the modulus of a complex one.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_helium_3_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal velocity of helium-3 ions at the plasma boundary (separatrix).

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of helium-3 ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

The flux-surface-averaged toroidal velocity of helium-3 ($^3$He) ions quantifies the bulk toroidal flow of this light helium isotope (2 protons, 1 neutron) evaluated on the last closed flux surface that separates the confined plasma from the open-field-line scrape-off layer. This boundary value characterizes net toroidal rotation of $^3$He ions at the separatrix, relevant to impurity transport, momentum balance, and plasma–wall interaction studies. The flux-surface average of the toroidal velocity component is defined as: $$ \langle v_\phi \rangle = \frac{\oint v_\phi \, dl / B_p}{\oint dl / B_p} $$ where $v_\phi = \mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{He_3}} \cdot \hat{\phi}$ is the ion fluid velocity projected onto $\hat{\phi}$ — the unit vector in the direction of increasing toroidal angle $\phi$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R, \phi, Z)$ frame — $B_p$ is the poloidal magnetic field magnitude, and $dl$ is the arc-length element along the separatrix contour. Toroidal rotation at the boundary is driven by external momentum sources such as neutral beam injection, intrinsic rotation arising from turbulent and neoclassical stresses, and the edge radial electric field. The quantity is inferred from charge exchange recombination spectroscopy (CXRS) using injected neutral beams that stimulate helium-3 charge-exchange emission lines whose Doppler shift encodes the local toroidal velocity. Boundary toroidal flow speeds for light ions in tokamak edge plasmas are of order $10^3$–$10^4$ m s$^{-1}$, depending on heating power, plasma current, and confinement regime. The analogous quantities for tritium and hydrogen are [toroidal_flux_surface_averaged_tritium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_tritium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) and [toroidal_flux_surface_averaged_hydrogen_velocity_at_plasma_boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_hydrogen_velocity_at_plasma_boundary); the generic multi-species form is toroidal_ion_velocity_at_plasma_boundary.

documentation after

This quantity is the toroidal component of the helium-3 ion bulk-flow velocity, flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary. The projection is onto the toroidal unit vector $\hat{\phi}$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The local velocity entering the average is $$ v_{\phi,{}^3\mathrm{He}} = \mathbf{v}_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}\cdot\hat{\phi} $$ where $v_{\phi,{}^3\mathrm{He}}$ is the local toroidal helium-3 ion bulk-flow velocity and $\mathbf{v}_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}$ is the helium-3 ion bulk-velocity vector, defined as the density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states. Singly and doubly ionized helium-3 are included; neutral helium-3, other helium isotopes, and other ion species are excluded. Charge-state velocities are combined through a local density-weighted mean, not summed, before spatial averaging. This quantity applies flux-surface averaging to the [toroidal helium-3 velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_helium_3_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), rather than selecting its value at one boundary point. The [helium-4 counterpart](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_helium_4_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) uses the same projection, spatial averaging, and locus but describes helium-4 ions rather than helium-3 ions. Sign convention: Positive when the averaged helium-3 ion toroidal flow is toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of helium-4 ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

This averaged quantity is distinct from the local toroidal helium-4 velocity at the plasma boundary; the corresponding isotope-specific construction for helium-3 is toroidal flux-surface-averaged helium-3 velocity at the plasma boundary.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_hydrogen_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Density-weighted toroidal bulk-flow velocity of hydrogen ions, averaged over the last closed magnetic flux surface and combining all ionization states.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of hydrogen ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

This quantity is the density-weighted mean toroidal bulk-flow velocity of hydrogen ions at the plasma boundary, combining all hydrogen ionization states. The average is taken over the last closed magnetic flux surface in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. $$ \bar v_{\phi,\mathrm{H}}^{\mathrm{FS}} = \frac{\sum_q \left\langle n_{\mathrm{H},q}v_{\phi,\mathrm{H},q}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}}{\sum_q \left\langle n_{\mathrm{H},q}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}} $$ Here $q$ indexes the hydrogen ionization states, $n_{\mathrm{H},q}$ is the number density of state $q$, $v_{\phi,\mathrm{H},q}$ is its toroidal velocity component, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}$ denotes the flux-surface average over the plasma boundary. The charge-state aggregation is a density-weighted mean, not a sum of velocities. It differs from the local quantity [toroidal hydrogen velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_hydrogen_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) because that quantity is not flux-surface averaged, and from [toroidal flux-surface-averaged ion velocity at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_ion_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), which represents the total ion fluid.

Only hydrogen ions are included; neutral hydrogen and other ion species are excluded. Because hydrogen has only one positive charge state, its charge-state mean reduces to the singly ionized hydrogen bulk velocity. This quantity applies flux-surface averaging to the [toroidal hydrogen velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_hydrogen_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), rather than selecting its value at one boundary point. flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_ion_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal bulk-flow velocity of the total ion fluid at the plasma boundary, representing the density-weighted mean over all ion species and charge states.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of a selected ion species, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

The toroidal component of the ion bulk-flow velocity, flux-surface averaged over the plasma boundary — the last closed flux surface forming the core–scrape-off-layer interface. For a plasma containing multiple ion species and charge states, the ion velocity is the density-weighted mean $$ v_\phi = \frac{\sum_s n_s \, v_{\phi,s}}{\sum_s n_s} $$ where $n_s$ is the number density and $v_{\phi,s}$ is the toroidal velocity of species $s$, with the sum running over all ion species and charge states present. The flux-surface average is evaluated as $\langle A \rangle = \oint A \, dl/B_p \,/\, \oint dl/B_p$, where $dl$ is the poloidal arc-length element and $B_p$ is the poloidal magnetic field magnitude, integrated along the plasma boundary flux surface. Species-resolved counterparts such as [toroidal_flux_surface_averaged_nitrogen_velocity_at_plasma_boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_nitrogen_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) and [toroidal_flux_surface_averaged_tritium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_tritium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) give contributions from individual ion species. Sign convention: Positive when $v_\phi$ points in the direction of increasing toroidal angle $\phi$.

documentation after

This quantity is the toroidal component of a selected ion species' bulk-flow velocity, flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary. The projection is onto the toroidal unit vector $\hat{\phi}$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The local velocity entering the average is $$ v_{\phi,i} = \mathbf{v}_i\cdot\hat{\phi} $$ where $i$ identifies the selected ion species, $v_{\phi,i}$ is its local toroidal bulk-flow velocity, and $\mathbf{v}_i$ is its bulk-velocity vector, defined as the density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states. Only ions of the selected species are included; neutral particles and other ion species are excluded. The quantity describes directed collective motion, not random thermal motion or a transport-model convection velocity. It is not an aggregate velocity of the combined ion fluid. This quantity applies flux-surface averaging to the [toroidal ion velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_ion_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) for the selected species, rather than selecting its value at one boundary point. Charge-state velocities are combined through a local density-weighted mean, not summed, before spatial averaging. The [neon-ion counterpart](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_neon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) specializes the same projection, spatial averaging, and locus to neon ions. Sign convention: Positive when the averaged toroidal flow of the selected ion species is toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_iron_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal component of the iron impurity ion bulk flow velocity at the plasma boundary (last closed flux surface).

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of iron ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

The toroidal component of the iron impurity bulk flow velocity, averaged over the magnetic flux surface coinciding with the plasma boundary, characterizes the net toroidal rotation of iron ions at the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average eliminates poloidal asymmetries in the local velocity distribution, providing a single scalar representative of the toroidal impurity flow at the edge. The flux-surface average of a quantity $f$ on a given flux surface is defined as: $$ \langle f \rangle = \frac{\oint f \, dl_p / B_p}{\oint dl_p / B_p} $$ where $l_p$ is the poloidal arc length along the flux-surface contour and $B_p$ is the poloidal magnetic field magnitude. Iron ($Z = 26$) is a high-$Z$ metallic impurity that enters the confined plasma through sputtering of plasma-facing components; its toroidal rotation at the boundary reflects edge momentum transport and neoclassical impurity flow physics. This quantity is measured via charge exchange recombination spectroscopy, exploiting Doppler shifts of iron charge-state emission lines recorded along multiple poloidal viewing chords; the flux-surface-averaged value is then reconstructed from multi-chord data. This name is directly analogous to boundary velocity names for lighter impurities such as [carbon](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_carbon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), [argon](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_argon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), and [neon](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_neon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary). Where poloidally resolved measurements are available, the local counterpart [toroidal iron velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_iron_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) provides additional information on poloidal asymmetries not captured by the flux-surface average. Sign convention: Positive when the iron ion velocity is directed in the direction of increasing toroidal angle $\phi$.

documentation after

This quantity is the toroidal component of the iron-ion bulk-flow velocity, flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary. The projection is onto the toroidal unit vector $\hat{\phi}$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The local velocity entering the average is $$ v_{\phi,\mathrm{Fe}} = \mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{Fe}}\cdot\hat{\phi} $$ where $v_{\phi,\mathrm{Fe}}$ is the local toroidal bulk-flow velocity of iron ions and $\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{Fe}}$ is their bulk-velocity vector, defined as the density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states. Only iron ions are included; neutral iron and other ion species are excluded. The quantity describes directed collective motion, not random thermal motion or a transport-model convection velocity. It is not the aggregate velocity of the combined ion fluid. This quantity applies flux-surface averaging to the [toroidal iron velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_iron_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), rather than selecting its value at one boundary point. Charge-state velocities are combined through a local density-weighted mean, not summed, before spatial averaging. The [xenon-ion counterpart](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_xenon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) applies the same projection, spatial averaging, and locus to xenon ions. Sign convention: Positive when the averaged toroidal flow of iron ions is toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_krypton_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Density-weighted toroidal bulk velocity of krypton ions, averaged over all ionization states and the last closed magnetic flux surface in the right-handed cylindrical (R, φ, Z) frame.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of krypton ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal krypton velocity is the density-weighted mean over all krypton ionization states on the plasma boundary, identified with the last closed magnetic flux surface. The toroidal direction is the direction of increasing angle $\phi$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame. $$ \left\langle v_{\phi,\mathrm{Kr}}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS},b} = \frac{\displaystyle\oint_{\psi=\psi_b} \left(\sum_q n_{\mathrm{Kr}^{q+}} v_{\phi,\mathrm{Kr}^{q+}}\right)\frac{dl}{B_p}}{\displaystyle\oint_{\psi=\psi_b} \left(\sum_q n_{\mathrm{Kr}^{q+}}\right)\frac{dl}{B_p}} $$ Here $q$ indexes krypton ionization states, $n_{\mathrm{Kr}^{q+}}$ is the number density of state $q$ in m$^{-3}$, $v_{\phi,\mathrm{Kr}^{q+}}$ is its toroidal bulk velocity, $dl$ is poloidal arc length, $B_p$ is the poloidal magnetic-field magnitude, and $\psi_b$ labels the boundary surface. This species-resolved quantity differs from the total-ion quantity toroidal flux-surface-averaged ion velocity at the plasma boundary; its locus differs from [toroidal krypton velocity at the magnetic axis](name:toroidal_krypton_velocity_at_magnetic_axis).

documentation after

The projection is onto the toroidal unit vector $\hat{\phi}$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The local velocity entering the average is $$ v_{\phi,\mathrm{Kr}} = \mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{Kr}}\cdot\hat{\phi} $$ where $v_{\phi,\mathrm{Kr}}$ is the local toroidal krypton-ion bulk-flow velocity and $\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{Kr}}$ is the krypton-ion bulk-velocity vector, defined as the density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states. Only krypton ions are included; neutral krypton and other ion species are excluded. The quantity describes directed collective motion, not random thermal motion or a transport-model convection velocity. It is not the aggregate velocity of the combined ion fluid. This quantity applies flux-surface averaging to the [toroidal krypton velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_krypton_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), rather than selecting its value at one boundary point. Charge-state velocities are combined through a local density-weighted mean, not summed, before spatial averaging. The [argon-ion counterpart](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_argon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) applies the same projection, spatial averaging, and locus to argon ions instead of krypton ions. Sign convention: Positive when the averaged toroidal krypton-ion flow is toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of lithium ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

Lithium ions comprise Li+ through Li3+, combined using their particle densities. $$ \left\langle v_{\phi,\mathrm{Li}}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}} = \frac{\sum_{q=1}^{3}\left\langle n_{\mathrm{Li}^{q+}}v_{\phi,\mathrm{Li}^{q+}}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}}{\sum_{q=1}^{3}\left\langle n_{\mathrm{Li}^{q+}}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}} $$ Here, $v_{\phi,\mathrm{Li}^{q+}}$ is the toroidal bulk-flow velocity of lithium charge state $q$, $n_{\mathrm{Li}^{q+}}$ is its number density, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}}$ denotes averaging over the separatrix. The toroidal component is defined in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame. The related flux-surface-averaged lithium density at the plasma boundary supplies the corresponding charge-state-summed density, not a velocity.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_neon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface average of the toroidal neon-ion bulk velocity on the last closed magnetic flux surface, with all ionization states combined by density weighting.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of neon ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

The quantity is the density-weighted mean toroidal bulk velocity of neon ions, averaged over the last closed magnetic flux surface at the plasma boundary. It includes ionized neon charge states and excludes neutral neon. The corresponding unaveraged local quantity is [toroidal neon velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_neon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary). For the flux surface labeled by $psi_b$, the defining average is $$ \overline{u}_{\phi,\mathrm{Ne}}(\psi_b)= \frac{\displaystyle\oint_{\psi=\psi_b}\left(\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{10} n_q\mathbf{u}_q}{\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{10} n_q}\cdot\hat{\phi}\right)\frac{dl}{B_p}} {\displaystyle\oint_{\psi=\psi_b}\frac{dl}{B_p}} $$ where $q$ is the neon charge state, $n_q$ is its number density, $\mathbf{u}_q$ is its bulk velocity, $\hat{\phi}$ is the toroidal unit vector, $dl$ is poloidal arc length, $B_p$ is poloidal magnetic-field magnitude, and $\psi_b$ is the boundary flux label. Components use the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame with basis $(\hat{R},\hat{\phi},\hat{Z})$. The density weighting gives the neon-ion fluid velocity rather than an unweighted average of charge-state velocities. The deuterium counterpart is [toroidal flux-surface-averaged deuterium velocity at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_deuterium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary). Sign convention: Positive when the density-weighted neon-ion velocity has a component in the direction of increasing toroidal angle $\phi$, along $+\hat{\phi}$.

documentation after

Only neon ions are included; neutral neon and other ion species are excluded. The quantity describes directed collective motion, not random thermal motion or a transport-model convection velocity. It is not an aggregate velocity of the combined ion fluid. flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_nitrogen_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal velocity of nitrogen ions at the plasma boundary (separatrix), with charge states combined by a density-weighted mean.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of nitrogen ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal nitrogen-ion velocity is the density-weighted mean of the toroidal bulk-flow velocities of nitrogen ions over the separatrix, the plasma boundary between closed and open magnetic field lines. Nitrogen ion charge states are combined as one intensive species quantity; their velocities are averaged, not summed. The defining charge-state and flux-surface average is $$ \langle v_{\phi,N}\rangle_{\mathrm{sep}} = \frac{\displaystyle\oint_{\mathcal S_{\mathrm{sep}}} \left(\frac{\sum_{q=1}^{7} n_{N^{q+}}v_{\phi,N^{q+}}}{\sum_{q=1}^{7} n_{N^{q+}}}\right) \frac{dS}{|\nabla\psi|}} {\displaystyle\oint_{\mathcal S_{\mathrm{sep}}}\frac{dS}{|\nabla\psi|}} $$ where $n_{N^{q+}}$ is the number density of nitrogen charge state $q$ in m$^{-3}$, $v_{\phi,N^{q+}}$ is its toroidal velocity component in m s$^{-1}$, $q$ ranges from 1 through 7, $dS$ is a surface-area element, and $\psi$ is a flux-surface label. The toroidal component uses the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. This quantity excludes neutral nitrogen. The related [flux-surface-averaged nitrogen density](name:flux_surface_averaged_nitrogen_density_at_plasma_boundary) is the corresponding charge-state-summed extensive quantity, while [toroidal flux-surface-averaged ion velocity](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_ion_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) combines ion species rather than isolating nitrogen.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_total_plasma_momentum_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface-averaged total plasma momentum in the toroidal direction at the plasma boundary is the signed flux-surface average, on the last closed magnetic flux surface, of the cumulative toroidal linear momentum enclosed by that surface, summed over electrons and all ion species. Positive values point along increasing toroidal angle φ in the right-handed cylindrical (R, φ, Z) frame.

description after

Signed toroidal linear momentum carried by electrons and all ion species, combined algebraically and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

Flux-surface-averaged total plasma momentum in the toroidal direction at the plasma boundary is the flux-surface average, on the last closed magnetic flux surface, of the cumulative toroidal component of plasma linear momentum enclosed by that surface. The cumulative momentum includes electrons and all ion species and is treated as a flux-surface scalar before averaging. The cumulative boundary value is defined by the enclosed-volume integral; writing the flux-surface average explicitly gives: $$ P_{\phi,\mathrm{FSA}}^{\mathrm{b}} = \frac{\oint_{\psi=\psi_\mathrm{b}} P_\phi(\psi_\mathrm{b})\, dA/|\nabla\psi|} {\oint_{\psi=\psi_\mathrm{b}} dA/|\nabla\psi|} = \int_{V_\mathrm{b}} \sum_s m_s n_s(\mathbf{x})\, v_{\phi,s}(\mathbf{x})\, dV $$ where $P_{\phi,\mathrm{FSA}}^\mathrm{b}$ is this quantity, $P_\phi(\psi_\mathrm{b})$ is the [toroidal total plasma momentum](name:toroidal_total_plasma_momentum) associated with the boundary flux label, $\psi_\mathrm{b}$ is the boundary value of the [poloidal magnetic-flux label](name:poloidal_magnetic_flux_at_flux_surface), $dA/|\nabla\psi|$ is the flux-surface averaging measure normalized by the denominator, $V_\mathrm{b}$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface, $dV$ is the volume element, $s$ runs over electrons and all ion species, $m_s$ is species mass, $n_s$ is species number density, and $v_{\phi,s}=\mathbf{v}_s\cdot\hat{\phi}$ is the toroidal velocity component in the right-handed cylindrical $(R, \phi, Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The product $m_s n_s v_{\phi,s}$ is the toroidal momentum density contribution of species $s$, so the flux-surface average is applied to the enclosed total $P_\phi(\psi_\mathrm{b})$, not directly to this local density. This quantity is not a boundary-surface integral of local toroidal momentum density, not the flux-surface average of the local density $g_\phi=\sum_s m_s n_s v_{\phi,s}$ alone, and not a major-radius-weighted angular-momentum measure. It is the flux-surface-averaged representation of toroidal total plasma momentum at the plasma boundary; the analogous cumulative value at the pedestal top is [toroidal plasma momentum at the pedestal top](name:toroidal_plasma_momentum_at_pedestal_top). Sign convention: Positive when the enclosed total toroidal momentum is in the direction of increasing toroidal angle $\phi$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R, \phi, Z)$ frame.

documentation after

This quantity is the flux-surface-averaged form of [total toroidal plasma momentum](name:toroidal_total_plasma_momentum), evaluated at the plasma boundary. It includes electrons and all ion species, with oppositely directed contributions combined algebraically. The species contributions combine according to $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $p_\phi$ is total toroidal linear momentum, $p_{\phi,e}$ is the electron contribution, $p_{\phi,i}$ is the contribution from ion species $i$, and $\phi$ is toroidal angle. The brackets with subscript $\mathrm b$ denote flux-surface averaging at the plasma boundary. Neutral-particle momentum is excluded. This quantity is linear momentum, not angular momentum, which requires an additional major-radius lever arm. It is also distinct from the [toroidal ion momentum flux](name:toroidal_ion_momentum_flux), which describes transport of ion toroidal momentum across a surface. Sign convention: Positive when the species-summed toroidal momentum points toward increasing $\phi$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius and $Z$ is vertical height. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_tritium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Density-weighted flux-surface average of the toroidal bulk-flow velocity of tritium ions on the last closed flux surface at the plasma boundary.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of tritium ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

The quantity is the toroidal component of the tritium-ion bulk-flow velocity, averaged over the separatrix, or last closed magnetic flux surface. The tritium value is the density-weighted mean over all tritium ion charge states and describes directed ion motion rather than thermal speed. The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \left\langle v_\phi \right\rangle_{\mathrm{FS}} = \frac{\oint_{\mathrm{sep}} v_\phi\, dl/B_p}{\oint_{\mathrm{sep}} dl/B_p} $$ where $v_\phi$ is the toroidal velocity, $dl$ is differential distance along the separatrix, and $B_p$ is the poloidal magnetic-field magnitude. The toroidal projection is defined in the right-handed cylindrical $(R, \phi, Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. Its charge-state aggregation complements [flux-surface-averaged tritium density at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_tritium_density_at_plasma_boundary); the corresponding total-ion quantity is [toroidal flux-surface-averaged ion velocity at the plasma boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_ion_velocity_at_plasma_boundary).

documentation after

This quantity is the toroidal component of the tritium-ion bulk-flow velocity, flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary. The projection is onto the toroidal unit vector $\hat{\phi}$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The local velocity entering the average is $$ v_{\phi,\mathrm{T}} = \mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{T}}\cdot\hat{\phi} $$ where the subscript $\mathrm{T}$ identifies tritium, $v_{\phi,\mathrm{T}}$ is its local toroidal bulk-flow velocity, and $\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{T}}$ is its bulk-velocity vector, defined as the density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states. Tritium has only one positive ionic charge state, so this local mean equals the bulk velocity of singly ionized tritium. Only tritium ions are included; neutral tritium and other ion species are excluded. The quantity describes directed collective motion, not random thermal motion or a transport-model convection velocity. It is not the aggregate velocity of a combined ion fluid. This quantity specializes the [selected-ion-species quantity](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_ion_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) to tritium and represents a spatial average rather than a value at one boundary point. Charge-state averaging is local and precedes spatial averaging; it does not introduce an additional density weight into the spatial average. The [deuterium counterpart](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_deuterium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) applies the same projection, spatial averaging, and locus to deuterium ions. Sign convention: Positive when the averaged toroidal tritium-ion flow is toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of tungsten ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

The right-handed cylindrical $(R, \phi, Z)$-frame toroidal component of the flux-surface-averaged tungsten ion bulk velocity at the plasma boundary is the density-weighted average of the tungsten impurity-ion bulk-flow component along $\hat{\phi}$ on the last closed magnetic flux surface. The species-level tungsten velocity is the density-weighted mean over all tungsten ionization states. The density-weighted flux-surface average is defined by: $$ V_{\phi,\mathrm{tungsten},b}^{\mathrm{FS}} = \frac{\displaystyle \int_{S_b} \left(\sum_q n_{\mathrm{tungsten},q} v_{\phi,\mathrm{tungsten},q}\right) \frac{dS}{|\nabla \rho|}} {\displaystyle \int_{S_b} \left(\sum_q n_{\mathrm{tungsten},q}\right) \frac{dS}{|\nabla \rho|}} $$ where $V_{\phi,\mathrm{tungsten},b}^{\mathrm{FS}}$ is this quantity, $S_b$ is the last closed magnetic flux surface, $q$ indexes tungsten ionization states, $n_{\mathrm{tungsten},q}$ is the local number density of ionization state $q$, $v_{\phi,\mathrm{tungsten},q}$ is its local toroidal bulk-flow component in the right-handed cylindrical $(R, \phi, Z)$ frame, $dS$ is the surface area element on $S_b$, and $\rho$ is the radial flux coordinate in a flux coordinate set $(\rho, \theta, \phi)$ labeling the nested magnetic flux surfaces. The factor $dS/|\nabla\rho|$ gives the volume-derivative flux-surface-average measure, and any monotonic relabeling of $\rho$ gives the same ratio on a given surface. This quantity represents the boundary value only; for a diverted boundary it is understood as the limit approaching the last closed magnetic flux surface from inside the confined plasma. It is distinct from the local [toroidal tungsten velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_tungsten_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), which is not flux-surface averaged, and from [toroidal tungsten velocity at the magnetic axis](name:toroidal_tungsten_velocity_at_magnetic_axis), which is evaluated at a different locus. Because velocity is intensive, aggregation over ionization states is by density-weighted mean rather than by summation of velocities. Sign convention: Positive when the density-weighted mean tungsten ion bulk-flow component points in the direction of increasing toroidal angle $\phi$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R, \phi, Z)$ frame.

documentation after

This quantity is the toroidal component of the tungsten-ion bulk-flow velocity, flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary. The projection is onto the toroidal unit vector $\hat{\phi}$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The local velocity entering the average is $$ v_{\phi,\mathrm{W}} = \mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{W}}\cdot\hat{\phi} $$ where $v_{\phi,\mathrm{W}}$ is the local toroidal tungsten-ion bulk-flow velocity and $\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{W}}$ is the tungsten-ion bulk-velocity vector, defined as the density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states. The subscript $\mathrm{W}$ identifies tungsten. Only tungsten ions are included; neutral tungsten and other ion species are excluded. The quantity describes directed collective motion, not random thermal motion or a transport-model convection velocity. It is not an aggregate velocity of the combined ion fluid. This quantity applies flux-surface averaging to the [toroidal tungsten velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_tungsten_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), rather than selecting its value at one boundary point. Charge-state velocities are combined through a local density-weighted mean, not summed, before spatial averaging. The [krypton-ion counterpart](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_krypton_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) applies the same projection, spatial averaging, and locus to krypton ions instead of tungsten ions. Sign convention: Positive when the averaged toroidal tungsten-ion flow is toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_xenon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface-averaged toroidal component of xenon ion bulk flow velocity at the plasma boundary (last closed flux surface).

description after

Toroidal bulk-flow velocity of xenon ions, density-weighted over all ionic charge states and flux-surface averaged at the plasma boundary.

documentation before

The flux-surface-averaged toroidal xenon velocity at the plasma boundary quantifies the mean azimuthal flow of xenon impurity ions around the torus, averaged along the last closed flux surface (separatrix). Xenon ($Z=54$) is a heavy noble-gas impurity used in radiative-mantle experiments to spread divertor heat loads, and its toroidal rotation reflects momentum balance between collisional coupling to the bulk plasma, neoclassical forces, and external toroidal momentum input. The flux-surface average of the toroidal velocity component $v_\phi^\mathrm{Xe}$ is defined as: $$ \langle v_\phi^\mathrm{Xe} \rangle = \frac{\oint v_\phi^\mathrm{Xe} \, B_p^{-1} \, dl}{\oint B_p^{-1} \, dl} $$ where $B_p$ is the poloidal magnetic field magnitude and the line integrals are taken along the plasma boundary contour. Toroidal impurity velocities at the separatrix are measured primarily by charge exchange recombination spectroscopy (CXRS), resolving Doppler shifts from charge exchange between injected neutral beam atoms and Xe ions; a poloidally distributed array of sightlines is necessary to reconstruct the flux-surface average. The non-averaged counterpart is given by [toroidal_xenon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary](name:toroidal_xenon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary), while analogous averaged quantities for lighter seeding species include [toroidal_flux_surface_averaged_neon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_neon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary) and [toroidal_flux_surface_averaged_argon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary](name:flux_surface_averaged_toroidal_argon_velocity_at_plasma_boundary). Sign convention: Positive when the xenon ion flow is directed in the sense of increasing toroidal angle $\phi$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R, \phi, Z)$ frame.

documentation after

flux_surface_averaged_total_ion_density_at_plasma_boundary

description before

Flux-surface-averaged number density of all charged ion species at the plasma boundary, summed over charge states and excluding neutral particles.

description after

Number density of ions averaged over the plasma boundary, summed over all ion species and positive charge states, with each ion counted once and neutrals excluded.

documentation before

Number density of ion The flux-surface average is defined by $$ \langle n_i\rangle = \oint n_i\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \, / \, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi| $$ where $\langle n_i\rangle$ is the averaged total ion number density, $n_i$ is the local total ion number density, $S$ is the plasma-boundary flux surface, $dS$ is its surface element, and $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux whose gradient magnitude is $|\nabla\psi|$. This quantity includes all charged ion species and is summed over their positive charge states; neutral particles are excluded. It is a flux-surface average rather than a local point value, a volume average, or a neutral-inclusive density. It is the averaged counterpart of the local [total ion number density at the plasma boundary](name:total_ion_density_at_plasma_boundary), replacing that pointwise value with inverse-poloidal-flux-gradient weighting. It differs from the [line-averaged total ion density](name:line_averaged_total_ion_density), which averages the same charge-state-summed ion population along a specified plasma chord rather than over the plasma-boundary flux surface. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. documentation after

Number density of ion. Applying the flux-surface average to the local [total ion density at the plasma boundary](name:total_ion_density_at_plasma_boundary) commutes with summation over species and charge states: $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $n_{i,\mathrm{tot}}$ is the local total ion number density, $s$ labels ion species, $n_s$ is the local species number density summed over all positive charge states, $q$ is the positive ionic charge number, and $n_s^{q+}$ is the local number density of that species in charge state $q$. Angle brackets denote flux-surface averaging at the plasma boundary. Each $\langle n_s\rangle$ is a [flux-surface-averaged ion density for one species](name:flux_surface_averaged_ion_density_at_plasma_boundary); this quantity sums those species contributions. Each ion is counted once, without weighting by charge number. Neutral particles and electrons are excluded. The result is a flux-surface average, not a local point value, volume average, or chord average. The last closed magnetic flux surface: the outermost flux surface on which field lines close on themselves, bounding the confined plasma. Where $\psi = \psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$, $\psi$ is poloidal magnetic flux; $\psi_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is its value on the last closed flux surface.
