helium_3_density

description before

Local number density of charged helium-3 ions, obtained by summing their populations over all included ionization states at a plasma location.

description after

Number density of helium-3 ions, counting all positive charge states but no neutral atoms, expressed as their local concentration per physical plasma volume.

documentation before

Helium-3 ion number density is the local count of charged helium-3 ions per unit plasma volume at a specified plasma location. It is summed over the singly ionized and fully stripped charge states and excludes neutral helium-3 atoms. $$ n_{{}^{3}\mathrm{He}}(\mathbf{x}) = \sum_{Z=1}^{2} n_{{}^{3}\mathrm{He},Z}(\mathbf{x}) $$ where $\mathbf{x}$ is the plasma location, $Z$ is the ionic charge state, and $n_{{}^{3}\mathrm{He},Z}$ is the number density of helium-3 ions in charge state $Z$, expressed in m$^{-3}$. The [volume-averaged helium-3 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_3_density) and [line-averaged helium-3 density](name:line_averaged_helium_3_density) are spatial averages of this local quantity over their respective domains.

documentation after

Number density of helium 3. The local number density is defined by $$ n_{{}^3\mathrm{He}} = \frac{d\mathcal{N}_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}}{dV} $$ where $n_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}$ is the helium-3 ion number density, $\mathcal{N}_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}$ is the number of helium-3 ions in physical volume $V$, and the derivative defines their local concentration in the continuum description. No particular plasma location or spatial averaging domain is prescribed. The [line-averaged helium-3 density](name:line_averaged_helium_3_density) is the chord-length mean of this local density along a specified path, whereas the [volume-averaged helium-3 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_3_density) is its volume-weighted mean over the confined plasma. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

helium_4_density

description before

Local number density of helium-4 ions per unit plasma volume, the principal measure of fusion ash concentration in deuterium-tritium burning plasmas.

description after

Number density of helium-4 ions, counting all positive charge states but no neutral atoms, expressed as their concentration per physical plasma volume.

documentation before

The helium-4 ion number density $n_{{}^4\text{He}}$ quantifies the local concentration of ${}^4\text{He}$ nuclei at a given point in the plasma. At fusion-relevant temperatures (several keV), helium-4 is fully stripped to He$^{2+}$, and in deuterium-tritium burning plasmas it accumulates as the primary fusion ash product of the reaction ${}^2\text{H} + {}^3\text{H} \rightarrow {}^4\text{He} + n$; its buildup dilutes the fuel and reduces the fusion reaction rate if not exhausted by the divertor system. The time evolution of $n_{{}^4\text{He}}$ is governed by the particle continuity equation $$ \frac{\partial n_{{}^4\text{He}}}{\partial t} + \nabla \cdot \boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{{}^4\text{He}} = S_{{}^4\text{He}} $$ where $\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{{}^4\text{He}}$ is the helium-4 particle flux and $S_{{}^4\text{He}}$ is the volumetric source rate from fusion reactions and ionization of recycled helium neutrals at the plasma edge. The helium-4 density is measured primarily via charge exchange recombination spectroscopy, where injected neutral beam atoms undergo charge exchange with He$^{2+}$ ions to produce line emission from excited helium atoms; passive spectroscopy of edge recycling emission provides a complementary approach. Chord-averaged and volume-averaged forms are provided by [line_averaged_helium_4_density](name:line_averaged_helium_4_density) and [volume_averaged_helium_4_density](name:volume_averaged_helium_4_density) respectively, while [helium_4_density_at_plasma_boundary](name:helium_4_density_at_plasma_boundary) gives the boundary value. In D-T burning plasmas, central He-4 densities are in the range $10^{18}$-$10^{19}$ m$^{-3}$, with the helium-to-electron density ratio — captured by [fraction](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_helium_4_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density) — kept below approximately 5-10% in steady state to avoid excessive fuel dilution.

documentation after

Number density of helium 4. The underlying local density is the zeroth velocity-space moment: $$ n_{\mathrm{He4}} = \int f_{\mathrm{He4}}(\mathbf{v})\,d^3v $$ where $n_{\mathrm{He4}}$ is the helium-4 ion number density, $f_{\mathrm{He4}}$ is the local velocity distribution of the counted ion population, $\mathbf{v}$ is particle velocity, and $d^3v$ is the velocity-space volume element. The integral extends over all particle velocities. This quantity is isotope-specific and distinct from [helium-3 density](name:helium_3_density). It selects no particular plasma region or spatial averaging operation. The [line-averaged helium-4 density](name:line_averaged_helium_4_density) is the chord-length mean of this local density, whereas the [volume-averaged helium-4 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_4_density) is its volume mean over the confined plasma. Both averages retain the same counted population. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

hydrogen_density

description before

Local number density of hydrogen (protium) ions in the plasma, giving the count of H+ particles per unit volume at a specified spatial position.

description after

Number density of hydrogen (protium) ions in the plasma, providing the concentration underlying local values and spatially averaged forms. The ions of the species are counted over all charge states, with neutral atoms excluded.

documentation before

The hydrogen density quantifies the local concentration of protium ions ($^1$H$^+$, the lightest hydrogen isotope with mass number $A = 1$) at a point in the plasma. It is distinct from the neutral atomic hydrogen density and from the number densities of heavier hydrogenic isotopes such as deuterium ($^2$H) and tritium ($^3$H). The number density $n_H$ at position $\mathbf{r}$ is defined as $$ n_H(\mathbf{r}) = \frac{dN_H}{dV} $$ where $N_H$ is the number of hydrogen ions within the infinitesimal volume element $dV$ centered at $\mathbf{r}$. Spatially averaged forms provide experimentally accessible measures of the same quantity: [line_averaged_hydrogen_density](name:line_averaged_hydrogen_density) gives the path-length-normalized ion count along a spectroscopic or interferometric chord, while [volume_averaged_hydrogen_density](name:volume_averaged_hydrogen_density) integrates the ion content over the full plasma volume. The isotopic composition of the hydrogenic fuel is tracked by [ratio_of_hydrogen_density_to_total_hydrogenic_density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_hydrogen_density_to_volume_averaged_total_hydrogenic_density), which expresses $n_H$ relative to the combined protium, deuterium, and tritium ion density. The aggregate over all three hydrogen isotopes is given by hydrogenic_density, and the full multi-species ion sum by [total_ion_density](name:total_ion_number_density).

documentation after

Number density of hydrogen. Its local value is the zeroth velocity-space moment: $$ n_{\mathrm{H}} = \int f_{\mathrm{H}^{+}}(\mathbf{v})\,d^3v $$ where $n_{\mathrm{H}}$ is the hydrogen-ion number density, $f_{\mathrm{H}^{+}}$ is the protium-ion velocity distribution function, $\mathbf{v}$ is particle velocity, and $d^3v$ is the velocity-space volume element. The integral covers the full velocity distribution without selecting a thermal or fast-particle subset. Hydrogen here denotes protium, not deuterium or tritium. Like [argon density](name:argon_density), this is a species-specific concentration rather than the combined density of all ion species; no particular spatial average or plasma region is prescribed. The [line-averaged hydrogen density](name:line_averaged_hydrogen_density) is its path-length-normalized integral along a specified chord, whereas the [volume-averaged hydrogen density](name:volume_averaged_hydrogen_density) is its volume-normalized integral over the specified plasma volume. The [hydrogen-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_hydrogen_density_to_electron_density) divides this hydrogen-ion concentration by the free-electron concentration and is a relative abundance rather than a number density. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

hydrogenic_density

description before

Charge-state- and isotope-summed number density of hydrogenic particles in the plasma.

description after

Number density of a selected species' ions, combining its charged states while excluding neutral atoms, expressed as a concentration per physical volume.

documentation before

Hydrogenic particle density is the local abundance of hydrogen isotopes, including every included ionization state, expressed per unit volume. $$ n_{\mathrm{H}} = \sum_{s \in \mathcal{H}} \sum_q n_{s,q} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{H}}$ is the total hydrogenic number density, $\mathcal{H}$ is the set of included hydrogenic isotopes, $q$ indexes their charge states, and $n_{s,q}$ is the number density of isotope $s$ in state $q$. The quantity includes all charge states and isotopic variants and is not restricted to a particular isotope, ionization state, or neutral population. The concrete catalog instance is [total hydrogenic density](name:total_hydrogenic_density).

documentation after

Number density of hydrogenic. The local density is defined by $$ n_s(\mathbf{x}) = \int f_s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{v})\,d^3v $$ where $n_s$ is the ion number density, $s$ labels the selected population, $\mathbf{x}$ is position, $\mathbf{v}$ is particle velocity, $f_s$ is that population's velocity distribution per physical volume, and $d^3v$ is the velocity-space volume element. The integral covers all particle velocities. This general quantity selects no particular isotope, location, or spatial averaging convention. Line-averaged and volume-averaged densities are spatial averages of the concentration, rather than integrated particle inventories. Number density counts ions without electric-charge weighting and is therefore distinct from charge density. [Hydrogen density](name:hydrogen_density) and [deuterium density](name:deuterium_density) specialize this definition to individual hydrogen isotopes; a grouped population includes the explicitly selected species. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

iron_density

description before

Total number density of iron impurity ions in the plasma, summed over all ionization states from singly to fully ionized iron (atomic number Z = 26).

description after

Number density of iron ions summed over positive charge states, with each ion counted once and neutral iron excluded.

documentation before

Iron density is the total number density of iron ions present in the plasma, aggregated over all charge states from Fe$^{1+}$ through Fe$^{26+}$ (atomic number $Z = 26$). It characterizes the iron impurity content without resolving the per-charge-state distribution, and may refer to a local point value or a spatially averaged quantity depending on the sampling convention. The total iron ion number density $n_\mathrm{Fe}$ is defined as: $$ n_\mathrm{Fe} = \sum_{z=1}^{26} n_{\mathrm{Fe}^{z+}} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Fe}^{z+}}$ is the number density of iron in charge state $z$. Iron enters confined plasmas primarily through physical sputtering of high-$Z$ plasma-facing components. Spectroscopic measurements in the vacuum-ultraviolet and soft X-ray ranges resolve individual charge-state emission lines; the total iron density is reconstructed by summing ionization-state-resolved densities derived from ionization balance calculations. This general quantity is concretized by spatial-sampling variants: flux_surface_averaged_iron_density averages the local density over a magnetic flux surface, [line_averaged_iron_density](name:line_averaged_iron_density) represents a chord-integrated value divided by path length, and [volume_averaged_iron_density](name:volume_averaged_iron_density) integrates over the full plasma volume. Position-specific forms are available at key radial locations such as [iron_density_at_magnetic_axis](name:iron_density_at_magnetic_axis), iron_density_at_pedestal, and [iron_density_at_limiter](name:iron_density_at_limiter).

documentation after

Number density of iron. The density is defined by $$ n_{\mathrm{Fe}} = \sum_{q=1}^{26} n_{\mathrm{Fe}^{q+}} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Fe}}$ is the iron-ion number density, $q$ is the positive ionic charge number, and $n_{\mathrm{Fe}^{q+}}$ is the number density of iron ions in charge state $q$; 26 is the atomic number of iron. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded. No particular plasma location or spatial averaging operation is implied. Each ion contributes one particle rather than a contribution weighted by its charge. Evaluation on the last closed magnetic flux surface gives [iron density at the plasma boundary](name:iron_density_at_plasma_boundary). The [line-averaged iron density](name:line_averaged_iron_density) is the path-length mean of this density, whereas [volume-averaged iron density](name:volume_averaged_iron_density) is its volume mean over the confined plasma. This density is also the numerator of the [ratio of iron density to electron density](name:ratio_of_iron_density_to_electron_density), which expresses iron-ion abundance relative to free electrons rather than an absolute concentration.

krypton_density

description before

Number density of krypton ions at a point in the plasma, summed over all ionized charge states from Kr1+ through fully stripped Kr36+ and excluding neutral krypton atoms.

description after

Number density of krypton ions across all positive charge states, excluding neutral krypton, expressed as their concentration per physical plasma volume.

documentation before

Number density of krypton ions at a point in the plasma, summed over all ionized charge states from Kr$^{1+}$ through fully stripped Kr$^{36+}$; neutral krypton atoms are excluded. With krypton atomic number $Z_\mathrm{Kr}=36$, the ion density is the charge-state sum: $$ n_\mathrm{Kr} = \sum_{q=1}^{36} n_\mathrm{Kr}^{(q)} $$ where $n_\mathrm{Kr}^{(q)}$ is the number density of krypton ions in charge state $q$ and the neutral state $q=0$ is omitted from the sum. This is an extensive, charge-state-summed species density, not a density-weighted mean over charge states and not the charge-state-resolved [ion charge-state density](name:ion_charge_state_density), which retains the individual $n_\mathrm{Kr}^{(q)}$ terms. It is an absolute density, distinct from the relative [krypton fraction](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_krypton_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density) normalized to the electron density. The locus-specific values [krypton_density_at_magnetic_axis](name:krypton_density_at_magnetic_axis), krypton_density_at_pedestal, and [krypton_density_at_divertor_target](name:krypton_density_at_divertor_target) share this ions-only charge-state scope. In contrast, [volume_averaged_krypton_density](name:volume_averaged_krypton_density) includes all krypton charge states from neutral through fully stripped, so its charge-state scope differs from this ions-only local quantity; the [line_averaged_krypton_density](name:line_averaged_krypton_density) likewise carries its own averaging definition.

documentation after

Number density of krypton. The local number density is defined by $$ n_{\mathrm{Kr}} = \frac{d\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{Kr}}}{dV} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Kr}}$ is the local krypton-ion number density, $\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{Kr}}$ is the krypton-ion count, and $V$ is physical plasma volume. This is the krypton-ion specialization of [particle number density](name:particle_density), distinct from mass density and charge density. The [line-averaged krypton density](name:line_averaged_krypton_density) is its chord-length mean, whereas the [volume-averaged krypton density](name:volume_averaged_krypton_density) is its volume mean over the confined plasma inside the last closed flux surface. Neither spatial averaging operation is implicit in the local density. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_averaged_argon_density

description before

Chord-averaged number density of argon ions along a specified plasma path, with contributions from all ionization stages summed together.

description after

Number density of argon ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, including all positive charge states and excluding neutral argon atoms.

documentation before

Line-averaged argon ion number density is the argon ion concentration averaged along a specified chord through the plasma, including all ionization stages. The line average is defined by $$ \overline{n}_\mathrm{Ar} = \frac{1}{L} \int_0^L \sum_{q=1}^{18} n_{\mathrm{Ar},q}(\mathbf{r}(l))\,dl $$ where $L$ is the chord length, $l$ is distance along the chord, $\mathbf{r}(l)$ is the position on the chord, and $n_{\mathrm{Ar},q}$ is the local number density of argon ions in charge state $q$. This path average is distinct from the local [argon ion density](name:argon_density) and from the plasma-volume average [volume-averaged argon density](name:volume_averaged_argon_density).

documentation after

Number density of argon. The argon-specific chord average is $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm{Ar}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L n_{\mathrm{Ar}}\,dl $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{Ar}}$ is the line-averaged argon ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{Ar}}$ is the local argon ion number density evaluated along the chord, $L$ is the chord length, and $l$ is distance along the chord. This chord-specific average is not a local value at an individual point; it equals the local density throughout the chord when that density is uniform. It differs from [volume-averaged argon density](name:volume_averaged_argon_density), which averages the same population over the confined plasma volume. The [line-averaged xenon density](name:line_averaged_xenon_density) applies the same chord-averaging convention to xenon instead of argon. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_averaged_beryllium_density

description before

Chord-average of the beryllium ion number density along a diagnostic line of sight, equal to the path integral of the local beryllium density divided by the chord length.

description after

Number density of beryllium ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, including all positive charge states and excluding neutral beryllium atoms.

documentation before

The line-averaged beryllium ion number density quantifies the mean beryllium ion content along a plasma diagnostic chord, normalized by chord length. It is the chord-integrated analog of the local [beryllium_density](name:beryllium_density) and is the most directly accessible measure of beryllium impurity concentration from line-of-sight diagnostics. The quantity is defined as: $$ \bar{n}_{Be} = \frac{1}{L} \int_0^L n_{Be}(l) \, dl $$ where $n_{Be}(l)$ is the local beryllium ion number density at path coordinate $l$ along the chord and $L$ is the total chord length. Inferred from passive spectroscopy of beryllium emission lines by inverting chord-integrated emission intensities using atomic emissivity coefficients; the result aggregates contributions from all beryllium ionization states present along the chord. The [volume_averaged_beryllium_density](name:volume_averaged_beryllium_density) provides a complementary global volume average, while [beryllium_density_at_magnetic_axis](name:beryllium_density_at_magnetic_axis) gives the local on-axis value. Sign convention: Positive when beryllium ions populate the measurement chord.

documentation after

Number density of beryllium. The beryllium-specific chord average is $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm{Be}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L n_{\mathrm{Be}}\,dl $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{Be}}$ is the line-averaged beryllium ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{Be}}$ is the local [beryllium density](name:beryllium_density) evaluated along the chord, $L$ is the stated chord length, and $l$ is distance along that chord. This quantity averages the local beryllium density over the stated chord rather than evaluating it at a prescribed point, as [beryllium density at the plasma boundary](name:beryllium_density_at_plasma_boundary) does. It is the beryllium counterpart of [line-averaged helium-4 density](name:line_averaged_helium_4_density) under the same chord-averaging convention. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_averaged_deuterium_density

description before

Deuterium ion density averaged along a plasma chord is the mean local number density of ionized deuterium in the fuel-ion population. It is charge-state-summed over ionized deuterium and excludes neutral or molecular deuterium and non-deuterium species.

description after

Number density of deuterium ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, including all positive charge states and excluding neutral deuterium atoms.

documentation before

Deuterium ion density averaged along a plasma chord is the mean local number density of ionized deuterium in the fuel-ion population. It represents the path-length mean of the local deuterium density along the specified chord, with deuterium treated as a species-level ion density. The line average is defined by the path-length average of the local [deuterium density](name:deuterium_density): $$ \langle n_D \rangle_\ell = \frac{1}{L} \int_{\ell_0}^{\ell_1} n_D(\ell) \, d\ell $$ where $\langle n_D \rangle_\ell$ is the line-averaged deuterium density, $\ell$ is arclength along the chord, $\ell_0$ and $\ell_1$ are the endpoints of the averaging interval, $L = \ell_1 - \ell_0$ is the chord path length, and $n_D(\ell)$ is the local [deuterium density](name:deuterium_density) at position $\ell$. The local deuterium density entering the integral is charge-state-summed over ionized atomic charge states; for deuterium this reduces to the singly ionized deuteron population. Neutral deuterium atoms, molecular deuterium, and non-deuterium species are excluded. This quantity is a path average; local [deuterium density](name:deuterium_density) and [deuterium ion density averaged over the total plasma volume](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_density) are distinct quantities.

documentation after

Number density of deuterium. Deuterium has only one positive atomic charge state, giving the species-specific identity $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm D} = \bar{n}_{\mathrm D^{+}} $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm D}$ is the line-averaged deuterium ion number density and $\bar{n}_{\mathrm D^{+}}$ is the line-averaged number density of singly ionized deuterium, whose ions are deuterons. This chord-specific quantity is not a local density at a single point; it equals the local density throughout the chord only when that density is uniform. It differs from [volume-averaged deuterium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_density), which averages the same ion population over the plasma volume. It is the deuterium counterpart of [line-averaged hydrogen density](name:line_averaged_hydrogen_density), with the isotope population changed from protium to deuterium. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density

description before

Chord-averaged ion number density of the combined deuterium-tritium fuel pseudo-species along a specified line of sight.

description after

Number density of deuterium–tritium fuel ions treated as one effective species and averaged along a plasma chord, including all ionic charge states and excluding neutral atoms.

documentation before

The deuterium-tritium density is the chord-averaged ion count per unit volume of the fictitious combined fuel pseudo-species formed by merging deuterium and tritium ions. It is derived from the local [deuterium-tritium density](name:deuterium_tritium_density) along a specified chord. The chord average is defined by $$ \bar{n}_{DT} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L n_{DT}(l)\,dl $$ where $\bar{n}_{DT}$ is the chord-averaged density, $L$ is the chord length, $l$ is distance along the chord, and $n_{DT}(l)$ is the local deuterium-tritium pseudo-species density. The quantity is a line average and must not be interpreted as the [volume-averaged deuterium-tritium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density); both retain the deuterium-plus-tritium aggregation convention.

documentation after

Number density of deuterium tritium. Line averaging preserves the effective-species density relation: $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm{DT}} = \bar{n}_{\mathrm{D}} + \bar{n}_{\mathrm{T}} $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{DT}}$ is the line-averaged effective deuterium–tritium ion number density, $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{D}}$ is the [line-averaged deuterium ion number density](name:line_averaged_deuterium_density), and $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{T}}$ is the line-averaged tritium ion number density. All three averages refer to the same chord segment. The local quantity being averaged is [deuterium–tritium density](name:deuterium_tritium_density). This chord-specific mean is distinct from a plasma-volume average and does not generally equal the local density at any specified point. Deuterium–tritium denotes the effective fuel species here, not the fusion reaction channel. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_averaged_effective_charge

description before

Effective charge of a plasma ion mixture averaged uniformly along a specified line of sight, expressing its chord-mean composition factor for electron–ion Coulomb scattering.

description after

Effective charge of a plasma ion mixture averaged along a specified viewing chord, expressing its mean composition factor for electron–ion Coulomb scattering.

documentation before

Effective charge averaged along a specified plasma line of sight describes the chord-mean charge-state composition of the ion mixture relevant to electron–ion Coulomb scattering. The line average is defined by: $$ \bar{Z}_{\mathrm{eff}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L Z_{\mathrm{eff}}\,dl $$ where $\bar{Z}_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the line-averaged effective charge, $Z_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the local [effective charge](name:effective_charge), $L$ is the length of the specified plasma chord, and $l$ is distance along that chord. The local composition factor is $Z_{\mathrm{eff}} = \sum_a n_a Z_a^2/n_e$, where $a$ indexes all ion species and their ionized charge states, $n_a$ is the number density of each ionic population, $Z_a$ is its charge number, and $n_e$ is the free-electron number density. The mixture includes both fuel and impurity ions; it is not the mean charge number of a selected species. The averaging uses uniform length weighting of the local effective charge. It is distinct from an unnormalized chord integral, an emissivity-weighted average, or a ratio formed from separately chord-integrated charge moments.

documentation after

Effective charge averaged along a specified viewing chord expresses the chord-mean composition factor for electron–ion Coulomb scattering. The local [effective charge](name:effective_charge) being averaged obeys $Z_{\mathrm{eff}} = \sum_a n_a Z_a^2/n_e$, where $Z_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the local mixture effective charge, $a$ labels an ion species and charge-state combination, $n_a$ is its number density, $Z_a$ is its ionic charge number, and $n_e$ is the free-electron number density. The sum includes all ion species and positive ionic charge states, not only impurities; neutral particles do not contribute. The chord-averaged quantity is distinct from a local value at a beam-crossing point, an unnormalized line integral, and an emissivity-weighted average. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length.

line_averaged_effective_hydrogenic_mass

description before

Line-averaged effective hydrogenic mass is the chord-length average of the local density-weighted mean atomic mass of bulk hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium.

description after

Line-averaged effective hydrogenic mass describes bulk hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium composition using the local isotope-density-weighted mean atomic mass along a specified chord.

documentation before

Line-averaged effective hydrogenic mass is the chord-length average of the local [effective hydrogenic mass](name:effective_hydrogenic_mass) of the bulk hydrogenic ion mixture. The local mean atomic mass is weighted by the isotope number densities. The line average is defined by: $$ \bar{m}_{\mathrm{eff}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L m_{\mathrm{eff}}\,dl $$ where $\bar{m}_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the line-averaged effective hydrogenic mass, $m_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the local effective hydrogenic mass, $L$ is the chord length, and $l$ is distance along the chord. The local mean satisfies $m_{\mathrm{eff}} = (\sum_s n_s m_s)/(\sum_s n_s)$, where $s$ labels hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium, $n_s$ is the local bulk-ion number density of isotope $s$ summed over its ionic charge states, and $m_s$ is that isotope's atomic mass. Electrons and non-hydrogenic species do not enter the isotope weighting. The chord average weights the local effective mass uniformly by length; it is not a ratio of chord-integrated mass density to chord-integrated number density. It differs from [volume-averaged effective hydrogenic mass](name:volume_averaged_effective_hydrogenic_mass) by averaging along a chord rather than over a plasma volume.

documentation after

Line-averaged effective hydrogenic mass describes the isotopic composition of the bulk hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ion mixture along a specified chord. It applies the line-average operator to the local [effective hydrogenic mass](name:effective_hydrogenic_mass), rather than describing the mass of one specified isotope. The local mixture mass entering the average is $$ m_{\mathrm{eff}}(l) = \frac{\sum_i n_i(l)m_i}{\sum_i n_i(l)} $$ where $m_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the local effective hydrogenic mass, $l$ is distance along the chord, $i$ labels hydrogen, deuterium, or tritium, $n_i$ is the local number density of bulk ions of isotope $i$ summed over ionic charge states, and $m_i$ is that isotope's atomic mass. Spatial averaging is applied after forming this local isotope-density-weighted mean; the quantity is not defined by substituting line-averaged isotope densities into the local mass formula. Nonhydrogenic species and fast-ion populations are excluded. Unlike [volume-averaged effective hydrogenic mass](name:volume_averaged_effective_hydrogenic_mass), this quantity averages along the specified chord rather than over the plasma volume. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length.

line_averaged_electron_density

description before

Number density of free electrons averaged along a specified plasma chord with equal path-length weighting, excluding electrons bound in atoms, molecules, or ions.

description after

Number density of free electrons averaged along a specified plasma chord, including thermal and nonthermal populations and excluding bound electrons.

documentation before

Number density of electron The line average is defined by $$\bar{f} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L f\,dl$$ where $\bar{n}_e$ is the line-averaged electron density, $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density evaluated along the chord, $L$ is the positive length of the specified chord, and $l$ is distance along it. The integration interval and length denominator refer to the same chord segment. The electron population includes thermal and nonthermal free electrons over the full velocity distribution; electrons bound in atoms, molecules, or ions are excluded. The average uses equal path-length weighting, without additional density or volume weighting. Unlike [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), this quantity averages along a chord rather than over the plasma volume. It equals the local density everywhere on the chord only when that density is uniform. The [line-integrated electron number density](name:line_integrated_electron_number_density) is instead a column abundance; dividing that abundance by its corresponding path length yields this mean when both quantities refer to the same path. The [line-averaged xenon density](name:line_averaged_xenon_density) applies the same chord-mean operation to xenon ions rather than electrons. The [ratio of line-averaged electron density to Greenwald density](name:ratio_of_line_averaged_electron_density_to_greenwald_density) uses this electron chord mean as its numerator and is a separate normalized quantity.

documentation after

Number density of electron. The electron-specific chord average is $$\bar{f} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L f\,dl$$ where $\bar{n}_e$ is the line-averaged electron number density, $n_e$ is the local [electron density](name:electron_density) evaluated along the chord, $L$ is the chord length, and $l$ is distance along the chord. The integration interval and chord length refer to the same complete path. For that same path, the [line-integrated electron number density](name:line_integrated_electron_number_density) is the corresponding column abundance before division by the chord length. The result characterizes the selected chord rather than a local point; a uniform local density equals its chord average. It differs from [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), which averages the free-electron population over the plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length.

line_averaged_electron_temperature

description before

Thermal energy of the bulk electron population expressed as an energy-equivalent temperature, averaged along a plasma chord.

description after

Temperature of bulk electrons averaged along a specified plasma chord, expressing their random translational energy rather than directed-flow energy.

documentation before

Line-averaged electron temperature is the energy-equivalent measure of the mean random kinetic energy of the bulk electron population, averaged along a plasma chord. The defining path-length average is: $$ T_{e,\mathrm{line}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L T_e(l)\,dl $$ where $T_{e,\mathrm{line}}$ is the line-averaged electron temperature, $T_e(l)$ is the local [electron temperature](name:electron_temperature) at path coordinate $l$, and $L$ is the total chord length. The coordinate $l$ measures distance along the specified plasma chord, and the averaging is uniform with respect to path length. This is an intensive quantity for the bulk electron population, aggregated along the chord rather than summed over electron energies or particle populations. It excludes ion temperatures and differs from the local electron temperature because it combines values along the entire chord. The line average is a chord-integrated representation of the parent [electron_temperature](name:electron_temperature), whereas [volume_averaged_electron_temperature](name:volume_averaged_electron_temperature) applies a volume average over the confined plasma and is therefore a distinct spatial aggregation.

documentation after

Temperature of electrons averaged along a specified plasma chord characterizes the random translational motion of the bulk electron population relative to its local mean flow. It excludes directed bulk-flow energy and is distinct from a radiation temperature. The local quantity being averaged is the [electron temperature](name:electron_temperature). The spatial scope is the specified plasma chord, with no additional electron-density or emissivity weighting. Unlike the [volume-averaged electron temperature](name:volume_averaged_electron_temperature), this quantity uses chord-length rather than plasma-volume weighting. The [line-averaged total ion temperature](name:line_averaged_total_ion_temperature) applies the same spatial averaging to the combined thermal ion population instead of electrons. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length.

line_averaged_helium_3_density

description before

Line-averaged helium-3 density is the chord-length average of local ionized helium-3 density along a specified viewing path, excluding neutral atoms.

description after

Number density of helium-3 ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, including all positive charge states and excluding neutral helium-3 atoms.

documentation before

Line-averaged helium-3 density is the chord-length average of the local helium-3 ion number density along a specified path. It includes singly ionized $^3\mathrm{He}^{+}$ and fully stripped $^3\mathrm{He}^{2+}$ ions, while excluding neutral helium-3 atoms. The line average is defined by $$ \overline{n}_{^3\mathrm{He}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_{0}^{L} n_{^3\mathrm{He}}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s $$ where $\overline{n}_{^3\mathrm{He}}$ is the line-averaged density, $n_{^3\mathrm{He}}(s)$ is the local charge-state-summed density at path coordinate $s$, and $L$ is the chord length. Unlike the volume average in [volume-averaged helium-3 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_3_density), this quantity weights each position uniformly by distance along the path. It is a path-integrated representation of [helium-3 density](name:helium_3_density), not a separate charge-state population.

documentation after

Number density of helium 3. The helium-3-specific chord average is $$ \bar{n}_{{}^3\mathrm{He}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L n_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}\,dl $$ where $\bar{n}_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}$ is the line-averaged helium-3 ion number density, $n_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}$ is the local [helium-3 density](name:helium_3_density) evaluated along the chord, $L$ is the stated chord length, and $l$ is distance along that chord. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded. This quantity describes the specified chord rather than a local value at a single point. It differs from [volume-averaged helium-3 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_3_density), which averages the same species population over the plasma volume. Its isotope-specific counterpart, [line-averaged helium-4 density](name:line_averaged_helium_4_density), applies the same chord-average operation to helium-4 rather than helium-3. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length.

line_averaged_helium_4_density

description before

Chord-averaged helium-4 ion number density along a specified line of sight through the plasma, representing the path mean of local fusion-ash concentration.

description after

Number density of helium-4 ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, including all positive charge states and excluding neutral helium-4 atoms.

documentation before

The line-averaged helium-4 ion number density is the path average of the local helium-4 ion density along a specified chord through the plasma. It represents an effective density for that chord, not the local value at a point or a volume-wide mean. The line average is defined by $$ \overline{n}_{{}^4\mathrm{He}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_{0}^{L} n_{{}^4\mathrm{He}}(l)\,dl $$ where $n_{{}^4\mathrm{He}}(l)$ is the local helium-4 ion number density from [helium_4_density](name:helium_4_density), $l$ is distance along the chord, and $L$ is the chord length; $l$ and $L$ are measured in m. The average includes the local helium-4 ion density along the specified path and uses uniform path-length weighting. Unlike [volume_averaged_helium_4_density](name:volume_averaged_helium_4_density), it is not averaged over the confined plasma volume.

documentation after

Number density of helium 4. Line averaging preserves the helium-4-specific charge-state relation: $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm{He4}} = \bar{n}_{\mathrm{He4}^{+}} + \bar{n}_{\mathrm{He4}^{2+}} $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{He4}}$ is the line-averaged helium-4 ion number density, $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{He4}^{+}}$ is the line-averaged singly ionized helium-4 number density, and $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{He4}^{2+}}$ is the line-averaged doubly ionized helium-4 number density. All three averages refer to the same chord. The underlying local quantity is [helium-4 density](name:helium_4_density); the chord average is neither a neutral-helium density nor a single-charge-state density. It does not specify the local density at an individual point. Unlike [volume-averaged helium-4 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_4_density), which averages over the confined plasma volume, this quantity characterizes the specified chord. It uses the same chord-averaging convention as [line-averaged hydrogen density](name:line_averaged_hydrogen_density). The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_averaged_hydrogen_density

description before

Line-averaged number density of hydrogen (protium) ions in the plasma, giving the path-length-normalized H+ concentration along a specified chord.

description after

Number density of hydrogen (protium) ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, including its sole positive charge state and excluding neutral atoms.

documentation before

The line-averaged hydrogen density quantifies the mean concentration of protium ions along a specified chord through the plasma. It represents the path-integrated hydrogen ion content normalized by the chord length. The line average is defined as $$ \overline{n}_H = \frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathcal{C}} n_H(\mathbf{r}(l))\,dl $$ where $\overline{n}_H$ is the line-averaged hydrogen density, $n_H(\mathbf{r}(l))$ is the local protium-ion number density at position $\mathbf{r}(l)$ along the chord, $l$ is the path-length coordinate, $\mathcal{C}$ is the specified chord, and $L$ is its total length. The quantity applies to charged protium ions, with any included protium ion charge states summed before the line average; neutral atomic hydrogen and heavier hydrogenic isotopes are excluded. It is a chord average rather than the local [hydrogen density](name:hydrogen_density) at a point or the volume average represented by [volume_averaged_hydrogen_density](name:volume_averaged_hydrogen_density). Unlike [line_averaged_total_ion_density](name:line_averaged_total_ion_density), it does not include other ion species or hydrogen isotopes. The path-length normalization preserves the same density meaning as the parent local quantity while replacing its pointwise value with an average along the chord.

documentation after

Number density of hydrogen. Protium has only one positive atomic charge state, giving the species-specific identity $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm{H}} = \bar{n}_{\mathrm{H}^{+}} $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{H}}$ is the line-averaged hydrogen ion number density and $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{H}^{+}}$ is the line-averaged singly ionized protium number density, both referring to the same chord. The species is protium alone, not the combined hydrogen-isotope population; [line-averaged deuterium density](name:line_averaged_deuterium_density) describes the corresponding quantity for a separate isotope. This quantity is a chord average rather than a local evaluation or an unnormalized line integral. It differs from [volume-averaged hydrogen density](name:volume_averaged_hydrogen_density), which averages the same local species density over the plasma volume instead of along the specified chord. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_averaged_iron_density

description before

Line-averaged iron density is the path-length mean of total iron impurity-ion number density, summed over Fe1+ through Fe26+.

description after

Number density of iron ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, including all positive charge states and excluding neutral iron atoms.

documentation before

Line-averaged iron density is the chord-length mean of the total iron ion number density along a specified line of sight. It includes all ionization states from Fe$^{1+}$ through Fe$^{26+}$ and does not resolve their individual contributions. The line average is defined as $$ n_{\mathrm{Fe,line}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L n_{\mathrm{Fe}}(l)\,dl $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Fe,line}}$ is the line-averaged density, $n_{\mathrm{Fe}}(l)$ is the local iron ion number density at path coordinate $l$, and $L$ is the total chord length. The local quantity being averaged is the charge-state-summed [iron density](name:iron_density). This chord average is distinct from [line_averaged_argon_density](name:line_averaged_argon_density), which applies the same path averaging to argon ions, and from a volume-averaged iron density, which averages over a plasma volume rather than a line of sight.

documentation after

Number density of iron. Line averaging preserves the iron-specific charge-state decomposition: $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm{Fe}} = \sum_{q=1}^{26} \bar{n}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{q+}} $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{Fe}}$ is the line-averaged iron-ion number density, $q$ is the positive ionic charge number, and $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{q+}}$ is the line-averaged number density of iron ions in charge state $q$. Every partial density is averaged over the same chord; 26 is the atomic number of iron. Unlike [volume-averaged iron density](name:volume_averaged_iron_density), this quantity averages along the specified chord rather than over the plasma volume. It is the iron counterpart of [line-averaged argon density](name:line_averaged_argon_density), with the same chord-average convention. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_averaged_lithium_density

description before

Path-length-averaged total number density of lithium ions along a specified plasma chord, summed over Li+ through fully stripped Li3+ and excluding neutral lithium.

description after

Number density of lithium ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, including all positive charge states and excluding neutral lithium atoms.

documentation before

This is the path-length average of lithium ion number density, summed over all ionization stages from Li$^+$ through fully stripped Li$^{3+}$, along a specified chord through the plasma. The chord average is defined by $$ \langle n_{Li} \rangle_L = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L n_{Li}(s)\,ds $$ where $n_{Li}(s)$ is the local lithium ion number density in m$^{-3}$, $s$ is path length in meters along the chord, and $L$ is the total chord length in meters. This quantity weights each path-length element equally and excludes neutral lithium. It differs from the flux-surface-averaged lithium density at the plasma boundary, which averages over a separatrix, and from [volume-averaged lithium density](name:volume_averaged_lithium_density), which averages over confined plasma volume.

documentation after

Number density of lithium. The lithium-specific chord average is $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm{Li}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L n_{\mathrm{Li}}\,dl $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{Li}}$ is the line-averaged lithium ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{Li}}$ is the local lithium ion number density evaluated along the chord, $L$ is the chord length, and $l$ is distance along the chord. The result represents the selected chord rather than a local value at a single point; a uniform local density equals its chord average. It differs from [volume-averaged lithium density](name:volume_averaged_lithium_density), which averages the same ion population over the plasma volume. For the same chord, lithium contributes one species component to the [line-averaged total ion density](name:line_averaged_total_ion_density). The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_averaged_time_derivative_of_electron_density

description before

Rate of change of free-electron number density averaged along a specified plasma chord, positive for density buildup and negative for depletion.

description after

Rate of change of free-electron number density averaged along a specified plasma chord, including thermal and nonthermal electrons and excluding bound electrons.

documentation before

Number density of electron The temporal rate is defined by $$\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}$$ where $\dot{\bar{n}}_e$ is the rate of change, $\bar{n}_e$ is the [line-averaged electron density](name:line_averaged_electron_density), and $t$ is time. The chord mean is $\bar{n}_e = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L n_e\,dl$, where $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density evaluated along the chord, $L$ is the chord length, and $l$ is distance along it. The population comprises free electrons and excludes electrons bound in atoms, ions, or molecules. This quantity is a net density-evolution rate, not an isolated electron source term: particle transport and electron production or loss can all contribute. Unlike the derivative of electron column density, it includes division by chord length. For a fixed chord and chord length, temporal differentiation commutes with chord averaging, so this quantity equals the chord average of the local [time derivative of electron density](name:time_derivative_of_electron_density). It differs from the [volume-averaged time derivative of electron density](name:volume_averaged_time_derivative_of_electron_density), which averages over a plasma volume rather than along a chord. Sign convention: Positive when the free-electron number density averaged along the specified chord increases with time.

documentation after

Number density of electron. The electron-specific defining expression is $$ \frac{\partial \bar{n}_e}{\partial t} $$ where $\bar{n}_e$ is the [line-averaged electron density](name:line_averaged_electron_density) and $t$ is time. For a fixed chord, line averaging and time differentiation commute, making this the chord-averaged specialization of the [electron-density time derivative](name:time_derivative_of_electron_density). It represents the selected chord rather than a local value or the [volume-averaged electron-density time derivative](name:volume_averaged_time_derivative_of_electron_density). It describes net density evolution, not an isolated electron production or loss term. Sign convention: Positive when the line-averaged free-electron number density increases with time. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The time derivative is the rate of change of a quantity with time at a fixed point or label.

line_averaged_toroidal_plasma_velocity

description before

Line-of-sight average of the plasma bulk velocity projected onto the toroidal direction, representing Doppler-sensitive motion around the machine axis.

description after

Signed toroidal plasma flow averaged along a viewing chord, describing directed motion about the symmetry axis rather than random thermal motion.

documentation before

This quantity is the effective toroidal bulk-flow velocity of plasma averaged along a diagnostic line of sight. It represents motion around the machine axis rather than a local velocity at one point or an accumulated line signal. For a line of sight with path length $L$, the line-averaged toroidal velocity is defined by $$ \overline{v}_{\phi} = \frac{1}{L}\int_{0}^{L} v_{\phi}(\ell)\,d\ell $$ where $\ell$ is distance along the line of sight, $L$ is its total path length, and $v_{\phi}(\ell)=\mathbf{v}(\ell)\cdot\hat{\phi}$ is the local toroidal component of the plasma bulk velocity. The normalization by $L$ distinguishes this velocity from a line-integrated quantity. The generic plasma designation does not select a particular ion species or charge state, unlike species-resolved quantities such as [toroidal deuterium velocity at the plasma boundary](name:toroidal_deuterium_velocity_at_plasma_boundary). Sign convention: Positive when the toroidal bulk velocity points in the direction of increasing $\phi$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame.

documentation after

This quantity characterizes directed plasma motion around the symmetry axis at the level of a specified viewing chord, rather than at a single plasma position. The local toroidal component entering the line average is $$ v_\phi = \mathbf{v}\cdot\hat{\phi} $$ where $v_\phi$ is the toroidal plasma velocity, $\mathbf{v}$ is the plasma bulk velocity, and $\hat{\phi}$ is the unit vector toward increasing toroidal angle. The right-handed cylindrical frame is $(R,\phi,Z)$, where $R$ is distance from the symmetry axis, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. The chord-level aggregation distinguishes this quantity from a local, species-resolved [toroidal ion velocity](name:toroidal_ion_velocity). It is not an unnormalized velocity path integral, a projection of the full velocity onto the viewing direction, or a thermal velocity spread. Sign convention: Positive when the chord-averaged toroidal plasma motion is toward increasing toroidal angle $\phi$. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length.

line_averaged_total_ion_density

description before

Total ion number density averaged along a specified plasma chord, summing all ion species and charge states, including thermal and non-thermal ions.

description after

Number density of ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, summed over all ion species and positive charge states, excluding electrons and neutral particles.

documentation before

The line-averaged total ion density is the chord average of the local [total ion density](name:total_ion_number_density), including all ion species, resolved charge states, and thermal and non-thermal ion populations. It excludes electron density. $$ n_{\mathrm{i,line}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L n_{\mathrm{i,total}}(l)\,dl $$ where $l$ is distance along the chord, $L$ is chord length, $n_{\mathrm{i,total}}(l)$ is local total ion density, and $n_{\mathrm{i,line}}$ is the resulting line average. Unlike the [volume-averaged total ion density](name:volume_averaged_total_ion_density), it averages along a chord rather than throughout a volume.

documentation after

Number density of ion. Line averaging preserves the species sum: $$ \bar{n}_{i,\mathrm{tot}} = \sum_s \bar{n}_s $$ where $\bar{n}_{i,\mathrm{tot}}$ is the line-averaged total ion number density, $s$ labels ion species, and $\bar{n}_s$ is the line-averaged number density of species $s$, summed over all positive charge states. Every species contribution uses the same chord. Thermal and nonthermal ions are included; electrons and neutral particles are excluded. Each ion is counted once regardless of its charge, so this is a particle number density rather than a charge-weighted density. The result characterizes the selected chord rather than a local point or a plasma-volume average. For the same chord, [line-averaged lithium density](name:line_averaged_lithium_density) supplies the lithium contribution to this total. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length.

line_averaged_total_ion_temperature

description before

Density-weighted effective temperature of the combined thermal ion population, with all species and charge states contributing by number density and averaged along a specified plasma chord.

description after

Temperature of thermal ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, with local number-density weighting over all ion species and charge states.

documentation before

The line-averaged total ion temperature is the line average of the local density-weighted effective temperature of the combined thermal ion population, including all included thermal ion species and charge states. It represents random thermal motion and excludes directed bulk-flow energy. The chord average is defined by $$ T_{i,\mathrm{line}} = \frac{\int_{\mathcal{C}} T_{i,\mathrm{tot}}(\ell)\,d\ell}{\int_{\mathcal{C}}d\ell} $$ where $T_{i,\mathrm{line}}$ is the line-averaged quantity, $T_{i,\mathrm{tot}}(\ell)$ is the local [total ion temperature](name:total_ion_temperature), $\ell$ is path length along the chord, and $\mathcal{C}$ is the specified chord. Species and charge states are combined by number density before the line average is taken. Unlike the [volume-averaged total ion temperature](name:volume_averaged_total_ion_temperature), this quantity weights positions by chord length rather than plasma volume.

documentation after

Temperature of ions averaged along a specified plasma chord characterizes the random translational motion of the combined thermal ion population relative to each population's local mean flow. It excludes directed bulk-flow energy and nonthermal ion energy. The local quantity being averaged is the [total ion temperature](name:total_ion_temperature), defined as the number-density-weighted mean over all thermal ion species and all their charge states, not a sum of their temperatures. The spatial operation is the [line average](concept:line_averaged) along the specified plasma chord, with no additional ion-density or emissivity weighting along that chord. Unlike the [volume-averaged total ion temperature](name:volume_averaged_total_ion_temperature), this quantity uses chord-length rather than plasma-volume weighting. The [line-averaged electron temperature](name:line_averaged_electron_temperature) applies the same spatial averaging to bulk electrons instead of the combined thermal ion population. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length.

line_averaged_tritium_density

description before

Chord-averaged number density of tritium ions, obtained by averaging local tritium ion density along a specified chord.

description after

Number density of tritium ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, including its sole positive charge state and excluding neutral tritium atoms.

documentation before

Chord-averaged tritium ion density is the path-length mean of the local tritium ion number density along a specified chord. It is derived from the local [tritium_density](name:tritium_density) and represents the chord-integrated fuel content normalized by chord length. $$ \overline{n}_T = \frac{1}{L} \int_0^L n_T(\ell)\,d\ell $$ where $\overline{n}_T$ is the chord-averaged density, $n_T(\ell)$ is the local tritium ion density at path coordinate $\ell$, and $L$ is the chord length. The average includes the specified chord segment and is distinct from the confined-volume mean [volume_averaged_tritium_density](name:volume_averaged_tritium_density) and from the corresponding helium-4 quantity [line_averaged_helium_4_density](name:line_averaged_helium_4_density).

documentation after

Number density of tritium. Line averaging preserves the tritium-specific charge-state identity: $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm{T}} = \bar{n}_{\mathrm{T}^{+}} $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{T}}$ is the line-averaged tritium ion number density and $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{T}^{+}}$ is the line-averaged number density of singly ionized tritium on the same chord. The underlying local quantity is [tritium density](name:tritium_density). The chord average is distinct from a local point value and from a plasma-volume average. For the same chord, adding the [line-averaged deuterium density](name:line_averaged_deuterium_density) gives the [line-averaged deuterium–tritium density](name:line_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density), which treats both fuel isotopes as one effective species. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_averaged_tungsten_density

description before

Line-of-sight average of the number density of positively charged tungsten ions, summed over all ionization states and excluding neutral tungsten.

description after

Number density of tungsten ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, summed over all charge states and excluding neutral tungsten atoms.

documentation before

This quantity is the line-of-sight average of the charge-state-summed number density of positively charged tungsten ions. $$ \bar n_{\mathrm{W}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathcal{L}} \left[\sum_{Z=1}^{74} n_{\mathrm{W}^{Z+}}(l)\right] \, dl $$ Here, $\bar n_{\mathrm{W}}$ is the line-averaged tungsten-ion number density, $\mathcal{L}$ is the specified averaging line, $L$ is its total length, $l$ is distance along that line, $Z$ is the positive tungsten charge-state index, and $n_{\mathrm{W}^{Z+}}(l)$ is the local number density of tungsten ions in charge state $Z$. The sum includes all positive tungsten ionization states and excludes neutral tungsten. The quantity applies to the aggregate tungsten-ion population along the specified line and is averaged over that line rather than evaluated locally or averaged over volume. It therefore differs from the local or otherwise spatially averaged [tungsten-ion density](name:tungsten_density) by its line-of-sight aggregation, and from [volume-averaged tungsten density](name:volume_averaged_tungsten_density) by using a line rather than the enclosed plasma volume.

documentation after

Number density of tungsten. For tungsten, the defining chord average is $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm{W}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L n_{\mathrm{W}}\,dl $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{W}}$ is the line-averaged tungsten ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{W}}$ is the local tungsten ion number density evaluated along the specified chord, $L$ is the chord length, and $l$ is distance along that chord. This quantity is not an unnormalized line integral or a local density at a particular point; it equals the local density throughout the chord when that density is uniform. Unlike [volume-averaged tungsten density](name:volume_averaged_tungsten_density), it averages over a chord rather than the plasma volume. It is the tungsten counterpart of [line-averaged xenon density](name:line_averaged_xenon_density), with the species changed but the averaging convention unchanged. The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_averaged_xenon_density

description before

Number density of xenon ions averaged along a chord through the plasma, summed over all ionization states from singly ionized through fully stripped xenon and excluding neutral xenon atoms.

description after

Number density of xenon ions averaged along a specified plasma chord, including all positive charge states and excluding neutral xenon atoms.

documentation before

The line-averaged xenon ion number density is the chord-length mean of the local [xenon density](name:xenon_density), which sums xenon ions from singly ionized through fully stripped xenon and excludes neutral xenon atoms. It represents the xenon ion content sampled by a specified chord, not the local value at any single point unless the local density is uniform along that chord. The chord average is defined by $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm{Xe},L} = \frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathcal{C}} n_\mathrm{Xe}(\ell)\, d\ell $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{Xe},L}$ is the line-averaged xenon ion number density, $\mathcal{C}$ is the chord segment over which the average is defined, $L = \int_{\mathcal{C}} d\ell$ is its path length, $\ell$ is distance along the chord, and $n_\mathrm{Xe}(\ell)$ is the local [xenon density](name:xenon_density) evaluated on the chord. The species aggregation is performed over charge states before the line average: $n_\mathrm{Xe}(\ell)=\sum_{z=1}^{54} n_{\mathrm{Xe}^{z+}}(\ell)$, where $z$ is the xenon ionic charge-state index, $n_{\mathrm{Xe}^{z+}}$ is the partial density of xenon ions carrying charge $ze$, and $e$ is the elementary charge. The physical population is charged xenon ions located on the chord segment through the plasma; neutral xenon atoms and charge-state-resolved xenon densities are excluded. This quantity differs from [volume-averaged xenon density](name:volume_averaged_xenon_density), which averages the same local xenon density over the plasma volume, and from [xenon density at magnetic axis](name:xenon_density_at_magnetic_axis), which evaluates it at the magnetic-axis locus. It is the xenon counterpart of [line-averaged argon density](name:line_averaged_argon_density) under the same chord-mean convention.

documentation after

Number density of xenon. The xenon-specific chord average is $$ \bar{n}_{\mathrm{Xe}} = \frac{1}{L}\int_0^L n_{\mathrm{Xe}}\,dl $$ where $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{Xe}}$ is the line-averaged xenon ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{Xe}}$ is the [local xenon ion number density](name:xenon_density) evaluated along the chord, $L$ is the chord length, and $l$ is distance along the chord. The result represents the selected chord rather than a local value at a single point; a uniform local density equals its chord average. It differs from [volume-averaged xenon density](name:volume_averaged_xenon_density), which averages the same ion population over the plasma volume. For the same chord, xenon contributes one species component to the [line-averaged total ion density](name:line_averaged_total_ion_density). The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

line_integrated_electron_number_density

description before

Line-integrated electron number density is the free-electron column abundance along the complete propagation path, including forward and return segments when present.

description after

Line-integrated electron number density is the free-electron column content along the complete propagation path, including forward and return segments when present.

documentation before

Line-integrated electron number density counts free electrons accumulated along the complete electromagnetic-wave propagation path. It is the complete-path specialization of [electron number density](name:electron_number_density). The defining line integral is $$\int_0^L f\,dl$$ where $N_e$ is the free-electron column abundance, $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density, $l$ is distance along the propagation path, and $L$ is the total path length. For reflected paths, forward and return segments both contribute, without a factor-of-two correction. Electrons bound in atoms, molecules, or ions are excluded. This quantity is neither a local volume density nor the [line-averaged electron density](name:line_averaged_electron_density), which divides the corresponding line integral by the length of its averaging path.

documentation after

Line-integrated electron number density represents the free-electron column content associated with the complete propagation path. It includes thermal and nonthermal free electrons and excludes electrons bound in atoms or ions. The electron column content is defined by $$\int_0^L f\,dl$$ where $N_e$ is the electron column content, $n_e$ is the local [electron density](name:electron_density), $l$ is distance along the propagation path, and $L$ is its total length. For reflected propagation, both forward and return segments contribute, without a factor-of-two correction. This is the column quantity described by [electron number density](name:electron_number_density), not a local volume density. It is not divided by path length: the corresponding [line-averaged electron density](name:line_averaged_electron_density) equals $N_e/L$ when evaluated over the same path. The line integral is the integral of a quantity along a stated path.

line_integrated_neutral_beam_radiance_at_spectral_line

description before

Photon radiance of one of nine motional-Stark components of fast neutral-beam emission, integrated along a sightline crossing the beam.

description after

Photon radiance of an individual motional-Stark component of fast neutral-beam emission at the specified spectral line, integrated along a sightline crossing the beam.

documentation before

Photon radiance at the spectral line quantifies emission from an individual Stark-split component of fast neutral-beam atoms along a viewing sightline. The splitting arises from the Lorentz electric field experienced by the moving atoms. In the optically thin limit, the defining line integral is $$\int_0^L f\,dl$$ where $L_i$ is the photon radiance of component $i$, $i$ identifies one of the nine Stark-split components, $j_i$ is the local directional photon emissivity integrated over that component's wavelength interval, $l$ is distance along the sightline crossing the neutral beam, and $l_{\max}$ is the total path length. Each value applies to one component, not the sum over the nine-component multiplet. It is integrated over the selected component's wavelength interval rather than resolved per wavelength, and it counts photons rather than their energy. Unlike local emissivity, it includes emission accumulated along the sightline. This quantity is the neutral-beam-specific case of [motional-Stark spectral-line radiance](name:motional_stark_radiance_of_spectral_line) and a component-resolved instance of [photon radiance at a spectral line](name:photon_radiance_at_spectral_line).

documentation after

The photon radiance at the specified spectral line resolves one of the nine motional-Stark components emitted by fast neutral-beam atoms. The splitting arises from the Lorentz electric field experienced by the moving atoms. For optically thin emission, the component radiance is $$\int_0^L f\,dl$$ where $L_k$ is the photon radiance of component $k$, $k$ labels one of the nine components, $j_k$ is its directional photon emissivity integrated over that component, $l$ is distance along the sightline, and $\ell_b$ is the total path length through the emitting beam. Each value includes the selected component's spectral extent but excludes the other eight components; it is not a radiance density per wavelength interval or a local emissivity. Summing the nine component radiances gives the motional-Stark contribution to the [neutral-beam radiance at the spectral line](name:neutral_beam_radiance_at_spectral_line). The line integral is the integral of a quantity along a stated path.

line_integrated_neutron_emissivity

description before

Neutron emissivity integrated along a detector line of sight gives the neutron brightness associated with that viewing path.

description after

Neutron emissivity accumulated along a detector line of sight defines the neutron-source brightness associated with that viewing path.

documentation before

neutron emissivity The defining line integral is $$\int_0^L f\,dl$$ where $\mathcal{B}_n$ is the neutron brightness, $\varepsilon_n$ is the local [neutron emissivity](name:neutron_emissivity), $l$ is distance along the line of sight, and $L$ is the total integration-path length. This quantity integrates neutron-number emission along the specified path, not over the source volume. It is distinct from the detector count rate, neutron energy flux, and the [total neutron emission rate](name:total_neutron_rate), which aggregates neutron production throughout the plasma.

documentation after

neutron emissivity. The quantity is the [line integral](concept:line_integrated) $$ \int_0^L \varepsilon_n\,dl $$ where $\varepsilon_n$ is the local [neutron emissivity](name:neutron_emissivity), evaluated along the viewing path, $l$ is distance along that path, and $L$ is its total integration length. The accumulation includes neutron production along the stated path without selecting a particular production mechanism. It is not an integral over the source volume and includes no detector-response or solid-angle weighting. It is therefore distinct from the [neutron flux incident on the detector](name:neutron_flux_of_neutron_detector) and the [accepted detection-event rate](name:rate_of_neutron_detector). The line integral is the integral of a quantity along a stated path.

linear_thermal_electron_decay_time_volume_averaged_due_to_disruption

description before

Linear 20–80% decay time of the volume-averaged thermal electron temperature during a disruption, based on its pre-disruptive value.

description after

Linear-extrapolated decay time of the volume-averaged thermal electron temperature during a disruption, defined from custom fractional thresholds or the fixed 20–80% interval.

documentation before

This quantity is the characteristic disruption decay time of the volume-averaged thermal electron temperature, determined by a linear 20–80% construction. It describes the reduction of the bulk electron thermal state over the plasma volume, rather than a local temperature or an exponential e-folding time. The defining relation is $$ \tau_{e,V}^{20\text{--}80} = \frac{t(0.2)-t(0.8)}{0.6} $$ where $\tau_{e,V}^{20\text{--}80}$ is the decay time, $t(f)$ is the time at which the volume-averaged temperature $T_{e,V}(t)$ reaches the fraction $f$ of its pre-disruptive value, and $f$ is a dimensionless temperature fraction. The reference $T_{e,V,0}$ is the pre-disruptive volume-averaged thermal electron temperature, so $t(0.8)$ marks the 80% level and $t(0.2)$ marks the 20% level. For a decreasing temperature, $t(0.2)>t(0.8)$. The quantity applies to the thermal electron population and uses a volume average over the plasma rather than a pointwise or magnetic-axis value. It is specific to the temperature loss occurring during a disruption and excludes decay times obtained from an exponential e-folding fit or from another electron-temperature reduction interval. The same linear 20–80% construction applied at the magnetic axis is [linear electron decay time at the magnetic axis due to disruption](name:linear_electron_decay_time_at_magnetic_axis_due_to_disruption); [electron decay time at the magnetic axis due to disruption](name:electron_decay_time_at_magnetic_axis_due_to_disruption) instead represents an exponential e-folding timescale for a local temperature decrease.

documentation after

This quantity is the linear-extrapolated decay time of the volume-averaged thermal electron temperature during a disruption. It characterizes the temperature decrease between two specified fractions of its pre-disruption value without requiring the entire decrease to follow a linear law. The linear decay time is defined by: $$ \tau_{\mathrm{L}}=\frac{t(X_2)-t(X_1)}{X_1-X_2} $$ where $\tau_{\mathrm{L}}$ is the linear decay time, $X_1$ and $X_2$ are the upper and lower fractional temperature thresholds with $0<X_2<X_1\leq1$, and $t(X)$ is the time when the volume-averaged thermal electron temperature reaches the fraction $X$ of its pre-disruption value during the specified decrease. The denominator extrapolates the temperature decline between the thresholds to a decrease equal to the full pre-disruption value; the result is not merely the elapsed time between threshold crossings. Custom linear definitions use their specified threshold fractions. The fixed 20–80% definition uses $X_1=0.8$ and $X_2=0.2$, giving $\tau_{\mathrm{L}}=[t(0.2)-t(0.8)]/0.6$. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. Here, the thermal [electron temperature](name:electron_temperature) is averaged over the stated plasma volume before applying the decay-time definition. This is not an average of local decay times or a density-weighted temperature average. The [on-axis linear electron decay time](name:linear_electron_decay_time_at_magnetic_axis_due_to_disruption) instead characterizes the local temperature decrease at the magnetic axis. The [volume-averaged exponential electron decay time](name:volume_averaged_electron_decay_time_due_to_disruption) is an e-folding time rather than a linear-threshold extrapolation, and the two conventions are not interchangeable. This temperature-decay quantity is distinct from the [plasma-current decay time](name:plasma_decay_time_due_to_disruption) and the [thermal-energy decay time](name:thermal_energy_decay_time_due_to_disruption).

neon_density

description before

Number density of neon impurity ions, summed over the included ionization states; line-averaged and volume-averaged forms express the same elemental content.

description after

Number density of neon ions across all positive charge states, excluding neutral neon, evaluated locally or with a specified spatial average.

documentation before

Neon density is the number of neon impurity ions per unit volume, summed over all included charged ionization states. It represents elemental neon content rather than the density of one selected charge state; neutral neon atoms are excluded. $$ n_{\mathrm{Ne}} = \sum_{z \in \mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{Ne}}} n_{\mathrm{Ne}^{z+}} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Ne}}$ is the charge-state-summed neon ion density, $n_{\mathrm{Ne}^{z+}}$ is the density of neon ions in charge state $z+$, and $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{Ne}}$ is the set of included charged ionization states. This species-specific quantity is a member of density. The same charge-state-summed field may be spatially averaged; [volume_averaged_neon_density](name:volume_averaged_neon_density) represents its volume average, while a charge-state-resolved quantity such as [ion_charge_state_density](name:ion_charge_state_density) selects one ionization state and is not equivalent.

documentation after

Number density of neon. The local number density is defined by $$ n_{\mathrm{Ne}} = \frac{dN_{\mathrm{Ne}}}{dV} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Ne}}$ is the number density of neon ions, $N_{\mathrm{Ne}}$ is the number of neon ions, and $V$ is physical volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded. The quantity may be evaluated locally or spatially averaged; it does not select a particular location or averaging domain. [Line-averaged neon density](name:line_averaged_neon_density) is the line-averaged form, while [volume-averaged neon density](name:volume_averaged_neon_density) is averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface. These are spatial averages of the density, not integrated particle inventories. In contrast, [ion charge state density](name:ion_charge_state_density) selects a single ionization state rather than the charge-state-summed density.

parallel_current_density_flux_surface_averaged_due_to_wave_driven_current_drive

description before

Flux-surface-averaged field-aligned conventional plasma current density generated non-inductively by electromagnetic-wave–particle interactions.

description after

Flux-surface-averaged field-aligned conventional plasma current density generated non-inductively by electromagnetic-wave–particle interactions.

documentation before

This quantity is the scalar field-aligned component of the conventional plasma current generated by electromagnetic-wave–particle interactions. It represents the wave-driven contribution to current flowing along the magnetic field on a magnetic flux surface.
