For a magnetic flux surface labeled by the poloidal flux coordinate $\psi$, the flux-surface-averaged parallel current density is defined by $$ j_{\parallel,\mathrm{FSA}}(\psi) = \frac{\left\langle \mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{wave}} \cdot \mathbf{B} \right\rangle_{\psi}}{B_0(\psi)} $$ where $j_{\parallel,\mathrm{FSA}}$ is this scalar current-density quantity, $\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{wave}}$ is the conventional current-density contribution generated by wave–particle interactions, $\mathbf{B}$ is the magnetic-field vector, $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\psi}$ denotes averaging over the flux surface labeled by $\psi$, and $B_0(\psi)$ is the reference magnetic-field magnitude used for the parallel-current normalization. The quantity applies to the wave-driven current contribution from the plasma populations participating in the electromagnetic-wave interaction and uses a flux-surface average rather than a local value. It excludes inductive current and current contributions from non-wave mechanisms, and it is distinct from the full vector flux-surface-averaged wave-driven current density. It is the magnetic-field-aligned scalar projection of that vector parent, retaining only the component parallel to the local magnetic field; [parallel current density due to ohmic current drive](name:parallel_current_density_due_to_ohmic_current_drive) instead represents the inductive resistive contribution. Sign convention: Positive when the wave-driven conventional current flows in the direction of the magnetic field, so that $\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{wave}} \cdot \mathbf{B} > 0$.

documentation after

This quantity is the scalar field-aligned component of the conventional plasma current generated by electromagnetic-wave–particle interactions. It represents the wave-driven contribution to current flowing along the magnetic field on a magnetic flux surface. For a magnetic flux surface labeled by the poloidal flux coordinate $\psi$, the flux-surface-averaged parallel current density is defined by $$ j_{\parallel,\mathrm{FSA}}(\psi) = \frac{\left\langle \mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{wave}} \cdot \mathbf{B} \right\rangle_{\psi}}{B_0(\psi)} $$ where $j_{\parallel,\mathrm{FSA}}$ is this scalar current-density quantity, $\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{wave}}$ is the conventional current-density contribution generated by wave–particle interactions, $\mathbf{B}$ is the magnetic-field vector, $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\psi}$ denotes averaging over the flux surface labeled by $\psi$, and $B_0(\psi)$ is the reference magnetic-field magnitude used for the parallel-current normalization. The quantity applies to the wave-driven current contribution from the plasma populations participating in the electromagnetic-wave interaction and uses a flux-surface average rather than a local value. It excludes inductive current and current contributions from non-wave mechanisms, and it is distinct from the full vector flux-surface-averaged wave-driven current density. It is the magnetic-field-aligned scalar projection of that vector parent, retaining only the component parallel to the local magnetic field; [parallel current density due to ohmic current drive](name:parallel_current_density_due_to_ohmic_current_drive) instead represents the inductive resistive contribution. Sign convention: Positive when the wave-driven conventional current flows in the direction of the magnetic field, so that $\mathbf{J}_{\mathrm{wave}} \cdot \mathbf{B} > 0$.

per_toroidal_mode_flux_surface_averaged_parallel_wave_electric_field_amplitude

description before

Peak strength of the magnetic-field-parallel coherent-wave electric field for each toroidal mode after power-deposition-weighted complex averaging over a closed magnetic flux surface.

description after

Peak strength of the magnetic-field-parallel coherent-wave electric field for one toroidal Fourier mode, obtained after power-deposition-weighted complex averaging over a closed magnetic flux surface.

documentation before

This quantity is the nonnegative amplitude of the component of a coherent-wave [electric-field](name:electric_field) phasor parallel to the equilibrium magnetic field. It represents the toroidal-mode-resolved field strength after power-deposition-density-weighted averaging over a closed magnetic flux surface. The real oscillation associated with the weighted modal phasor has the form $$f(t) = A\cos(\omega t + \varphi)$$ where $E_{\parallel,n}^{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the real parallel field associated with the weighted modal phasor, $t$ is time, $A_{\parallel,n}$ is its peak amplitude, $\omega$ is the wave angular frequency, $\varphi_{\parallel,n}$ is its temporal phase, and $n$ is the integer toroidal Fourier mode number. The amplitude is $A_{\parallel,n}=|\langle p_{\mathrm{dep}}\widetilde E_{\parallel,n}\rangle/\langle p_{\mathrm{dep}}\rangle|$, where $\widetilde E_{\parallel,n}$ is the local complex parallel modal field and $p_{\mathrm{dep}}$ is the coherent-wave power-deposition density used as the weight. The flux-surface average is $\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$, where $f$ is the quantity being averaged, $S$ is the closed magnetic flux surface, $dS$ is its surface-area element, $\psi$ is the poloidal magnetic flux labeling the surface, and $\nabla$ denotes the spatial gradient. The weighted amplitude is defined only when the surface-averaged weight is nonzero. The quantity resolves one toroidal mode; no summation over modes or division by the mode number is implied. It excludes perpendicular field components and complex phase information, and it is a peak amplitude rather than a root-mean-square field. The modulus is taken after complex averaging, so phase cancelation is retained; averaging pointwise amplitudes defines a different quantity. It is the toroidal-mode-resolved specialization of the [flux-surface-averaged parallel wave electric-field amplitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_parallel_wave_electric_field_amplitude). Like the [right-hand circularly polarized amplitude per toroidal mode](name:per_toroidal_mode_right_hand_circularly_polarized_wave_electric_field_amplitude), it uses power-deposition-weighted complex averaging, but it selects the magnetic-field-parallel component rather than a circularly polarized perpendicular component.

documentation after

This quantity is the nonnegative amplitude of the magnetic-field-parallel component of a coherent-wave electric-field phasor for one toroidal Fourier mode. It represents the surface-representative field strength after power-deposition-weighted complex averaging. Its defining relation is $$\langle f\rangle = \oint f\,dS/|\nabla\psi| \,/\, \oint dS/|\nabla\psi|$$ where $\mathcal{E}_{\parallel,n}^{\mathrm{amp}}$ is the amplitude defined here, $\widetilde{E}_{\parallel,n}$ is the local complex electric-field component parallel to the background magnetic field, $n$ is the integer toroidal Fourier mode number, and $p_{\mathrm{dep}}$ is the local wave-power-deposition density supplying the additional weighting. Angle brackets denote the flux-surface-average operator, and vertical bars denote the complex modulus. The normalization requires nonzero surface-averaged deposition. The quantity resolves one toroidal mode on each closed magnetic flux surface; no summation over modes or division by mode number is implied. Complex averaging precedes taking the modulus, so phase cancelation contributes to the result. This is neither an average of pointwise amplitudes nor a root-mean-square field. It excludes perpendicular field components and does not retain the phase of the averaged phasor. It is the toroidal-mode-resolved specialization of the [flux-surface-averaged parallel wave electric-field amplitude](name:flux_surface_averaged_parallel_wave_electric_field_amplitude); amplitudes of separate modes are not additive. The [right-hand circularly polarized amplitude per toroidal mode](name:per_toroidal_mode_right_hand_circularly_polarized_wave_electric_field_amplitude) applies the corresponding surface-representative construction to the E-minus polarization rather than the magnetic-field-parallel component. The amplitude is the peak magnitude of a sinusoidally varying quantity. The flux-surface average is the average of a quantity over a closed magnetic flux surface, weighted by the inverse magnitude of the poloidal flux gradient.

poloidal_electron_beta_flux_surface_averaged_at_pedestal_top

description before

Poloidal flux-surface-averaged electron beta at the pedestal top is the dimensionless ratio of flux-surface-averaged electron kinetic pressure to the magnetic pressure associated with the flux-surface-averaged poloidal magnetic field, evaluated at the fitted core-side shoulder of the H-mode edge transport barrier.

description after

Poloidal flux-surface-averaged electron beta at the pedestal top is the dimensionless ratio of flux-surface-averaged electron kinetic pressure to the magnetic pressure associated with the flux-surface-averaged poloidal magnetic field, evaluated at the fitted core-side shoulder of the H-mode edge transport barrier.

documentation before

Poloidal flux-surface-averaged electron beta at the pedestal top is the electron contribution to poloidal beta on the flux surface at the fitted core-side shoulder of the H-mode edge transport barrier. It compares the flux-surface-averaged electron kinetic pressure with the magnetic pressure formed from the flux-surface-averaged magnitude of the poloidal magnetic field on the same surface. The defining relation is: $$ \beta_{p,e}^{\mathrm{fs}}(\psi_{\mathrm{top}}) = \frac{2\mu_0\,\langle p_e\rangle_{\psi_{\mathrm{top}}}}{\left(\langle |B_p|\rangle_{\psi_{\mathrm{top}}}\right)^2} $$ where $\psi_{\mathrm{top}}$ is the poloidal magnetic flux coordinate labeling the pedestal-top flux surface, $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\psi_{\mathrm{top}}}$ denotes a flux-surface average on that surface using the normalized measure $dS/|\nabla\psi|$, $p_e$ is the electron kinetic pressure in pascals, $|B_p|$ is the magnitude of the poloidal magnetic field in tesla, and $\mu_0$ is the magnetic permeability of free space in henries per meter. The denominator uses the square of the averaged field magnitude, not the flux-surface average of $B_p^2$. The pressure term is the pedestal-top electron kinetic pressure represented by [electron_pressure_at_pedestal_top](name:electron_pressure_at_pedestal_top), with averaging over the same pedestal-top flux surface. Ion pressure and total thermal pressure contributions are excluded. The flux-surface-averaged denominator distinguishes this quantity from [poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top_low_field_side](name:poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top_low_field_side) and [poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top_high_field_side](name:poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top_high_field_side), which use local poloidal-field values at specified poloidal locations, and from [poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top](name:poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top_high_field_side), which does not specify flux-surface averaging of the poloidal field.

documentation after

Poloidal flux-surface-averaged electron beta at the pedestal top is the electron contribution to poloidal beta on the flux surface at the fitted core-side shoulder of the H-mode edge transport barrier. It compares the flux-surface-averaged electron kinetic pressure with the magnetic pressure formed from the flux-surface-averaged magnitude of the poloidal magnetic field on the same surface. The defining relation is: $$ \beta_{p,e}^{\mathrm{fs}}(\psi_{\mathrm{top}}) = \frac{2\mu_0\,\langle p_e\rangle_{\psi_{\mathrm{top}}}}{\left(\langle |B_p|\rangle_{\psi_{\mathrm{top}}}\right)^2} $$ where $\psi_{\mathrm{top}}$ is the poloidal magnetic flux coordinate labeling the pedestal-top flux surface, $\langle\cdot\rangle_{\psi_{\mathrm{top}}}$ denotes a flux-surface average on that surface using the normalized measure $dS/|\nabla\psi|$, $p_e$ is the electron kinetic pressure in pascals, $|B_p|$ is the magnitude of the poloidal magnetic field in tesla, and $\mu_0$ is the magnetic permeability of free space in henries per meter. The denominator uses the square of the averaged field magnitude, not the flux-surface average of $B_p^2$. The pressure term is the pedestal-top electron kinetic pressure represented by [electron_pressure_at_pedestal_top](name:electron_pressure_at_pedestal_top), with averaging over the same pedestal-top flux surface. Ion pressure and total thermal pressure contributions are excluded. The flux-surface-averaged denominator distinguishes this quantity from [poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top_low_field_side](name:poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top_low_field_side) and [poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top_high_field_side](name:poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top_high_field_side), which use local poloidal-field values at specified poloidal locations, and from [poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top](name:poloidal_electron_beta_at_pedestal_top_high_field_side), which does not specify flux-surface averaging of the poloidal field.

radial_width_time_derivative_of_neoclassical_tearing_mode

description before

Instantaneous growth or shrinkage rate of a single neoclassical tearing mode's full magnetic-island width along the major-radius direction.

description after

Radial width change rate of an individual neoclassical tearing mode expresses growth or shrinkage of its full magnetic-island span along major radius.

documentation before

Width of neoclassical tearing mode The defining time derivative follows the same individual mode: $$\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}$$ where $\dot{w}$ is the full-width change rate, $w$ is the [full radial island width](name:radial_width_of_neoclassical_tearing_mode), and $t$ is time. The rate applies to one mode and is not summed over modes. It differentiates the full width rather than the half-width and is distinct from motion of the island center. Negative values denote island shrinkage. Sign convention: Positive when the full island width increases with time.

documentation after

Width of neoclassical tearing mode. The defining expression is $$ \frac{\partial w}{\partial t} $$ where $w$ is the [radial full width of the individual mode](name:radial_width_of_neoclassical_tearing_mode), $t$ is time, and the mode identity is held fixed. The major-radius direction is the direction of increasing $R$ in the right-handed cylindrical $(R, \phi, Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical position. The differentiated quantity is the full island width, not its half-width, and the rate applies to one mode rather than a sum over modes. It describes island-size evolution, not translation of the island center or rotation of the island pattern. This width-evolution rate enters the generalized Rutherford equation. Sign convention: Positive when the full major-radius island width increases with time; negative when it decreases. The time derivative is the rate of change of a quantity with time at a fixed point or label.

ratio_of_electron_temperature_at_magnetic_axis_to_volume_averaged_electron_temperature

description before

Ratio of electron temperature at the magnetic axis to its volume average inside the last closed magnetic flux surface, expressing central electron-temperature peaking.

description after

Ratio of magnetic-axis electron temperature to its volume average within the last closed magnetic flux surface, expressing central electron-temperature peaking.

documentation before

Ratio of electron temperature at the magnetic axis to its volume average inside the last closed magnetic flux surface. It expresses central electron-temperature peaking relative to the enclosed plasma's mean thermal state. It is defined by $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $P_{T_e}$ is the electron-temperature peaking ratio, $T_{e,\mathrm{axis}}$ is the [electron temperature at the magnetic axis](name:electron_temperature_at_magnetic_axis), and $\langle T_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron temperature](name:volume_averaged_electron_temperature). The volume average is $\langle T_e\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V T_e\,dV$, where $T_e$ is the local electron kinetic temperature, $V$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface, and $dV$ is a physical volume element. The denominator is weighted by physical volume, not by electron density, and is not a flux-surface average. The numerator is evaluated at the magnetic axis rather than at an independently located temperature maximum. The ratio requires a positive denominator and is not an absolute temperature; unity means that the axis temperature equals the volume average, while values greater than unity indicate central peaking relative to that average. The [corresponding ion-temperature ratio](name:ratio_of_ion_temperature_at_magnetic_axis_to_volume_averaged_ion_temperature) applies the same axis-to-volume-average comparison to ion temperature. $$a/b$$

documentation after

Ratio of electron temperature at the magnetic axis to its volume average inside the last closed magnetic flux surface. It expresses central electron-temperature peaking relative to the enclosed plasma's mean thermal state. It is defined by $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $F_{T_e}$ is the electron-temperature peaking ratio, $T_e$ is the local electron kinetic temperature, $T_{e,\mathrm{axis}}$ is its [value at the magnetic axis](name:electron_temperature_at_magnetic_axis), and $\langle T_e\rangle_V$ is its [volume average](name:volume_averaged_electron_temperature). The averaging volume $V$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface. The denominator must be positive. The numerator is the magnetic-axis value, not a maximum selected elsewhere in the plasma. The ratio is not an absolute temperature: values above unity indicate an axis temperature exceeding the volume mean, while a spatially uniform temperature gives unity. The [ion-temperature counterpart](name:ratio_of_ion_temperature_at_magnetic_axis_to_volume_averaged_ion_temperature) applies the same axis-to-volume-average comparison to ions rather than electrons. $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_hydrogen_density_to_electron_density

description before

Ratio of hydrogen-ion number density to free-electron number density, expressing the abundance of ionized protium relative to electrons in the plasma.

description after

Ratio of hydrogen (protium) ion number density to free-electron number density, expressing the abundance of ionized protium relative to plasma electrons.

documentation before

Ratio of hydrogen-ion number density to free-electron number density at the same plasma location and time. It expresses particle abundance rather than mass-density or charge-density composition. The local ratio is defined by $$ R_{\mathrm{H}/e} = \frac{n_{\mathrm{H}}}{n_e} $$ where $R_{\mathrm{H}/e}$ is the hydrogen-to-electron abundance ratio, $n_{\mathrm{H}}$ is the local [hydrogen-ion number density](name:hydrogen_density), and $n_e$ is the local [free-electron number density](name:electron_density). The hydrogen population is protium summed over all ionic charge states, consisting only of H+; neutral hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium are excluded. The electron denominator has no imposed thermal or fast-population selection. The ratio is defined where $n_e$ is nonzero and is not the protium fraction of the total hydrogenic population. No spatial averaging or peak-density time selection is imposed on the underlying ratio. The [volume-averaged hydrogen-to-electron density ratio](name:volume_averaged_ratio_of_hydrogen_density_to_electron_density) averages this local ratio over volume and evaluates it when electron density peaks. That average is distinct from dividing separately volume-averaged hydrogen and electron densities. The [lithium-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_lithium_density_to_electron_density) is the corresponding species-abundance quantity with lithium ions in the numerator.

documentation after

Ratio of hydrogen (protium) ion number density to free-electron number density. It compares particle populations rather than electric charge or mass. The underlying local abundance ratio is $$ R_{\mathrm{H}/e} = \frac{n_{\mathrm{H}}}{n_e} $$ where $R_{\mathrm{H}/e}$ is the protium-to-electron abundance ratio, $n_{\mathrm{H}}$ is the [hydrogen-ion number density](name:hydrogen_density), and $n_e$ is the [free-electron number density](name:electron_density), with both densities evaluated at the same position and time. The numerator includes all positive charge states of protium, consisting only of singly charged protium ions. Neutral protium, deuterium, and tritium are excluded from the numerator. The denominator must be nonzero. This ratio is not the protium fraction among hydrogenic ions, because its reference population is free electrons rather than hydrogenic ions. The [volume-averaged member](name:volume_averaged_ratio_of_hydrogen_density_to_electron_density) averages this local ratio over the plasma volume at the time of peak electron density. Averaging the local ratio is not generally equivalent to dividing separately volume-averaged densities; that averaging operation and time selection belong to the child quantity, not the underlying ratio. The [lithium-to-electron abundance ratio](name:ratio_of_lithium_density_to_electron_density) is the analogous species ratio with lithium-ion density replacing protium-ion density. The ratio is one quantity divided by another.

ratio_of_ion_temperature_at_magnetic_axis_to_volume_averaged_ion_temperature

description before

Ratio of ion temperature at the magnetic axis to its volume average inside the last closed magnetic flux surface, expressing central ion-temperature peaking.

description after

Ratio of magnetic-axis ion temperature to its volume average within the last closed magnetic flux surface, expressing central ion-temperature peaking.

documentation before

Ratio of ion temperature at the magnetic axis to its volume average inside the last closed magnetic flux surface. It expresses central ion-temperature peaking relative to the enclosed plasma's mean thermal state. It is defined by $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $P_i$ is the ion-temperature peaking factor, $T_{i,0}$ is the magnetic-axis ion temperature, $T_i$ is the local ion temperature, and $\langle T_i\rangle_V$ is its average over the enclosed plasma volume $V$. The denominator is the [volume-averaged ion temperature](name:volume_averaged_ion_temperature): the integral of ion temperature over the enclosed plasma volume divided by that volume, without density weighting of the spatial average. Both temperatures must refer to the same ion population and describe random translational kinetic motion, excluding directed flow and internal excitation. The numerator is the magnetic-axis value, not a maximum selected at an arbitrary location. The ratio requires a positive denominator and is not an absolute temperature. A value above unity means that the magnetic-axis temperature exceeds the volume average. The [electron-temperature peaking ratio](name:ratio_of_electron_temperature_at_magnetic_axis_to_volume_averaged_electron_temperature) applies the same axis-to-volume-average comparison to electrons. $$a/b$$

documentation after

Ratio of ion temperature at the magnetic axis to its volume average inside the last closed magnetic flux surface. It expresses central ion-temperature peaking relative to the enclosed plasma's mean thermal state. It is defined by $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $F_{T_i}$ is the ion-temperature peaking ratio, $T_{i,\mathrm{axis}}$ is the ion kinetic temperature at the magnetic axis, and $\langle T_i\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged ion temperature](name:volume_averaged_ion_temperature). Here $T_i$ denotes the local ion kinetic temperature and $V$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface. Both operands refer to the same specified ion species. Its local temperature is the density-weighted mean over all charge states and describes the isotropic part of random translational motion, excluding directed bulk-flow energy. The spatial average uses volume weighting, not particle-density weighting. The denominator must be positive. The numerator is the magnetic-axis value, not a maximum selected elsewhere in the plasma. The ratio is not an absolute temperature: values above unity indicate an axis temperature exceeding the volume mean, while a spatially uniform temperature gives unity. The [electron-temperature counterpart](name:ratio_of_electron_temperature_at_magnetic_axis_to_volume_averaged_electron_temperature) applies the same axis-to-volume-average comparison to electrons rather than ions. $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_iron_density_to_electron_density

description before

Dimensionless abundance ratio of charge-state-summed iron-ion number density to local free-electron number density.

description after

Ratio of charge-state-summed iron-ion number density to free-electron number density, expressing the local abundance of ionized iron relative to plasma electrons.

documentation before

This quantity is the local iron-to-electron abundance ratio in a plasma, comparing the population of iron ions with unbound free electrons. $$ R_{\mathrm{Fe}/e} = \frac{n_{\mathrm{Fe}}}{n_e} $$ where $R_{\mathrm{Fe}/e}$ is the iron-to-electron abundance ratio, $n_{\mathrm{Fe}}$ is the total [iron-ion number density](name:iron_density), and $n_e$ is the local [free-electron number density](name:electron_density). The numerator is summed over all positive iron ionization states, while the denominator counts free electrons and excludes electrons bound in atoms, molecules, or ions. This is a local ratio with no volume averaging, maximum operation, or specified spatial locus. The ratio is the iron-specific counterpart of the general [ion-density-to-electron-density ratio](name:ratio_of_ion_density_to_electron_density); its numerator is the charge-state-summed iron density and its denominator is the electron-density parent quantity.

documentation after

Ratio of iron-ion number density to free-electron number density at the same plasma location and time. It compares particle populations rather than electric charge or mass. The local iron abundance is defined by $$ R_{\mathrm{Fe}/e} = \frac{n_{\mathrm{Fe}}}{n_e} = \frac{\sum_{z=1}^{26} n_{\mathrm{Fe}^{z+}}}{n_e} $$ where $R_{\mathrm{Fe}/e}$ is the iron-to-electron abundance ratio, $n_{\mathrm{Fe}}$ is the iron-ion number density summed over all positive charge states, $n_e$ is the [free-electron number density](name:electron_density), $n_{\mathrm{Fe}^{z+}}$ is the number density of iron ions with charge number $z$, and $z$ indexes the positive iron charge states from 1 through 26. Each iron ion contributes once to the numerator, regardless of charge state; neutral iron is excluded. Both densities are local quantities, with no spatial averaging implied. The ratio is defined only when the electron density is nonzero. Like the [lithium-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_lithium_density_to_electron_density), this quantity expresses unweighted ion-particle abundance relative to free electrons. It is neither a charge-weighted contribution to electron density nor an effective-charge measure. The ratio is one quantity divided by another.

ratio_of_line_averaged_electron_density_to_greenwald_density

description before

Dimensionless Greenwald fraction comparing the electron number density averaged along a plasma chord with the empirical density limit set by plasma current and effective minor radius.

description after

Ratio of line-averaged free-electron number density to the empirical Greenwald density limit, expressing proximity to a plasma-current-dependent density reference.

documentation before

The Greenwald fraction measures the proximity of a confined plasma's line-averaged electron density to its empirical Greenwald density limit. It is defined by: $$ f_G = \frac{\bar{n}_e}{n_G} $$ where $f_G$ is the Greenwald fraction, $\bar{n}_e$ is the [line-averaged electron density](name:line_averaged_electron_density), and $n_G$ is the [Greenwald density](name:greenwald_density) determined from the plasma-current magnitude and effective minor radius. The numerator represents the electron population averaged along a plasma chord; the denominator is the empirical density reference for the confined plasma. This quantity is a global normalized ratio, not a local electron density, a density limit itself, or an average over charge states or spatial regions. The fraction is the direct normalization of line-averaged electron density by Greenwald density: values near unity indicate proximity to the empirical reference limit, while values above or below unity indicate a line-averaged density respectively above or below that reference.

documentation after

Ratio of line-averaged free-electron number density to the empirical Greenwald density reference. It expresses the line-averaged density relative to this plasma-current-dependent reference. The ratio is defined as $$ f_{\mathrm{G}} = \frac{\bar{n}_e}{n_{\mathrm{G}}} $$ where $f_{\mathrm{G}}$ is this ratio, $\bar{n}_e$ is the [line-averaged electron density](name:line_averaged_electron_density), and $n_{\mathrm{G}}$ is the [Greenwald density](name:greenwald_density). The numerator is formed by the [line average](concept:line_averaged) along a stated chord; it is not a local or [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density). The denominator is the Greenwald density reference, not another chord-averaged density. This is the ratio of the line-averaged density to the reference, not the line average of a local density-to-reference ratio. This is the explicitly line-averaged form of the [electron-density-to-Greenwald-density ratio](name:ratio_of_electron_density_to_greenwald_density). A value of one means the line-averaged density equals the Greenwald reference; values above one are possible because the empirical reference is not a strict mathematical bound. $$a/b$$ The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The ratio is one quantity divided by another.

ratio_of_line_averaged_hydrogen_density_to_line_averaged_total_hydrogenic_density

description before

Dimensionless line-averaged protium fraction among hydrogenic ions, obtained by dividing the protium density by the total hydrogenic density along the same chord.

description after

Ratio of line-averaged protium-ion density to line-averaged total hydrogenic-ion density, giving the protium fraction among hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ions.

documentation before

This quantity is the fraction of hydrogenic ion content carried by protium along a specified chord through the plasma. It is a ratio of consistently line-averaged densities, rather than a local fraction or a volume-averaged fraction. $$ f_{\rm H}^{\rm line} = \frac{\bar{n}_{\rm H}^{\rm line}}{\bar{n}_{\rm H}^{\rm line}+\bar{n}_{\rm D}^{\rm line}+\bar{n}_{\rm T}^{\rm line}} $$ where $f_{\rm H}^{\rm line}$ is the line-averaged protium fraction, $\bar{n}_{\rm H}^{\rm line}$ is the line-averaged protium-ion number density, $\bar{n}_{\rm D}^{\rm line}$ is the corresponding deuterium-ion number density, and $\bar{n}_{\rm T}^{\rm line}$ is the corresponding tritium-ion number density. The symbols H, D, and T denote protium, deuterium, and tritium, respectively. The quantity applies to the hydrogenic ion population along one common chord and aggregates the isotope densities through the total hydrogenic denominator. Neutral atomic hydrogen and non-hydrogenic species are excluded. The numerator is the [line-averaged hydrogen density](name:line_averaged_hydrogen_density), while the denominator is the [total hydrogenic density](name:total_hydrogenic_density) under the same line-averaging convention. This ratio differs from the [ratio of volume-averaged hydrogen density to volume-averaged total hydrogenic density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_hydrogen_density_to_volume_averaged_total_hydrogenic_density), because the latter uses volume-averaged rather than chord-averaged isotope densities.

documentation after

Ratio of line-averaged hydrogen (protium) ion number density to line-averaged total hydrogenic-ion number density. It expresses the protium particle fraction among hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ions along a specified plasma chord. The isotope fraction is defined by $$ f_{\mathrm{H}} = \frac{\bar{n}_{\mathrm{H}}}{\bar{n}_{\mathrm{hyd}}} = \frac{\bar{n}_{\mathrm{H}}}{\bar{n}_{\mathrm{H}}+\bar{n}_{\mathrm{D}}+\bar{n}_{\mathrm{T}}} $$ where $f_{\mathrm{H}}$ is the protium fraction, $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{H}}$ is the [line-averaged hydrogen density](name:line_averaged_hydrogen_density), $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{D}}$ and $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{T}}$ are the line-averaged deuterium-ion and tritium-ion number densities, respectively, and $\bar{n}_{\mathrm{hyd}}$ is their total hydrogenic-ion number density averaged along the same chord. All averages refer to the same chord and time. Each isotope density is summed over all positive ionic charge states; for these isotopes, the sole positive atomic charge state is singly charged. Neutral particles, electrons, and nonhydrogenic species are excluded. The denominator counts ions without mass or charge weighting. The aggregation is the ratio of line-averaged densities, not the line average of the local isotope fraction. The fraction is defined only for a nonzero denominator and lies between zero and one for nonnegative isotope densities. This quantity is the chord-based counterpart of the [volume-averaged hydrogen isotope fraction](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_hydrogen_density_to_volume_averaged_total_hydrogenic_density), which instead forms the ratio from densities averaged over a common plasma volume. $$a/b$$ The line average is the integral of a quantity along a stated chord divided by the chord length. The ratio is one quantity divided by another.

ratio_of_lithium_density_to_electron_density

description before

Ratio of charge-state-summed lithium-ion number density to free-electron number density, expressing the abundance of ionized lithium relative to electrons.

description after

Ratio of an ion species’ charge-state-summed number density to free-electron number density, expressing particle abundance rather than charge contribution.

documentation before

Ratio of lithium-ion number density to free-electron number density, expressing lithium abundance relative to electrons in the plasma. It compares particle populations rather than electric charge or mass. The local abundance ratio is defined by $$ R_{\mathrm{Li}/e} = \frac{n_{\mathrm{Li}}}{n_e} $$ where $R_{\mathrm{Li}/e}$ is the lithium-to-electron abundance ratio, $n_{\mathrm{Li}}$ is the lithium-ion number density summed over all positive lithium charge states, and $n_e$ is the [free-electron number density](name:electron_density). Both densities refer to the same location and instant, and the ratio is defined only where the electron density is nonzero. The lithium population includes singly, doubly, and triply ionized lithium and excludes neutral lithium. Each lithium ion contributes equally to the numerator regardless of charge state; this is not a charge-weighted contribution to electron density or an effective-charge measure. Volume averaging this local ratio gives the [volume-averaged lithium-to-electron density ratio](name:volume_averaged_ratio_of_lithium_density_to_electron_density), whose stated evaluation time is the electron-density peak. Averaging the local ratio is distinct from dividing two separately volume-averaged densities. The [hydrogen-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_hydrogen_density_to_electron_density) uses the same particle-abundance definition with hydrogen replacing lithium.

documentation after

Ratio of a specified ion species’ number density to free-electron number density. It compares ion and electron particle populations without weighting ions by their electric charge. The underlying local abundance ratio is $$ R_{i/e} = \frac{n_i}{n_e} $$ where $R_{i/e}$ is the ion-species abundance relative to free electrons, $n_i$ is the specified species’ ion number density summed over all positive charge states, and $n_e$ is the [free-electron number density](name:electron_density). The two densities refer to the same plasma location and time, and the local ratio is defined only where the electron density is nonzero. Neutral atoms are excluded from the ion population, and each ion counts once regardless of charge state. This abundance is neither a charge-weighted contribution to electron density nor an effective-charge measure. Species-specific members include the [iron-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_iron_density_to_electron_density). The [volume-averaged lithium-to-electron density ratio](name:volume_averaged_ratio_of_lithium_density_to_electron_density) averages the corresponding local lithium ratio over the plasma volume at the time of peak electron density. That aggregation is an average of local ratios, not a ratio of separately volume-averaged densities; neither its species selection nor its averaging and timing restrictions apply to the general quantity. The ratio is one quantity divided by another.

ratio_of_volume_averaged_argon_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density

description before

Elemental abundance ratio of argon nuclei to free electrons inside the last closed flux surface, formed from the corresponding volume-averaged densities. The argon population is summed over all argon ion charge states and excludes neutral argon.

description after

Ratio of volume-averaged argon-ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density, expressing charge-state-summed argon abundance in the confined plasma.

documentation before

Elemental abundance ratio of argon nuclei to free electrons inside the last closed flux surface is the confined-plasma composition fraction obtained by normalizing the volume-averaged argon density to the volume-averaged electron density. The argon population is the species-level ion population summed over all argon ion charge states. The ratio is defined as the quotient of the corresponding volume-averaged densities: $$ C_{\mathrm{Ar}} = \frac{\langle n_{\mathrm{Ar}}\rangle_V}{\langle n_e\rangle_V} $$ where $C_{\mathrm{Ar}}$ is this argon-to-electron abundance ratio, $\langle n_{\mathrm{Ar}}\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged argon-ion number density](name:volume_averaged_argon_density), $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron number density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), and $V$ denotes the confined plasma volume bounded by the last closed flux surface. The scope is the confined plasma population inside the last closed flux surface, with the same volume-averaging operation applied to the argon and electron densities before forming the quotient. It excludes neutral argon, is not charge-state-resolved, and is a ratio of volume averages rather than the volume average of a local argon-to-electron density ratio. This quantity is related to [argon_density](name:argon_density) as its volume-averaged, electron-normalized elemental abundance; [argon_density](name:argon_density) denotes the argon density before normalization to the electron population.

documentation after

Ratio of volume-averaged argon-ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density. It compares particle populations rather than electric charge or mass. The argon abundance ratio is defined by $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $a_{\mathrm{Ar},V}$ is this argon abundance ratio, $\langle n_{\mathrm{Ar}}\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged argon density](name:volume_averaged_argon_density), $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the volume-averaged free-electron number density, and $V$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface. The underlying quantities $n_{\mathrm{Ar}}$ and $n_e$ are the local [argon-ion number density](name:argon_density) and free-electron number density, respectively. Both averages use the same confined plasma volume and evaluation time; the supplied composition context specifies the time of peak electron density. The argon population is summed over all positive charge states, from singly ionized to fully stripped argon; neutral argon is excluded. The aggregation is the ratio of two volume averages, not the volume average of the local argon-to-electron density ratio. It is defined only when the volume-averaged electron density is nonzero. It is an unweighted argon-particle abundance, not a charge-weighted contribution to electron density or an effective-charge measure. It is the argon member of the same composition-ratio family as the [corresponding neon abundance ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_neon_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density), with the numerator population changed from neon ions to argon ions. $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_volume_averaged_beryllium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density

description before

Dimensionless beryllium abundance formed by dividing the volume-averaged beryllium density by the volume-averaged electron density.

description after

Ratio of volume-averaged beryllium-ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density, expressing confined-plasma beryllium abundance summed over positive charge states.

documentation before

Dimensionless beryllium abundance formed by dividing the volume-averaged beryllium density by the volume-averaged electron density. It represents the abundance of ionized beryllium relative to free electrons in the confined plasma volume. The abundance is defined by the ratio of the two volume averages: $$ a_{\mathrm{Be}} = \frac{\langle n_{\mathrm{Be}} \rangle_V}{\langle n_e \rangle_V} = \frac{\left\langle \sum_{q=1}^{4} n_{\mathrm{Be}^{q+}} \right\rangle_V}{\langle n_e \rangle_V} $$ where $a_{\mathrm{Be}}$ is the beryllium abundance ratio, $\langle \cdot \rangle_V$ denotes averaging over the confined plasma volume, $n_{\mathrm{Be}}$ is the charge-state-summed beryllium ion number density, $n_{\mathrm{Be}^{q+}}$ is the number density of beryllium ions in charge state $q$, and $n_e$ is the [electron density](name:electron_density). The numerator is the [volume-averaged beryllium density](name:volume_averaged_beryllium_density), summed over all ionized beryllium charge states and excluding neutral beryllium atoms. The denominator is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density) over the same confined plasma volume. This quantity is a ratio of two global volume averages, not a pointwise impurity fraction and not the volume average of $n_{\mathrm{Be}}/n_e$. It is not charge-weighted; a charge-weighted beryllium contribution to quasineutrality would use terms proportional to $q n_{\mathrm{Be}^{q+}}$ rather than the unweighted ion sum. The local pre-averaged counterpart of the numerator is [beryllium density](name:beryllium_density).

documentation after

Ratio of volume-averaged beryllium-ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density. It compares particle populations rather than electric charge or mass. The beryllium abundance ratio is $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $R_{\mathrm{Be}/e,V}$ denotes this abundance ratio, $\langle n_{\mathrm{Be}}\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged beryllium density](name:volume_averaged_beryllium_density), $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the volume-averaged free-electron number density, and $V$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface. The local densities $n_{\mathrm{Be}}$ and $n_e$ denote beryllium-ion and free-electron number density, respectively. Both averages use the same plasma volume and are evaluated at the time of peak electron density. The [local beryllium density](name:beryllium_density) includes all positive charge states: $n_{\mathrm{Be}}=\sum_{z=1}^{4}n_{\mathrm{Be}^{z+}}$, where $z$ is the positive charge number and $n_{\mathrm{Be}^{z+}}$ is the number density of beryllium ions in that charge state. Neutral beryllium is excluded. The aggregation is the ratio of two volume averages, not the volume average of the local ratio $n_{\mathrm{Be}}/n_e$. The quantity is defined only when the volume-averaged electron density is nonzero. Like the [corresponding tungsten abundance ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_tungsten_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density), this quantity counts each included ion once; it is not a charge-weighted contribution to electron density or an effective-charge measure. $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_volume_averaged_deuterium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density

description before

Dimensionless volume-averaged deuterium abundance ratio is the quotient of the volume-averaged deuterium ion number density and the volume-averaged electron number density over the same plasma volume. The deuterium numerator is charge-state-summed over positive atomic ion charge states and excludes neutral or molecular deuterium.

description after

Ratio of volume-averaged deuterium-ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density in the confined plasma, evaluated at the time of peak electron density.

documentation before

Dimensionless volume-averaged deuterium abundance ratio is the electron-density-normalized abundance of deuterium ions in a specified plasma volume. It is the quotient of the [volume-averaged deuterium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_density) and the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density) evaluated over that same volume. The ratio is defined by the quotient of volume averages: $$ f_{D,V} = \frac{\langle n_D \rangle_V}{\langle n_e \rangle_V} $$ where $f_{D,V}$ is this abundance ratio, $n_D$ is the [deuterium density](name:deuterium_density), $n_e$ is the electron density, $\langle n_D \rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged deuterium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_density), $\langle n_e \rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), and the subscript $V$ denotes averaging over the common plasma volume. The numerator is the volume average of deuterium ion number density summed over all positive atomic ion charge states. It excludes neutral deuterium atoms, molecular deuterium, and deuterium bound in non-ion populations. The aggregation convention is a quotient of two volume-averaged densities, not the volume average of the pointwise ratio $n_D/n_e$. For non-negative number densities and positive $\langle n_e \rangle_V$, $f_{D,V}$ is non-negative. It is not the fraction of deuterium among all fuel nuclei and does not represent the absolute deuterium particle inventory. It differs from the [volume-averaged deuterium-tritium abundance ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density), which uses the combined deuterium-tritium numerator, while this quantity uses deuterium alone.

documentation after

Ratio of volume-averaged deuterium-ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density. It expresses confined-plasma deuterium abundance relative to free electrons at the time of peak electron density. The deuterium abundance ratio is $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $a_{\mathrm D,V}$ denotes this ratio, $\langle n_{\mathrm D}\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged deuterium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_density), $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the volume-averaged free-electron number density, and $V$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface. The local quantities $n_{\mathrm D}$ and $n_e$ denote the [deuterium-ion number density](name:deuterium_density) and free-electron number density, respectively. Both averages use the same confined plasma volume and evaluation time. The deuterium numerator is summed over all positive atomic charge states; for deuterium, this consists solely of singly ionized deuterons. Neutral deuterium and molecular species are excluded. The aggregation is the ratio of two volume averages, not the volume average of the local density ratio, and the denominator must be nonzero. Normalizing the volume-averaged deuterium density by the electron density gives abundance relative to free electrons, not the deuterium fraction among hydrogen-isotope ions or an effective-charge measure. Unlike the [combined deuterium–tritium abundance ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density), its numerator includes deuterium ions only, not tritium ions. $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density

description before

Volume-averaged deuterium-tritium density divided by volume-averaged electron density for the combined deuterium-tritium fuel pseudo-species, treating deuterium and tritium as one effective species.

description after

Ratio of volume-averaged effective deuterium–tritium fuel-ion number density, summed over ionic charge states, to volume-averaged free-electron number density.

documentation before

Volume-averaged deuterium-tritium density divided by volume-averaged electron density for the combined deuterium-tritium fuel pseudo-species, treating deuterium and tritium as one effective species, is the electron-density-normalized abundance of that fuel species. It compares the fuel-ion population with the electron population after each density is averaged over the same plasma volume. The ratio is defined by: $$ \mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{DT}/e} = \frac{\langle n_{\mathrm{DT}} \rangle_V}{\langle n_e \rangle_V} $$ where $\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{DT}/e}$ is this ratio, $\langle n_{\mathrm{DT}} \rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged deuterium-tritium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density), $\langle n_e \rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), $n_{\mathrm{DT}}$ is the local number density of the combined deuterium-tritium effective species, $n_e$ is the local electron number density, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_V$ denotes averaging over the common plasma volume $V$. The scope is the plasma volume common to numerator and denominator; the value is a ratio of two volume averages, not the volume average of a pointwise density ratio. The numerator is a species-level extensive density for the effective deuterium-tritium fuel species and is summed over all charge states before averaging. It includes only that fuel species and excludes helium ash, impurity ions, and separate isotope-composition ratios such as the [tritium-to-deuterium density ratio](name:ratio_of_tritium_density_to_deuterium_density). Equivalently, this quantity is the [volume-averaged deuterium-tritium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density) normalized by the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density); it is a normalized ratio, not a derivative of either density. When the combined deuterium-tritium density is resolved into deuterium and tritium contributions over the same volume and denominator, it equals the sum of the [volume-averaged deuterium-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_deuterium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density) and the [volume-averaged tritium-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density).

documentation after

Ratio of volume-averaged effective deuterium-tritium fuel-ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density. It expresses the abundance of the combined effective fuel-ion population relative to free electrons, comparing particle populations rather than electric charge or mass. For the common plasma volume $V$, the ratio is $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{DT}/e}$ is this abundance ratio, $\langle n_{\mathrm{DT}}\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged deuterium-tritium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density), $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), $n_{\mathrm{DT}}$ is the local number density of the effective deuterium-tritium fuel-ion species, and $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density. Both averages apply to the same confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface. The numerator population is summed over all ionic charge states, and the ratio is defined only when the volume-averaged free-electron density is nonzero. The aggregation is the ratio of the two volume averages, not the volume average of the local density ratio. This is an unweighted particle-abundance ratio, not a charge-weighted contribution to electron density, a mass fraction, or a fusion reaction rate. It compares the combined effective fuel-ion population with free electrons, rather than comparing deuterium and tritium with each other. When the effective deuterium-tritium population is resolved into deuterium and tritium contributions over the same volume and normalized by the same electron average, this ratio equals the sum of the [volume-averaged deuterium-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_deuterium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density) and the [volume-averaged tritium-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density). $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_volume_averaged_helium_3_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density

description before

Dimensionless composition ratio comparing the charge-state-summed helium-3 ion density averaged over the confined plasma volume with the electron density averaged over the same volume. It is the helium-3 average divided by the electron average.

description after

Ratio of volume-averaged helium-3 ion density to volume-averaged free-electron density in the confined plasma, counting helium-3 ions across all positive charge states.

documentation before

Dimensionless composition ratio comparing the charge-state-summed helium-3 ion density averaged over the confined plasma volume with the electron density averaged over the same volume. It is the helium-3 average divided by the electron average and represents the global helium-3 ion composition relative to electrons. The ratio is defined by applying the volume average separately to each density over the same confined plasma volume before division: $$ r_{{}^3\mathrm{He}/e} = \frac{\frac{1}{V_c}\int_{V_c} n_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}(\mathbf{x})\,dV}{\frac{1}{V_c}\int_{V_c} n_e(\mathbf{x})\,dV} $$ where $r_{{}^3\mathrm{He}/e}$ is this composition ratio, $n_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}(\mathbf{x})$ is the local helium-3 ion number density summed over all charged ionization states, $n_e(\mathbf{x})$ is the local electron number density, $\mathbf{x}$ denotes position, $dV$ is the volume element, and $V_c$ is the confined plasma volume, with the same symbol denoting its volume measure in the normalization factors. The scope is a species-level, volume-averaged, confined-plasma composition ratio. The helium-3 population includes charged helium-3 ions summed over charge states and excludes neutral helium-3 atoms; the electron population is the corresponding electron population in the same confined volume. No particular time within an evolution is part of the quantity definition. This quantity is not the confined-volume average of the local ratio $n_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}/n_e$; it equals that average only for special spatial distributions. Its numerator is the [volume-averaged helium-3 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_3_density), and its denominator is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density). The [ratio of volume-averaged helium-4 density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_helium_4_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density) is the analogous isotope composition ratio for helium-4.

documentation after

Ratio of volume-averaged helium-3 ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density. It expresses the particle abundance of the helium isotope with three nucleons relative to free electrons in the confined plasma. The helium-3 abundance ratio is $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $a_{3,V}$ denotes this abundance ratio, $\langle n_3\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged helium-3 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_3_density), $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), and $V$ is the common plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface. The brackets denote volume averaging; $n_3$ and $n_e$ denote the corresponding local helium-3 ion and free-electron number densities. Both averages are evaluated at the time of peak electron density. The helium-3 population is summed over all positive charge states, including singly and doubly ionized helium-3; neutral helium-3 and other helium isotopes are excluded. Neither density is restricted to thermal particles. The aggregation is the ratio of two volume averages, not the volume average of the local ratio $n_3/n_e$. The quantity is defined only when the volume-averaged electron density is nonzero. Helium-3 ions are counted without charge weighting, so this is neither their charge-weighted contribution to electron density nor an effective-charge measure. The [corresponding tritium abundance ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density) uses the same averaging-before-division convention for the tritium-ion population. $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_volume_averaged_helium_4_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density

description before

Helium-4 abundance ratio formed from mean densities inside the last closed flux surface, comparing the charge-state-summed helium-4 ion density with the electron density after each density is volume averaged.

description after

Ratio of volume-averaged helium-4 ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density, expressing confined-plasma helium-4 abundance summed over positive charge states.

documentation before

Helium-4 abundance ratio formed from mean densities inside the last closed flux surface is the quotient of the volume-averaged helium-4 ion number density and the volume-averaged electron number density. It is a global isotope-abundance measure for ionized helium-4 relative to electrons in the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface. The ratio is defined from independent volume averages over the same enclosed plasma volume: $$ f_{{}^4\mathrm{He},e}^{V} = \frac{\langle n_{{}^4\mathrm{He}}\rangle_V}{\langle n_e\rangle_V}, \qquad \langle n_s\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V_\mathrm{LCFS}}\int_{V_\mathrm{LCFS}} n_s(\mathbf{x})\,dV $$ where $f_{{}^4\mathrm{He},e}^{V}$ is this helium-4 abundance ratio, the superscript $V$ denotes formation from volume averages, $\langle n_{{}^4\mathrm{He}}\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged helium-4 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_4_density), $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), $n_s(\mathbf{x})$ is the local number density of species $s$ at position $\mathbf{x}$, $V_\mathrm{LCFS}$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is an element of that volume. For $s={}^4\mathrm{He}$, $n_s$ is the local helium-4 ion number density summed over all ion charge states; for $s=e$, $n_s$ is the local electron number density. The semantic scope is species-level helium-4 ion density inside the last closed flux surface. The helium-4 numerator is summed over all ion charge states before averaging, while neutral helium-4 atoms and helium isotopes other than helium-4 are excluded. Because the numerator and denominator are averaged separately, this quantity is not the volume average of the local ratio $n_{{}^4\mathrm{He}}/n_e$. For fully stripped helium-4 ions in a quasi-neutral plasma, $2 f_{{}^4\mathrm{He},e}^{V}$ represents the helium-4 ion contribution to the electron charge balance, apart from contributions of other ions and departures from full stripping. The isotope analog is [ratio of volume-averaged helium-3 density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_helium_3_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density), and the analogous elemental abundance quantity is [ratio of volume-averaged neon density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_neon_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density). It is related to ratio of volume-averaged helium-4 ion density to electron density by the shared helium-4 numerator species, while this name explicitly normalizes by the independently volume-averaged electron density.

documentation after

Ratio of volume-averaged helium-4 ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density. It compares helium-4 nuclei in positive ionization states with free electrons inside the last closed flux surface. The helium-4 abundance ratio is $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $a_{4,V}$ is this abundance ratio, $n_4$ is the local helium-4 ion number density, $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density, and angle brackets with subscript $V$ denote volume averaging over the confined plasma volume $V$. The numerator is the [volume-averaged helium-4 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_4_density); the denominator is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), which provides the electron-population normalization. Both averages use the same plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface and are evaluated at the time of peak electron density. Helium-4 denotes the isotope with two protons and two neutrons. Its density is summed over all positive charge states, including singly and doubly ionized helium-4 but excluding neutral helium-4 and other helium isotopes. Neither population is restricted to thermal particles. The aggregation is the ratio of the two volume averages, not the volume average of the local helium-4-to-electron density ratio. The denominator must be nonzero. Each helium-4 ion contributes one particle regardless of charge state, so this abundance is neither a charge-weighted contribution to electron density nor an effective-charge measure. The [volume-averaged neon-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_neon_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density) uses the same ratio-of-averages construction for the neon population. $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_volume_averaged_hydrogen_density_to_volume_averaged_total_hydrogenic_density

description before

Dimensionless hydrogen-isotope fraction equal to the volume-averaged protium-ion density divided by the volume-averaged density of all hydrogenic ions.

description after

Ratio of volume-averaged protium-ion density to volume-averaged total hydrogenic-ion density, giving the protium fraction among hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ions.

documentation before

This quantity is the fraction of the hydrogenic ion population attributable to protium, evaluated from volume-averaged densities over the same plasma volume. The numerator is the [volume-averaged hydrogen density](name:volume_averaged_hydrogen_density), while the denominator includes the corresponding hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ion populations. $$ f_H = \frac{\overline{n}_H}{\overline{n}_H + \overline{n}_D + \overline{n}_T} $$ Here $f_H$ is the hydrogen-isotope fraction; $\overline{n}_H$, $\overline{n}_D$, and $\overline{n}_T$ are the volume-averaged densities of protium, deuterium, and tritium ions, respectively. The quantity is a ratio of separately volume-averaged densities, not a local isotope fraction or a fraction restricted to a single charge state.

documentation after

Ratio of volume-averaged hydrogen (protium) ion number density to volume-averaged total hydrogenic-ion number density. It expresses the protium particle fraction among hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ions in the confined plasma. The protium fraction is $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $f_{\mathrm{H},V}$ is this protium fraction; $V$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface; $n_{\mathrm{H}}$, $n_{\mathrm{D}}$, and $n_{\mathrm{T}}$ are the local protium-, deuterium-, and tritium-ion number densities; and $n_{\mathrm{hyd}}$ is their sum, the [total hydrogenic density](name:total_hydrogenic_density). Angle brackets with subscript $V$ denote volume averages. The numerator is the [volume-averaged hydrogen density](name:volume_averaged_hydrogen_density). Both averages refer to the same confined plasma volume and time. Each isotope density is summed over all positive ionic charge states; neutral particles are excluded. The denominator includes only hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ions, not electrons or non-hydrogenic ions. This is a particle-number fraction, not a mass fraction. It lies between zero and one when the denominator is positive and is undefined when the denominator vanishes. The aggregation is a ratio of volume averages, not the volume average of the local fraction $n_{\mathrm{H}}/n_{\mathrm{hyd}}$. Because both averages use the same volume, it also equals the fraction of the confined hydrogenic-ion inventory carried by protium. Unlike the [line-averaged protium fraction](name:ratio_of_line_averaged_hydrogen_density_to_line_averaged_total_hydrogenic_density), it describes composition over the confined plasma volume rather than along a specified chord. $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_volume_averaged_neon_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density

description before

Dimensionless elemental abundance ratio of charge-state-summed neon nuclei to free electrons, formed from their respective mean number densities inside the last closed flux surface.

description after

Ratio of volume-averaged neon-ion number density, summed over positive charge states, to volume-averaged free-electron number density in the confined plasma.

documentation before

This quantity is the abundance of neon nuclei relative to free electrons, with both species represented by number densities averaged over the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface. Neon is summed over all charge states, so the quantity is an elemental composition ratio rather than a charge-state-specific abundance or a local density ratio. The ratio of volume-averaged densities is defined by $$ \frac{\overline{n}_{\mathrm{Ne}}}{\overline{n}_e} = \frac{\displaystyle \frac{1}{V_p}\int_{V_p} n_{\mathrm{Ne}}(\mathbf{x})\,dV}{\displaystyle \frac{1}{V_p}\int_{V_p} n_e(\mathbf{x})\,dV} $$ where $\overline{n}_{\mathrm{Ne}}$ is the [volume-averaged neon density](name:volume_averaged_neon_density), $\overline{n}_e$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), $n_{\mathrm{Ne}}(\mathbf{x})$ is the local neon nuclear number density summed over charge states, $n_e(\mathbf{x})$ is the local free-electron number density, $V_p$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, $\mathbf{x}$ is position, and $dV$ is the volume element. Both operands use the same plasma volume and the same volume-averaging convention. This quantity is the ratio of the two global means, not the volume average of the local ratio $n_{\mathrm{Ne}}(\mathbf{x})/n_e(\mathbf{x})$; its denominator is specifically the parent [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), while its numerator is the corresponding [volume-averaged neon density](name:volume_averaged_neon_density).

documentation after

Ratio of volume-averaged neon-ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density. It expresses elemental neon abundance relative to free electrons in the confined plasma. The neon abundance ratio is $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $a_{\mathrm{Ne},V}$ denotes this abundance ratio, $n_{\mathrm{Ne}}$ and $n_e$ are the local neon-ion and free-electron number densities, respectively, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_V$ denotes volume averaging over the plasma volume $V$ enclosed by the last closed flux surface. The numerator is the [volume-averaged neon density](name:volume_averaged_neon_density), summed over all positive neon charge states; neutral neon is excluded. Each neon ion counts once, irrespective of its charge state. Both operands use the same volume and averaging operation, and the ratio is defined only when the electron-density average is nonzero. This quantity is a ratio of volume averages, not the volume average of the local ratio $n_{\mathrm{Ne}}/n_e$. It is not a charge-weighted neon contribution to electron density or an effective-charge measure. The [corresponding nitrogen abundance ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_nitrogen_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density) uses the same definition with nitrogen replacing neon. $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_volume_averaged_oxygen_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density

description before

Volume-averaged oxygen ion density relative to volume-averaged electron density for the plasma, with oxygen summed over ionized charge states and neutral oxygen excluded.

description after

Ratio of plasma-volume-averaged oxygen-ion number density, summed over positive charge states, to plasma-volume-averaged free-electron number density. It expresses oxygen-ion abundance relative to free electrons in the plasma volume.

documentation before

Volume-averaged oxygen ion density relative to volume-averaged electron density is a scalar oxygen impurity fraction for the plasma. It compares the charge-state-summed volume average of ionized oxygen number density with the corresponding volume average of electron number density over the same averaging volume. The ratio is defined by $$ f_O = \frac{\left\langle \sum_{q=1}^{8} n_{O^{q+}} \right\rangle_V}{\langle n_e \rangle_V} $$ where $f_O$ is this oxygen-to-electron density ratio, $\langle\cdot\rangle_V$ denotes a volume average over the averaging volume $V$, $q$ is the oxygen charge-state index, $n_{O^{q+}}$ is the local number density of oxygen ions in charge state $q+$, and $n_e$ is the local electron number density. The denominator $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density). The numerator is summed over ionized oxygen charge states $O^{1+}$ through $O^{8+}$ and excludes neutral oxygen. This quantity is a single scalar composition fraction formed from volume-averaged populations; it is distinct from a local [oxygen density](name:oxygen_density), from a [line-averaged oxygen density](name:line_averaged_oxygen_density), and from a neutral-inclusive [volume-averaged oxygen density](name:volume_averaged_oxygen_density) when that quantity includes neutral oxygen. The essential relationship is normalization: the charge-state-summed volume-averaged oxygen ion density is divided by the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), so the result represents oxygen ion abundance relative to the electron population rather than an absolute density.

documentation after

Ratio of the plasma-volume-averaged oxygen-ion number density to the plasma-volume-averaged free-electron number density. It compares particle populations, so each oxygen ion counts once regardless of its charge state. It is not a charge-weighted or mass-weighted measure. The ratio is formed from the two volume averages over the same plasma volume: $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $a_{\mathrm{O}}$ is this oxygen-to-electron abundance ratio, $\langle n_{\mathrm{O}}\rangle_V$ is the volume average of the oxygen-ion number density summed over all positive charge states of oxygen, and $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density). The averaging volume $V$ is the plasma volume, and both averages use the same $V$. The oxygen population is elemental oxygen in positive ionization states, summed over all charge states. Neutral oxygen is excluded. The [volume-averaged oxygen density](name:volume_averaged_oxygen_density) is the corresponding absolute oxygen density, and this ratio is that density normalized by the volume-averaged electron density. The quantity is the ratio of the two volume averages, not the volume average of the local ratio of oxygen density to electron density. It is distinct from a local [oxygen density](name:oxygen_density) and from any line-averaged density. It is defined only when $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is nonzero. This is the oxygen member of the family of volume-averaged abundance ratios that includes the [tungsten ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_tungsten_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density). $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_volume_averaged_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density

description before

Ratio of volume-averaged tritium density to volume-averaged electron density is the volume-averaged tritium number density, summed over all charge states, divided by the volume-averaged electron number density.

description after

Ratio of plasma-volume-averaged tritium-ion number density to plasma-volume-averaged free-electron number density, comparing the tritium-ion and free-electron particle populations in the same plasma volume.

documentation before

Ratio of volume-averaged tritium density to volume-averaged electron density is the volume-averaged tritium number density divided by the volume-averaged electron number density. It expresses the tritium contribution to plasma composition relative to the electron population after both densities have been averaged over the same plasma volume. The ratio is defined as: $$ f_{T/e}^{V} = \frac{\langle n_T \rangle_V}{\langle n_e \rangle_V} $$ where $f_{T/e}^{V}$ is this ratio, $\langle n_T \rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged tritium density](name:volume_averaged_tritium_density), $\langle n_e \rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), $n_T$ is the local [tritium density](name:tritium_density), $n_e$ is the local [electron density](name:electron_density), and $\langle q\rangle_V$ denotes $V^{-1}\int_V q\,dV$ for scalar field $q$ over the common plasma volume $V$. The scope is the specified plasma volume and the particle populations contained in that volume. The tritium density is a species-level per-volume amount and is therefore summed over all tritium charge states before volume averaging. The definition contains no selection by the time of peak electron density or by any other operating condition. This quantity is the ratio of volume averages, not the volume average of the local ratio $n_T/n_e$, and it is not normalized to the total hydrogenic-fuel density. It is the tritium-only counterpart of the [ratio of volume-averaged deuterium-tritium density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density), which uses the combined deuterium-plus-tritium numerator; it also differs from the local ratio of tritium density to electron density.

documentation after

Ratio of the plasma-volume-averaged tritium-ion number density to the plasma-volume-averaged free-electron number density. It compares particle populations rather than electric charge or mass. The ratio is formed from the two volume averages over a common plasma volume: $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $a_{\mathrm{T}/e,V}$ denotes this ratio, $\langle n_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged tritium density](name:volume_averaged_tritium_density), and $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density). Each average has the form $\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$, with $f$ replaced by the local tritium-ion number density $n_{\mathrm{T}}$ (the local [tritium density](name:tritium_density)) or by the local free-electron number density $n_e$ (the local [electron density](name:electron_density)). $V$ is the plasma volume over which both averages are taken, and it is the same for numerator and denominator. The tritium population is ionized tritium summed over all charge states; neutral tritium is excluded. The aggregation is the ratio of the two volume averages, not the volume average of the local ratio $n_{\mathrm{T}}/n_e$. The ratio is defined only when $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is nonzero. This is an unweighted tritium-ion abundance relative to free electrons. It is not the tritium fraction among fuel ions, not a charge-weighted contribution to electron density, and not an effective-charge measure. Its local counterpart is [ratio of tritium density to electron density](name:ratio_of_tritium_density_to_electron_density), which is not volume averaged. $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

ratio_of_volume_averaged_tungsten_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density

description before

Dimensionless tungsten abundance ratio equal to the plasma-volume-averaged elemental tungsten density divided by the plasma-volume-averaged electron density.

description after

Ratio of volume-averaged tungsten-ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density, expressing confined-plasma tungsten abundance summed over all positive charge states.

documentation before

Dimensionless tungsten abundance ratio equal to the plasma-volume-averaged elemental tungsten density divided by the plasma-volume-averaged electron density. It compares the charge-state-summed tungsten-ion population with the free-electron population in the same plasma volume.
