The ratio is defined from the two volume averages over the plasma volume $V$ as $$ a_{\mathrm{W},V} = \frac{\langle n_{\mathrm{W}}\rangle_V}{\langle n_e\rangle_V} = \frac{V^{-1}\int_V \sum_{z=1}^{74} n_{\mathrm{W}^{z+}}(\mathbf{x})\, dV}{V^{-1}\int_V n_e(\mathbf{x})\, dV} $$ where $a_{\mathrm{W},V}$ denotes this tungsten abundance ratio, $V$ denotes the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface over which both averages are formed, $\langle n_{\mathrm{W}}\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged tungsten density](name:volume_averaged_tungsten_density), $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), $n_{\mathrm{W}^{z+}}(\mathbf{x})$ is the local number density of tungsten ions in charge state $z$ at position $\mathbf{x}$, $n_e(\mathbf{x})$ is the local free-electron number density, $z=1,\ldots,74$ indexes the positive tungsten ionization states, and $dV$ is the volume element. The scope is the confined plasma volume: both numerator and denominator use the same volume and the same volume-averaging operation. The tungsten population is elemental tungsten in positive ionization states, summed over charge state; neutral tungsten is excluded. The aggregation is the ratio of the two volume averages, not the volume average of the local ratio $n_{\mathrm{W}}/n_e$, and the quantity is defined only when $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is nonzero. This ratio is an unweighted tungsten-nucleus abundance relative to free electrons; it is not a charge-weighted tungsten contribution to electron density and is not an effective-charge measure. It is the tungsten member of the same volume-averaged composition-ratio family as the [deuterium-tritium abundance ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density) and the [carbon abundance ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_carbon_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density), which replace the tungsten numerator with the corresponding species volume average while retaining the same electron-density denominator.

documentation after

Ratio of volume-averaged tungsten-ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density. It compares particle populations rather than electric charge or mass. The tungsten abundance ratio is defined by $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{W}/e}$ is the tungsten abundance ratio, $n_{\mathrm{W}}$ is the local tungsten-ion number density, $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density, and $V$ is the common averaging volume. The numerator $\langle n_{\mathrm{W}}\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged tungsten density](name:volume_averaged_tungsten_density), and the denominator $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density). Both averages apply to the plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface and are evaluated at the time of peak electron density. Tungsten density is summed over all positive charge states, counting each ion once and excluding neutral tungsten. The electron density includes thermal and nonthermal free electrons. This quantity is a ratio of volume averages, not a volume average of the local density ratio. Because the averaging volume is common to both operands, it also equals the total number of tungsten ions divided by the total number of free electrons within that volume. The electron-density denominator must be nonzero. The [analogous tritium abundance ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density) replaces the tungsten-ion numerator with the volume-averaged tritium-ion density. $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

rotation_frequency_time_derivative_of_neoclassical_tearing_mode

description before

Instantaneous angular acceleration of a neoclassical tearing-mode magnetic island, indicating whether its helical rotation is accelerating or decelerating.

description after

Instantaneous angular acceleration of a neoclassical tearing-mode magnetic island, indicating whether its helical rotation is accelerating or decelerating.

documentation before

This quantity is the instantaneous angular acceleration of a single neoclassical tearing-mode magnetic island on its resonant flux surface. The defining relation is $$ \alpha_{\mathrm{NTM}}(t) = \frac{d\omega_{\mathrm{NTM}}(t)}{dt} $$ where $\alpha_{\mathrm{NTM}}$ is the island's angular acceleration, $\omega_{\mathrm{NTM}}$ is its [angular rotation frequency](name:rotation_frequency_of_neoclassical_tearing_mode), $t$ is time, and the subscript $\mathrm{NTM}$ identifies the neoclassical tearing mode. The quantity applies to one identified magnetic island at a specified instant; it is not a time average, a sum over multiple modes, or an aggregate over multiple islands. It is distinct from the island's angular rotation frequency itself and from its [helical phase](name:neoclassical_tearing_mode_phase), whose second time derivative it represents. It also differs from the [cyclic frequency](name:frequency_of_neoclassical_tearing_mode), which describes cycles per unit time rather than angular rotation. The orientation is defined in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. Sign convention: Positive when the signed angular rotation frequency increases with time, with positive rotation defined by helical-phase advance in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame.

documentation after

This quantity is the instantaneous angular acceleration of a single neoclassical tearing-mode magnetic island on its resonant flux surface. The defining relation is $$ \alpha_{\mathrm{NTM}}(t) = \frac{d\omega_{\mathrm{NTM}}(t)}{dt} $$ where $\alpha_{\mathrm{NTM}}$ is the island's angular acceleration, $\omega_{\mathrm{NTM}}$ is its [angular rotation frequency](name:rotation_frequency_of_neoclassical_tearing_mode), $t$ is time, and the subscript $\mathrm{NTM}$ identifies the neoclassical tearing mode. The quantity applies to one identified magnetic island at a specified instant; it is not a time average, a sum over multiple modes, or an aggregate over multiple islands. It is distinct from the island's angular rotation frequency itself and from its [helical phase](name:neoclassical_tearing_mode_phase), whose second time derivative it represents. It also differs from the [cyclic frequency](name:frequency_of_neoclassical_tearing_mode), which describes cycles per unit time rather than angular rotation. The orientation is defined in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame, where $R$ is major radius, $\phi$ is toroidal angle, and $Z$ is vertical height. Sign convention: Positive when the signed angular rotation frequency increases with time, with positive rotation defined by helical-phase advance in the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\phi,Z)$ frame.

runaway_electron_critical_momentum_volume_averaged_due_to_avalanche

description before

Volume-averaged momentum threshold for runaway-electron avalanche multiplication, separating collisional drag-dominated electrons from those capable of net acceleration and secondary runaway production.

description after

Volume-averaged momentum threshold for runaway-electron avalanche multiplication, separating collisional drag-dominated electrons from those capable of net acceleration and secondary runaway production.

documentation before

This quantity is the plasma-volume average of the local critical momentum threshold for avalanche-driven runaway-electron generation. It characterizes the boundary between collisional drag-dominated and net-accelerated electron dynamics. The volume average is defined by $$ p_{c,\mathrm{av}} = \frac{1}{V}\int_V p_c(\mathbf{r})\,dV $$ where $p_{c,\mathrm{av}}$ is the averaged threshold, $V$ is the plasma volume, $dV$ is a differential volume element, and $p_c(\mathbf{r})$ is the local critical momentum due to avalanche at position $\mathbf{r}$. This quantity is an unweighted volume average, not the momentum carried by the runaway-electron population and not a volume average of electron momentum. It is the spatially aggregated form of the local avalanche threshold and is distinct from the broader momentum due to avalanche, which need not denote a critical threshold.

documentation after

This quantity is the plasma-volume average of the local critical momentum threshold for avalanche-driven runaway-electron generation. It characterizes the boundary between collisional drag-dominated and net-accelerated electron dynamics. The volume average is defined by $$ p_{c,\mathrm{av}} = \frac{1}{V}\int_V p_c(\mathbf{r})\,dV $$ where $p_{c,\mathrm{av}}$ is the averaged threshold, $V$ is the plasma volume, $dV$ is a differential volume element, and $p_c(\mathbf{r})$ is the local critical momentum due to avalanche at position $\mathbf{r}$. This quantity is an unweighted volume average, not the momentum carried by the runaway-electron population and not a volume average of electron momentum. It is the spatially aggregated form of the local avalanche threshold and is distinct from the broader momentum due to avalanche, which need not denote a critical threshold.

time_derivative_of_derivative_of_volume_of_flux_surface_with_respect_to_toroidal_flux_coordinate

description before

Temporal change of the geometric volume derivative with respect to the dimensionful toroidal flux coordinate for each nested magnetic flux surface.

description after

Temporal change of the geometric volume derivative with respect to the dimensionful toroidal flux coordinate for each nested magnetic flux surface.

documentation before

This quantity is the temporal rate of change of the volume change associated with an increment of dimensionful toroidal flux coordinate on a nested magnetic flux surface. It is defined by $$ \left.\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\left(\frac{\partial V}{\partial \rho_{\mathrm{tor}}}\right)\right|_{\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}} $$ where $V$ is the cumulative volume enclosed by the flux surface, $\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$ is the dimensionful toroidal flux coordinate labeling that surface, and $t$ is time. The outer derivative is taken at fixed $\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$, following the corresponding moving flux surface as the equilibrium evolves. The quantity applies to an individual nested magnetic flux surface and has no species, population, or source-process weighting. It is a temporal derivative of a geometric metric coefficient, not the enclosed volume itself, a volume integral, a flux-surface average, or an aggregation over multiple surfaces. It is the time-transformed counterpart of the [volume derivative with respect to toroidal flux coordinate](name:flux_surface_averaged_inverse_of_major_radius): the linked quantity is the inner derivative $\partial V/\partial \rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$, while this quantity applies the outer partial-time-derivative operator to it.

documentation after

This quantity is the temporal rate of change of the volume change associated with an increment of dimensionful toroidal flux coordinate on a nested magnetic flux surface. It is defined by $$ \left.\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\left(\frac{\partial V}{\partial \rho_{\mathrm{tor}}}\right)\right|_{\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}} $$ where $V$ is the cumulative volume enclosed by the flux surface, $\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$ is the dimensionful toroidal flux coordinate labeling that surface, and $t$ is time. The outer derivative is taken at fixed $\rho_{\mathrm{tor}}$, following the corresponding moving flux surface as the equilibrium evolves. The quantity applies to an individual nested magnetic flux surface and has no species, population, or source-process weighting. It is a temporal derivative of a geometric metric coefficient, not the enclosed volume itself, a volume integral, a flux-surface average, or an aggregation over multiple surfaces. time_derivative_of_diamagnetic_energy

description before

Time derivative of the global diamagnetic stored energy, representing the rate of change of diamagnetic plasma energy content inferred from diamagnetic measurements.

description after

Diamagnetic energy accumulation rate for the whole plasma, based on total perpendicular pressure including thermal and fast-particle contributions.

documentation before

documentation after

The diamagnetic energy accumulation rate describes changes in the global plasma stored-energy measure associated with pressure perpendicular to the local magnetic field. Applying the [time derivative](concept:time_derivative) to [diamagnetic energy](name:diamagnetic_energy) gives: $$ \frac{\partial W_{\mathrm{dia}}}{\partial t} $$ where $W_{\mathrm{dia}}$ is the global diamagnetic plasma stored energy and $t$ is time. The differentiated energy is three-halves of the volume integral of total perpendicular pressure over the plasma, including both thermal and fast-particle contributions. Consequently, this derivative is not restricted to thermal energy evolution. It is a global accumulation term, not a local power density or a power attributed to an individual heating or loss mechanism. Sign convention: Positive when the global diamagnetic plasma stored energy increases with time. The time derivative is the rate of change of a quantity with time at a fixed point or label.

time_derivative_of_electron_density

description before

Rate of change of free-electron number density, with spatial aggregation determined by the specialization and positive values indicating increasing electron content.

description after

Rate of change of free-electron number density, with spatial aggregation determined by the specialization and positive values indicating increasing electron content.

documentation before

This quantity is the temporal rate of change of free-electron number density. It describes how the electron content per physical volume changes, whether the density is evaluated locally or after a defined spatial aggregation. The transformed quantity is defined by $$ \dot{n}_{e,\mathcal{A}}(t) = \frac{d}{dt}\,\mathcal{A}[n_e(\mathbf{r},t)] $$ where $\dot{n}_{e,\mathcal{A}}$ is the rate of change, $n_e(\mathbf{r},t)$ is the local [electron density](name:electron_density), $\mathbf{r}$ is position, $t$ is time, and $\mathcal{A}$ is the applicable spatial aggregation operation. The quantity applies to the free-electron population and retains the density aggregation of its specialization. The parent does not itself prescribe a local value, line average, volume average, region, or other spatial weighting; these are supplied by the more specific standard name. It denotes a density rate rather than a total electron-particle rate obtained by integrating over volume. The [volume-averaged time derivative of electron density](name:volume_averaged_time_derivative_of_electron_density) is a specialization in which $\mathcal{A}$ is the confined-plasma volume average. Positive values indicate increasing aggregated electron density, while negative values indicate decreasing aggregated electron density.

documentation after

This quantity is the temporal rate of change of free-electron number density. It describes how the electron content per physical volume changes, whether the density is evaluated locally or after a defined spatial aggregation. The transformed quantity is defined by $$ \dot{n}_{e,\mathcal{A}}(t) = \frac{d}{dt}\,\mathcal{A}[n_e(\mathbf{r},t)] $$ where $\dot{n}_{e,\mathcal{A}}$ is the rate of change, $n_e(\mathbf{r},t)$ is the local [electron density](name:electron_density), $\mathbf{r}$ is position, $t$ is time, and $\mathcal{A}$ is the applicable spatial aggregation operation. The quantity applies to the free-electron population and retains the density aggregation of its specialization. The parent does not itself prescribe a local value, line average, volume average, region, or other spatial weighting; these are supplied by the more specific standard name. It denotes a density rate rather than a total electron-particle rate obtained by integrating over volume. The [volume-averaged time derivative of electron density](name:volume_averaged_time_derivative_of_electron_density) is a specialization in which $\mathcal{A}$ is the confined-plasma volume average. Positive values indicate increasing aggregated electron density, while negative values indicate decreasing aggregated electron density.

time_derivative_of_total_thermal_plasma_internal_energy

description before

Temporal rate of change of the plasma’s total thermal internal energy, representing net thermal power deposited into or removed from the plasma.

description after

Net rate of change of stored random kinetic energy in thermal plasma populations, integrated over the plasma volume and summed over those populations.

documentation before

The time derivative of total thermal plasma internal energy is the net temporal rate at which the aggregate thermal energy of the plasma changes. Positive accumulation denotes increasing thermal energy, while negative accumulation denotes net thermal-energy loss. $$ P_{\mathrm{net,th}}(t) = \frac{dU_{\mathrm{th,pl}}(t)}{dt} $$ where $P_{\mathrm{net,th}}$ is the total thermal-energy change rate, $U_{\mathrm{th,pl}}$ is the total thermal internal energy of the plasma, and $t$ is time. The underlying energy is integrated over the plasma volume and aggregated over the included thermal plasma populations. It excludes nonthermal-particle energy, bulk-flow kinetic energy, and electromagnetic-field energy; it is not restricted to a single species or energy-transfer channel. This quantity is the time derivative of [total thermal plasma internal energy](name:total_thermal_plasma_internal_energy). It is a global thermal-energy balance quantity, distinct from [volume-integrated electron power density](name:volume_integrated_electron_power_density), which is restricted to the electron population, and from [power at inside flux surface](name:power_at_inside_flux_surface), which is associated with a specified enclosed region. Sign convention: Positive when the total thermal plasma internal energy increases with time.

documentation after

The [time derivative](concept:time_derivative) of total thermal plasma internal energy is the signed thermal-energy accumulation term in the global plasma energy balance. The defining expression is: $$ \frac{\partial W_{\mathrm{th}}}{\partial t} $$ where $W_{\mathrm{th}}$ is the [total thermal plasma internal energy](name:total_thermal_plasma_internal_energy) and $t$ is time. The differentiated energy is integrated over the plasma volume and summed over thermal plasma populations. It excludes ordered-flow, magnetic-field, radiation-field, neutral-particle, and fast-particle energy. The derivative reflects the net balance of heating, transport, and radiative losses; it is neither a local power density nor deposited heating power alone. Sign convention: Positive when the total thermal plasma internal energy increases with time. The time derivative is the rate of change of a quantity with time at a fixed point or label.

toroidal_current_density_flux_surface_averaged_at_constraint_position

description before

Signed toroidal current-density constraint obtained by geometrically normalized averaging over the magnetic surface that passes through a specified constraint position.

description after

Signed toroidal current-density constraint obtained by geometrically normalized averaging over the magnetic surface that passes through a specified constraint position.

documentation before

The quantity is the toroidal component of plasma current density represented by a flux-surface average, with the magnetic surface selected by a specified constraint position. It is a signed current-density quantity, not the local toroidal current density at the position itself. The flux-surface-averaged value is defined by $$ J_{\\phi,\\mathrm{FS}}(\\chi_c)=\\frac{\\left\\langle j_\\phi/R\\right\\rangle_{\\chi_c}}{\\left\\langle 1/R\\right\\rangle_{\\chi_c}} $$ where $J_{\\phi,\\mathrm{FS}}$ is the resulting flux-surface-averaged toroidal current density, $j_\\phi$ is the local toroidal component of plasma current density, $R$ is major radius, and $\\langle\\cdot\\rangle_{\\chi_c}$ denotes a flux-surface average over the magnetic surface selected by the constraint-position label $\\chi_c$. The quantities $R$, $\\phi$, and $Z$ refer to the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\\phi,Z)$ frame, with $\\phi$ the toroidal angle and $Z$ the vertical coordinate. The scope is the plasma current density on the complete magnetic surface through the specified constraint position, aggregated by the normalized geometric weighting in the defining relation rather than by a local point value. It does not select a particle species, orbit class, or current-drive mechanism, and it is distinct from a local or component-restricted current-density contribution. This quantity is the constraint-position, flux-surface-averaged form of [toroidal total current density](name:toroidal_total_current_density). It differs from [parallel flux-surface-averaged current density at constraint position](name:parallel_flux_surface_averaged_current_density_at_constraint_position), which uses the magnetic-field-parallel projection instead of the geometric toroidal projection. Sign convention: Positive when the toroidal current-density component points in the direction of increasing toroidal angle $\\phi$.

documentation after

The quantity is the toroidal component of plasma current density represented by a flux-surface average, with the magnetic surface selected by a specified constraint position. It is a signed current-density quantity, not the local toroidal current density at the position itself. The flux-surface-averaged value is defined by $$ J_{\\phi,\\mathrm{FS}}(\\chi_c)=\\frac{\\left\\langle j_\\phi/R\\right\\rangle_{\\chi_c}}{\\left\\langle 1/R\\right\\rangle_{\\chi_c}} $$ where $J_{\\phi,\\mathrm{FS}}$ is the resulting flux-surface-averaged toroidal current density, $j_\\phi$ is the local toroidal component of plasma current density, $R$ is major radius, and $\\langle\\cdot\\rangle_{\\chi_c}$ denotes a flux-surface average over the magnetic surface selected by the constraint-position label $\\chi_c$. The quantities $R$, $\\phi$, and $Z$ refer to the right-handed cylindrical $(R,\\phi,Z)$ frame, with $\\phi$ the toroidal angle and $Z$ the vertical coordinate. The scope is the plasma current density on the complete magnetic surface through the specified constraint position, aggregated by the normalized geometric weighting in the defining relation rather than by a local point value. It does not select a particle species, orbit class, or current-drive mechanism, and it is distinct from a local or component-restricted current-density contribution. This quantity is the constraint-position, flux-surface-averaged form of [toroidal total current density](name:toroidal_total_current_density). It differs from [parallel flux-surface-averaged current density at constraint position](name:parallel_flux_surface_averaged_current_density_at_constraint_position), which uses the magnetic-field-parallel projection instead of the geometric toroidal projection. Sign convention: Positive when the toroidal current-density component points in the direction of increasing toroidal angle $\\phi$.

total_hydrogenic_density

description before

Hydrogen-isotope abundance measure derived from included charge states and isotopes, either averaged along a line or over a volume or used to normalize neutral-state density.

description after

Hydrogen-isotope abundance measure derived from included charge states and isotopes, either averaged along a line or over a volume or used to normalize neutral-state density.

documentation before

Hydrogenic-density-derived quantities quantify the abundance of hydrogen-isotope particles with the included isotopic species and charge states. Depending on the derivative, the total density is averaged along a line, averaged over a volume, or used as the reference denominator for a neutral-state composition fraction. The underlying [hydrogenic density](name:hydrogenic_density) is defined by $$ n_{\mathrm{H}} = \sum_{s \in \mathcal{H}} \sum_q n_{s,q} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{H}}$ is the total hydrogenic number density, $\mathcal{H}$ is the set of included hydrogen-isotope species, $q$ is charge state, and $n_{s,q}$ is the number density of species $s$ in state $q$. The line-averaged total hydrogenic density and volume-averaged total hydrogenic density retain density character under their respective averaging operations. The [neutral-state density ratio](name:ratio_of_neutral_state_density_to_total_hydrogenic_density) is instead a composition fraction formed by dividing a neutral charge-state density by $n_{\mathrm{H}}$, and is therefore not itself a density.

documentation after

Hydrogenic-density-derived quantities quantify the abundance of hydrogen-isotope particles with the included isotopic species and charge states. Depending on the derivative, the total density is averaged along a line, averaged over a volume, or used as the reference denominator for a neutral-state composition fraction. The underlying [hydrogenic density](name:hydrogenic_density) is defined by $$ n_{\mathrm{H}} = \sum_{s \in \mathcal{H}} \sum_q n_{s,q} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{H}}$ is the total hydrogenic number density, $\mathcal{H}$ is the set of included hydrogen-isotope species, $q$ is charge state, and $n_{s,q}$ is the number density of species $s$ in state $q$. The line-averaged total hydrogenic density and volume-averaged total hydrogenic density retain density character under their respective averaging operations. The [neutral-state density ratio](name:ratio_of_neutral_state_density_to_total_hydrogenic_density) is instead a composition fraction formed by dividing a neutral charge-state density by $n_{\mathrm{H}}$, and is therefore not itself a density.

tritium_density

description before

Local number density of tritium ions in the plasma, the foundational fuel-ion parameter underlying chord-averaged, volume-averaged, and isotopic ratio child quantities.

description after

Number density of tritium ions is the number of positively charged tritium nuclei per physical volume of plasma. The ions of the species are counted over all charge states, with neutral atoms excluded.

documentation before

Tritium ion number density $n_T$ quantifies the local concentration of hydrogen-3 fuel ions in the plasma. It serves as the common physical basis for a family of derived quantities: the chord-integrated mean [line_averaged_tritium_density](name:line_averaged_tritium_density), the confined-volume mean [volume_averaged_tritium_density](name:volume_averaged_tritium_density), and dimensionless isotopic ratios such as ratio_of_tritium_density_to_electron_density and [ratio_of_tritium_density_to_deuterium_density](name:ratio_of_tritium_density_to_deuterium_density). In deuterium-tritium (DT) fusion plasmas the local volumetric reaction rate is: $$ R_{DT} = n_T \, n_D \langle \sigma v \rangle_{DT} $$ where $n_D$ is the deuterium ion number density and $\langle \sigma v \rangle_{DT}$ is the Maxwellian-averaged DT fusion reactivity. The combined DT fuel-ion purity relative to the electron population is tracked by ratio_of_deuterium_tritium_density_to_electron_density. Tritium density is inferred from active charge-exchange recombination spectroscopy, neutron emission measurements in DT plasmas, or pellet injection and fueling diagnostics.

documentation after

Number density of tritium. The local density is the zeroth velocity-space moment: $$ n_{\mathrm{T}} = \int f_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathbf{v})\,\mathrm{d}^3v $$ where $n_{\mathrm{T}}$ is the tritium-ion number density, $f_{\mathrm{T}}$ is the tritium-ion velocity distribution at the point under consideration, $\mathbf{v}$ is particle velocity, and $\mathrm{d}^3v$ is the velocity-space volume element. The integral covers the complete tritium-ion velocity distribution. This species-specific density is not restricted to a particular plasma region and is distinct from the total number density of all ion species. No spatial averaging is implicit. The [line-averaged tritium density](name:line_averaged_tritium_density) and [volume-averaged tritium density](name:volume_averaged_tritium_density) are respectively chord and confined-plasma-volume averages of this local density. It is also the numerator of the [tritium-to-deuterium density ratio](name:ratio_of_tritium_density_to_deuterium_density), whose denominator is [deuterium density](name:deuterium_density); that ratio describes isotope composition rather than absolute concentration. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

tungsten_density

description before

Number density of tungsten ions summed over all positive ionization states, excluding neutral tungsten, at a specified location or spatial average.

description after

Number density of tungsten ions summed over all positive ionization states, excluding neutral tungsten, at a specified location or spatial average.

documentation before

Tungsten-ion number density is the particle count per unit volume for all positively charged tungsten ions, with neutral tungsten excluded. It may denote a local value or a spatially averaged value over a defined domain. The charge-state-summed density is defined by $$ n_{\mathrm{W}} = \sum_{Z=1}^{74} n_{\mathrm{W}}^{Z+} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{W}}$ and $n_{\mathrm{W}}^{Z+}$ are number densities in m$^{-3}$, and $Z$ is the positive tungsten charge state. Specific instances include [volume-averaged tungsten density](name:volume_averaged_tungsten_density), [tungsten density at magnetic axis](name:tungsten_density_at_magnetic_axis), and [tungsten density at divertor target](name:tungsten_density_at_divertor_target); these differ by evaluation locus or averaging domain. Neutral tungsten is not included.

documentation after

Tungsten-ion number density is the particle count per unit volume for all positively charged tungsten ions, with neutral tungsten excluded. It may denote a local value or a spatially averaged value over a defined domain. The charge-state-summed density is defined by $$ n_{\mathrm{W}} = \sum_{Z=1}^{74} n_{\mathrm{W}}^{Z+} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{W}}$ and $n_{\mathrm{W}}^{Z+}$ are number densities in m$^{-3}$, and $Z$ is the positive tungsten charge state. Specific instances include [volume-averaged tungsten density](name:volume_averaged_tungsten_density), [tungsten density at magnetic axis](name:tungsten_density_at_magnetic_axis), and [tungsten density at divertor target](name:tungsten_density_at_divertor_target); these differ by evaluation locus or averaging domain. Neutral tungsten is not included.

volume_averaged_argon_density

description before

Volume-averaged number density of argon ions over the confined plasma volume, summed over all ionization stages.

description after

Number density of argon ions summed over positive charge states and averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface, excluding neutral argon.

documentation before

The volume-averaged argon ion number density is the spatial mean of the local [argon ion density](name:argon_density) throughout the confined plasma volume, including contributions from every argon ionization stage. It is distinct from a local or charge-state-resolved density. The volume average is defined by $$ \langle n_\mathrm{Ar}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V_\mathrm{p}}\int_{V_\mathrm{p}} n_\mathrm{Ar}(\mathbf{r})\,\mathrm{d}V $$ where $V_\mathrm{p}$ is the confined plasma volume, $\mathbf{r}$ is position, $\mathrm{d}V$ is a volume element, and $n_\mathrm{Ar}(\mathbf{r})$ is the local argon ion density. The local density is charge-state summed, $n_\mathrm{Ar}(\mathbf{r}) = \sum_{q=1}^{18} n_{\mathrm{Ar},q}(\mathbf{r})$, where $q$ is the argon ionization stage. This quantity provides the argon-density numerator for the impurity concentration ratio [ratio of volume-averaged argon density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_argon_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density).

documentation after

Number density of argon. Its relation to the local [argon density](name:argon_density) is $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{Ar}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{\mathrm{Ar}}\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Ar}}$ is the local argon-ion number density, $\langle n_{\mathrm{Ar}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged value, $V$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is a volume element. The contributing argon charge numbers range from one through eighteen, and both thermal and nonthermal ions contribute. Each ion contributes one particle, without weighting by electric charge or mass. This is the argon-specific instance of [volume-averaged ion density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density), not the all-species [volume-averaged total ion density](name:volume_averaged_total_ion_density). It differs from [line-averaged argon density](name:line_averaged_argon_density), which averages along a specified plasma chord. Multiplying this volume average by the confined plasma volume gives the total number of argon ions within that volume. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

volume_averaged_beryllium_density

description before

Volume-averaged number density of beryllium impurity ions over the confined plasma volume, summed over all ionization states and excluding neutral beryllium atoms.

description after

Number density of beryllium ions summed over positive charge states and averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface, excluding neutral beryllium.

documentation before

The volume-averaged beryllium ion number density quantifies the mean beryllium impurity content throughout the confined plasma volume. It is the volume average of the local [beryllium ion number density](name:beryllium_density), rather than a value at a particular position or along a diagnostic chord. $$ \overline{n}_\mathrm{Be} = \frac{1}{V_\mathrm{p}} \int_{V_\mathrm{p}} n_\mathrm{Be}(\mathbf{r})\,dV = \frac{1}{V_\mathrm{p}} \int_{V_\mathrm{p}} \sum_{z=1}^{4} n_{\mathrm{Be}^{z+}}(\mathbf{r})\,dV $$ where $V_\mathrm{p}$ is the confined plasma volume, $dV$ is a volume element, $\mathbf{r}$ is position, and $n_{\mathrm{Be}^{z+}}$ is the local number density of beryllium ions with charge state $z+$, with $z=1$ through $4$. Neutral beryllium is excluded. The resulting global quantity is distinct from the [line-averaged beryllium density](name:line_averaged_beryllium_density) and provides the numerator for the [ratio of volume-averaged beryllium density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_beryllium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density).

documentation after

Number density of beryllium. Its relation to the local [beryllium density](name:beryllium_density) is $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{Be}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{\mathrm{Be}}\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Be}}$ is the local beryllium-ion number density, $\langle n_{\mathrm{Be}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged value, $V$ is the confined plasma volume, and $dV$ is a volume element. The contributing beryllium charge numbers range from one through four, and both thermal and nonthermal ions contribute. Each ion counts once, without electric-charge or mass weighting. This is a single-species number density, not an all-species total, charge density, or mass density; the spatial mean is distinct from a local value or a chord mean. This is the beryllium-specific member of the [volume-averaged ion density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density) family. Multiplying it by the averaging volume gives the number of beryllium ions within that volume. Dividing it by the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density) over the same volume gives the [beryllium-to-electron abundance ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_beryllium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density); this ratio of averages is not generally the volume average of the local density ratio. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

volume_averaged_deuterium_density

description before

Deuterium ion density averaged over the plasma volume is the mean local number density of ionized deuterium in the fuel-ion population.

description after

Number density of deuterium ions summed over positive charge states and averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface, excluding neutral deuterium.

documentation before

Deuterium ion density averaged over the plasma volume is the mean local number density of ionized deuterium in the fuel-ion population. It is the charge-state-summed spatial average of the local [deuterium density](name:deuterium_density). The volume average is defined by $$ \overline{n}_D = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_D(\mathbf{r})\,dV $$ where $\overline{n}_D$ is the volume-averaged deuterium density, $V$ is the specified plasma-volume domain, and $n_D(\mathbf{r})$ is the local deuterium density at position $\mathbf{r}$. The local density is summed over positive ionization states and excludes neutral atoms, molecular deuterium, and non-deuterium species. This quantity averages over the specified plasma volume rather than along a chord; the latter is represented by [line-averaged deuterium density](name:line_averaged_deuterium_density).

documentation after

Number density of deuterium. Its relation to the local [deuterium density](name:deuterium_density) is $$ \langle n_{\mathrm D}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{\mathrm D}\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm D}$ is the local deuterium-ion number density, $\langle n_{\mathrm D}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged value, $V$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is a volume element. Each deuteron contributes one particle, without charge or mass weighting. This quantity is a volume mean rather than a local concentration or chord mean. Multiplying it by the enclosed volume gives the deuteron inventory within that volume. Unlike the [volume-averaged deuterium-tritium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density), this quantity includes deuterium alone, not the combined fuel-ion population. It supplies the numerator of the [ratio of volume-averaged deuterium density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_deuterium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density), whose numerator and denominator refer to the same plasma volume. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density

description before

Deuterium-tritium density is the volume-averaged ion number density of the combined deuterium-tritium fuel pseudo-species, treating deuterium and tritium as one effective species.

description after

Number density of deuterium–tritium fuel ions treated as one effective species, including all ionic charge states but no neutral atoms, averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface.

documentation before

Deuterium-tritium density is the volume-averaged ion number density of the combined deuterium-tritium fuel-ion pseudo-species, treating deuterium and tritium ions as one effective species. It is obtained from the local [deuterium-tritium density](name:deuterium_tritium_density), which is summed over all ion charge states. The defining volume average is $$ \bar n_{DT} = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{DT}\,dV $$ where $\bar n_{DT}$ and $n_{DT}$ are number densities in m$^{-3}$, $V$ is the specified averaging volume in m$^{3}$, and $dV$ is its volume element. The local density satisfies $n_{DT}=n_D+n_T$, with $n_D$ and $n_T$ the corresponding charge-state-summed deuterium and tritium ion densities. The quantity includes only deuterium and tritium fuel ions represented by the effective pseudo-species; neutral atoms and molecules, reaction products, and impurity ions are excluded. Unlike the [line-averaged deuterium-tritium density](name:line_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density), it uses a volume measure rather than a line-of-sight average, and it supplies the numerator of the [ratio of volume-averaged deuterium-tritium density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density).

documentation after

Number density of deuterium tritium. Its relation to the local [deuterium–tritium density](name:deuterium_tritium_density) is $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{DT}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{\mathrm{DT}}\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm{DT}}$ is the local effective fuel-ion number density, $\langle n_{\mathrm{DT}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged value, $V$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is a volume element. The local effective-species density satisfies $n_{\mathrm{DT}}=n_{\mathrm D}+n_{\mathrm T}$, where $n_{\mathrm D}$ is the [deuterium-ion density](name:deuterium_density) and $n_{\mathrm T}$ is the tritium-ion density. Consequently, this average equals the sum of the [volume-averaged deuterium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_density) and the corresponding tritium density when both use the same averaging volume. The effective species denotes the combined fuel population, not the deuterium–tritium fusion reaction channel. Multiplying this average by the confined plasma volume gives the combined fuel-ion inventory. Unlike [line-averaged deuterium–tritium density](name:line_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density), it describes a volume mean rather than a chord mean. It supplies the numerator of the [ratio of volume-averaged deuterium–tritium density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_deuterium_tritium_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density); that ratio compares two volume means rather than averaging a local density ratio. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

volume_averaged_effective_charge

description before

Dimensionless volume-averaged effective ionic charge of a plasma mixture, formed by averaging the local second ionic charge moment normalized by free-electron density over the confined plasma volume.

description after

Volume-averaged effective charge expresses the plasma ion mixture's mean composition factor for electron–ion Coulomb scattering over the plasma volume.

documentation before

Volume-averaged effective ionic charge is the effective charge of the complete ion mixture after spatially averaging the local charge-weighted ionic charge moment over the confined plasma volume. The defining relation is $$ Z_{\mathrm{eff},V} = \frac{1}{V_p}\int_{V_p} Z_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathbf{x})\,dV $$ where $Z_{\mathrm{eff},V}$ is the volume-averaged effective charge, $Z_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathbf{x})$ is the local [effective charge](name:effective_charge), $V_p$ is the confined plasma volume, $\mathbf{x}$ is spatial position, and $dV$ is the volume element. The average applies to the complete included ion mixture at every position, with ionic contributions summed over all represented species and charge states. It covers the confined plasma volume and is not restricted to one species, one charge state, one region, or one flux surface; it is distinct from an ordinary ion charge average. This quantity is the volume-averaged counterpart of [effective charge](name:effective_charge). It differs from flux-surface-averaged effective charge at the plasma boundary, which averages the local effective charge over a boundary surface, and from [volume-averaged ion charge](name:volume_averaged_ion_charge), which represents an ordinary ion charge number rather than the second charge moment normalized by free-electron density.

documentation after

Volume-averaged effective charge characterizes the composition-dependent contribution of the full plasma ion mixture to electron–ion Coulomb scattering. It applies the volume average to the local [effective charge](name:effective_charge) throughout the plasma volume. The local quantity entering the average is defined by: $$ Z_{\mathrm{eff}} = \frac{\sum_a n_a Z_a^2}{n_e} $$ where $Z_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the local effective charge, $a$ indexes each ion species and positive charge state, $n_a$ is its local number density, $Z_a$ is its ionic charge number, and $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density. The sum includes all positive charge states of all ion species, including impurities. Neutral atoms do not contribute to the charge-number moment, and bound electrons are excluded from the free-electron density. The spatial average acts on the local ratio; it is not generally the ratio of the separately volume-averaged numerator and denominator, and no additional electron-density weighting is applied. This is a mixture-level composition quantity, distinct from the species-specific [volume-averaged ion charge](name:volume_averaged_ion_charge). The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_effective_hydrogenic_mass

description before

Volume-averaged effective hydrogenic mass is the plasma-volume mean of the local density-weighted mass per hydrogen, deuterium, or tritium ion.

description after

Volume-averaged effective hydrogenic mass is the plasma-volume average of the local density-weighted atomic mass of hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ions.

documentation before

Volume-averaged effective hydrogenic mass is the volume mean of the local [effective hydrogenic mass](name:effective_hydrogenic_mass) of the hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ion mixture. Density weighting determines the local mixture mass, while volume weighting determines its spatial average. The volume average is defined by: $$ \langle m_{\mathrm{eff,H}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V m_{\mathrm{eff,H}}\,dV $$ where $\langle m_{\mathrm{eff,H}}\rangle_V$ is the volume-averaged effective hydrogenic mass, $m_{\mathrm{eff,H}}$ is the local effective hydrogenic mass, $V$ is the plasma volume over which the average is taken, and $dV$ is an infinitesimal volume element. The local mass is $m_{\mathrm{eff,H}}=(m_H n_H+m_D n_D+m_T n_T)/(n_H+n_D+n_T)$, where $m_H$, $m_D$, and $m_T$ are the masses of hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ions, respectively, and $n_H$, $n_D$, and $n_T$ are their respective ion number densities. Only hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ions contribute to the local mixture; neutral particles, electrons, and other ion species are excluded. This volume average of the local density-weighted mass is not generally equal to the ratio of volume-integrated hydrogenic mass density to volume-integrated hydrogenic number density. Unlike the [line-averaged effective hydrogenic mass](name:line_averaged_effective_hydrogenic_mass), it averages over the plasma volume rather than along a path.

documentation after

Volume-averaged effective hydrogenic mass describes the hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium ion mixture through the volume average of its local [effective hydrogenic mass](name:effective_hydrogenic_mass) over the plasma volume. It characterizes isotope composition rather than the mass of one specified isotope. The local mass entering this average is $$ m_{\mathrm{eff}} = \frac{m_{\mathrm H}n_{\mathrm H} + m_{\mathrm D}n_{\mathrm D} + m_{\mathrm T}n_{\mathrm T}}{n_{\mathrm H} + n_{\mathrm D} + n_{\mathrm T}} $$ where $m_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the local effective hydrogenic mass; $m_{\mathrm H}$, $m_{\mathrm D}$, and $m_{\mathrm T}$ are the atomic masses of hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium, respectively; and $n_{\mathrm H}$, $n_{\mathrm D}$, and $n_{\mathrm T}$ are their corresponding ion number densities. Each isotope density is summed over all ionic charge states and excludes neutral atoms. Nonhydrogenic species do not contribute, and the local definition requires a nonzero total hydrogenic ion density. Volume averaging acts on the local density-weighted mass ratio. It does not instead form that ratio from volume-averaged isotope densities; the two operations need not agree when isotope composition and total hydrogenic ion density vary across the plasma. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_electron_decay_time_due_to_disruption

description before

E-folding time of the plasma-volume-averaged electron temperature during the thermal quench of a disruption.

description after

Time over which the plasma-volume-averaged electron temperature falls by a factor of e during exponential cooling in a disruption thermal quench.

documentation before

This decay time describes the exponential collapse of electron temperature averaged over the entire plasma volume during a disruption thermal quench. Within the exponential decay interval, it is defined by: $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $\tau$ is the exponential decay time, $t$ is time, $t_0$ is a reference time within that interval, and $\langle T_e\rangle_V(t_0)$ is the volume-averaged electron temperature at that reference time. The volume average is $\langle T_e\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V T_e\,dV$, where $T_e$ is the local [electron temperature](name:electron_temperature), $V$ is the plasma volume, and $dV$ is a volume element. The average is weighted by volume, not by electron density. Its exponential decay time is distinct from the [electron-temperature decay time at the magnetic axis](name:electron_decay_time_at_magnetic_axis_due_to_disruption), which describes a local temperature decrease. Unlike the [linear 20–80% decay time of volume-averaged thermal electron temperature](name:linear_thermal_electron_decay_time_volume_averaged_due_to_disruption), this quantity is defined by an exponential law rather than a linear decline between specified temperature fractions.

documentation after

The exponential decay time describes the collapse of the plasma-volume-averaged electron temperature during the thermal quench of a disruption. It is defined by the exponential decay law: $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $T_e$ is the [electron temperature](name:electron_temperature), $V$ is the plasma volume, $\langle T_e\rangle_V$ denotes its volume average, $t$ is time, $t_0$ is a reference time within the thermal quench, and $\tau$ is the exponential decay time. The value $\langle T_e\rangle_V(t_0)$ is the averaged temperature at that reference time. The decay time applies to the plasma-volume-averaged temperature, not to a volume average of local decay times. It is distinct from the [magnetic-axis electron-temperature decay time](name:electron_decay_time_at_magnetic_axis_due_to_disruption), which describes cooling at a single locus. It also differs from the [linear-extrapolated decay time](name:linear_thermal_electron_decay_time_volume_averaged_due_to_disruption), which uses fractional-threshold linear extrapolation rather than an exponential decay law. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_electron_density

description before

Number density of free electrons averaged over the plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface, including thermal and nonthermal populations.

description after

Number density of electrons averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface, counting both thermal and nonthermal free electrons.

documentation before

Number density of electron The volume average of the local [electron density](name:electron_density) is defined by $$ \langle n_e\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_e\,dV $$ where $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density, $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is its volume average, $V$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface and specifies the integration region, and $dV$ is a physical volume element. Multiplying the average by that volume gives the total number of free electrons inside the boundary. All free-electron populations, including thermal and nonthermal electrons, are included; electrons bound in atoms, molecules, or ions are excluded. Plasma outside the last closed flux surface is excluded from the average. Unlike [line-averaged electron density](name:line_averaged_electron_density), this quantity is a physical-volume mean rather than a chord mean. The same spatial averaging applies to [volume-averaged ion density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density), but that quantity counts the specified ion population rather than electrons. This electron mean supplies the denominator of abundance ratios such as the [ratio of volume-averaged carbon density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_carbon_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density); such a ratio uses separately averaged densities over the same volume, not a volume average of their local ratio.

documentation after

Number density of electron. The averaged quantity is the local [electron density](name:electron_density). Its relationship to the electron inventory in the confined plasma is $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $N_e$ is the total number of free electrons within the confined plasma, $V$ is the volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, $n_e$ is the local electron number density, and $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is its volume average. Bound electrons and electrons outside this volume are excluded. Each free electron contributes one particle, without charge or mass weighting. This is a volume mean, not a local value, a chord mean, or the [electron density at the plasma boundary](name:electron_density_at_plasma_boundary). The [volume-averaged ion density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density) uses the same averaging region but counts ions of a specified species rather than electrons. The electron mean supplies the denominator in ion-to-electron abundance ratios, including the [ratio of volume-averaged argon density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_argon_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density), with both populations averaged over the same volume. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_electron_number_density_over_scrape_off_layer

description before

Volume-averaged electron number density in the scrape-off layer, equal to the total number of free electrons in the averaging region divided by its volume.

description after

Volume-averaged electron number density in the scrape-off layer, counting free electrons and excluding electrons bound in atoms or ions.

documentation before

Volume-averaged electron number density over the scrape-off layer expresses the free-electron population per total averaging volume. The average weights each volume element by its physical volume. The defining volume average is $$ \langle n_e\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_e\,dV $$ where $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the volume-averaged electron number density, $n_e$ is the [local electron number density](name:electron_number_density_over_scrape_off_layer), $V$ is the volume of the specified scrape-off-layer averaging region, and $dV$ is a physical volume element. The averaging domain is restricted to the specified scrape-off-layer region rather than the full plasma volume. Only free electrons are counted; electrons bound in atoms or ions are excluded. This quantity is a regional volume mean, not a local density, line average, or line integral. Multiplying it by the averaging volume gives the total free-electron count within that same region.

documentation after

Volume-averaged electron number density in the scrape-off layer describes the regional abundance of free electrons, with each electron counted once. Electrons bound in atoms or ions are excluded; particles are not weighted by charge, mass, or energy. The averaged field is the local [electron number density in the scrape-off layer](name:electron_number_density_over_scrape_off_layer), and the averaging volume is restricted to that region. This regional mean is neither a pointwise value nor a line-integrated density. It differs from the [confined-plasma volume average](name:volume_averaged_electron_density) by its averaging region. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_electron_temperature

description before

Temperature of bulk electrons from random translational motion, volume-averaged inside the last closed magnetic flux surface, including during plasma initiation.

description after

Temperature of bulk electrons from random translational motion, arithmetically averaged over the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface.

documentation before

Temperature of bulk electrons averaged over the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface is the spatial mean of their local energy-equivalent kinetic temperature. For nonrelativistic electrons, the local temperature equals two-thirds of the mean random kinetic energy per particle, excluding directed mean-flow energy. The volume average is defined by $$ \langle T_e\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V T_e\,dV $$ where $\langle T_e\rangle_V$ is the volume-averaged electron temperature, $T_e$ is the local bulk electron temperature, $V$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface, and $dV$ is the differential physical-volume element. The quantity applies throughout that enclosed volume, including during plasma initiation. Its aggregation convention is an arithmetic spatial volume average, not a density-weighted particle average or a local value. It differs from [line-averaged electron temperature](name:line_averaged_electron_temperature), which averages along a plasma chord. It is neither total electron thermal energy nor a radiation brightness temperature. This quantity is obtained by applying volume averaging to [electron temperature](name:electron_temperature); [volume-averaged ion temperature](name:volume_averaged_ion_temperature) applies the corresponding spatial average to a specified ion species. It supplies the denominator of the [ratio of magnetic-axis to volume-averaged electron temperature](name:ratio_of_electron_temperature_at_magnetic_axis_to_volume_averaged_electron_temperature), which expresses central electron-temperature peaking relative to the plasma-wide mean.

documentation after

Temperature of bulk electrons averaged over the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface is the spatial mean of their local kinetic temperature. The local temperature represents the isotropic part of random translational energy and excludes directed bulk-flow energy. For the local [electron temperature](name:electron_temperature) $T_e$, the corresponding volume-averaged temperature $\langle T_e\rangle_V$ satisfies $$ \langle T_e\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V T_e\,dV $$ where $V$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface and $dV$ is the differential physical-volume element. The spatial average is arithmetic, not weighted by electron number density, and is distinct from volume-integrated electron thermal energy. Its domain excludes plasma outside the last closed magnetic flux surface. Unlike [line-averaged electron temperature](name:line_averaged_electron_temperature), it averages throughout the enclosed volume rather than along a specified chord. The definition also applies during plasma initiation whenever the enclosing surface exists; it is not restricted to that phase. This quantity includes only the bulk electron population; [volume-averaged ion temperature](name:volume_averaged_ion_temperature) is the corresponding quantity for a specified ion species. It supplies the denominator in the [ratio of magnetic-axis electron temperature to volume-averaged electron temperature](name:ratio_of_electron_temperature_at_magnetic_axis_to_volume_averaged_electron_temperature), which expresses central electron-temperature peaking. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_helium_3_density

description before

Volume-averaged helium-3 ion number density is the mean charged-ion population over the confined plasma volume, summed over singly ionized and fully stripped states and excluding neutral atoms.

description after

Number density of helium-3 ions in all positive charge states, excluding neutral atoms, averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface.

documentation before

Volume-averaged helium-3 ion number density is the spatial mean of the charged helium-3 ion population throughout the confined plasma volume. It is derived from the local [helium-3 density](name:helium_3_density), rather than representing a density at one location. $$ \overline{n}_{{}^{3}\mathrm{He}} = \frac{1}{V_c}\int_{V_c} n_{{}^{3}\mathrm{He}}(\mathbf{x})\,d^3\mathbf{x} $$ where $V_c$ is the confined plasma volume, $\mathbf{x}$ is position, and $n_{{}^{3}\mathrm{He}}(\mathbf{x})$ is the local helium-3 ion density summed over singly ionized and fully stripped states. Neutral helium-3 atoms are excluded. The [line-averaged helium-3 density](name:line_averaged_helium_3_density) instead averages the same local quantity along a specified path.

documentation after

Number density of helium 3. Its relation to the local [helium-3 density](name:helium_3_density) is $$ \langle n_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}\,dV $$ where $n_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}$ is the local helium-3 ion number density, $\langle n_{{}^3\mathrm{He}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged number density, $V$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is a volume element. Both thermal and nonthermal helium-3 ions contribute. Each ion contributes one particle without electric-charge or mass weighting. This is a single-isotope volume mean, not an all-species total, a local value, or a chord mean. Multiplying this average by the confined plasma volume gives the number of helium-3 ions within that volume. It is a species-specific instance of [volume-averaged ion density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density); the adjacent [volume-averaged helium-4 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_4_density) instead counts helium-4 ions. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

volume_averaged_helium_4_density

description before

Volume-averaged number density of helium-4 ions over the confined plasma volume, providing a global measure of fusion-ash concentration in deuterium-tritium plasmas.

description after

Number density of helium-4 ions in all positive charge states, excluding neutral atoms, averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface.

documentation before

The volume-averaged helium-4 ion number density quantifies the mean concentration of fusion-ash ions throughout the confined plasma volume. It is the global counterpart of the local [helium-4 ion density](name:helium_4_density) and is distinct from the chord mean [line-averaged helium-4 density](name:line_averaged_helium_4_density). The volume average is defined by $$ \overline{n}_{{}^4\mathrm{He}} = \frac{1}{V_p}\int_{V_p} n_{{}^4\mathrm{He}}(\mathbf{r})\,d^3\mathbf{r} $$ where $n_{{}^4\mathrm{He}}(\mathbf{r})$ is the local helium-4 ion number density in m$^{-3}$, $V_p$ is the confined plasma volume in m$^3$, and $\mathbf{r}$ denotes position. The helium-4 ion population is summed over all ionic charge states, while neutral helium is excluded. Its ratio to the volume-averaged electron density is defined by [ratio of volume-averaged helium-4 density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_helium_4_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density).

documentation after

Number density of helium 4. Its relation to the local [helium-4 density](name:helium_4_density) is $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{He4}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{\mathrm{He4}}\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm{He4}}$ is the local helium-4 ion number density, $\langle n_{\mathrm{He4}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged number density, $V$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is a volume element. Each counted ion contributes one particle without weighting by electric charge or mass. This is a single-isotope particle-number mean, not an all-species total, electric charge density, mass density, local value, or chord mean. This quantity is the helium-4-specific instance of [volume-averaged ion density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density). The [volume-averaged helium-3 density](name:volume_averaged_helium_3_density) describes the other helium isotope separately; helium-3 ions do not contribute here. Multiplying this mean by the confined plasma volume gives the total number of counted helium-4 ions within that volume. Dividing it by the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density) over the same volume gives the [helium-4-to-electron mean-density ratio](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_helium_4_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density), rather than a volume average of the local density ratio. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

volume_averaged_hydrogen_density

description before

Volume-averaged number density of hydrogen (protium) ions in the plasma, giving the mean H+ concentration across the confined plasma volume.

description after

Number density of hydrogen (protium) ions in all ionic charge states, excluding neutral atoms, averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface.

documentation before

The volume-averaged hydrogen density quantifies the mean concentration of ionized protium throughout the confined plasma volume. It is the spatial average of the local [hydrogen density](name:hydrogen_density), rather than its value at a particular position. $$ \overline{n}_H = \frac{1}{V_p}\int_{V_p} n_H(\mathbf{r})\,dV $$ where $\overline{n}_H$ is the volume-averaged hydrogen density, $n_H(\mathbf{r})$ is the local ionized-protium density at position $\mathbf{r}$, $V_p$ is the confined plasma volume, and $dV$ is a volume element. The average includes ionized protium throughout $V_p$ and excludes neutral atomic hydrogen and other isotopes. Unlike [line-averaged hydrogen density](name:line_averaged_hydrogen_density), it averages over the full plasma volume. Its isotope fraction relative to all hydrogenic fuel is represented by [the volume-averaged hydrogen-density fraction](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_hydrogen_density_to_volume_averaged_total_hydrogenic_density).

documentation after

Number density of hydrogen. Its relation to the local [hydrogen density](name:hydrogen_density) is $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{H}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{\mathrm{H}}\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm{H}}$ is the local protium-ion number density, $\langle n_{\mathrm{H}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged value, $V$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is a volume element. Hydrogen here denotes protium, not the combined hydrogen-isotope population. Each proton contributes one particle without electric-charge or mass weighting. This quantity is a volume mean rather than a local density or a chord mean; multiplying it by the averaging volume gives the number of protium ions within that volume. This is the protium-specific instance of [volume-averaged ion density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density), whereas [volume-averaged deuterium density](name:volume_averaged_deuterium_density) describes the separate deuterium population. Dividing this quantity by the volume-averaged total hydrogenic-ion density over the same volume gives the protium-ion number fraction. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

volume_averaged_ion_charge_state_average_charge_number

description before

Mean ionization state of a specified charge-state bundle of one ion species, weighted locally by charge-state concentration and then averaged over the plasma volume.

description after

Mean ionization state of a specified charge-state bundle of one ion species, concentration-weighted locally and volume-averaged over the plasma.

documentation before

This quantity is the plasma-volume average of the local concentration-weighted mean ionic charge number within a specified charge-state bundle of one ion species. Charge-state averaging within the bundle precedes spatial volume averaging. The volume average is defined by $$ \langle \overline{Z}_{\mathcal{B}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V \overline{Z}_{\mathcal{B}}\,dV $$ where $V$ is the plasma volume, $dV$ is a volume element, and $\overline{Z}_{\mathcal{B}}$ is the local [bundle-averaged ionic charge number](name:ion_charge_state_average_charge_number). Locally, $\overline{Z}_{\mathcal{B}} = \sum_{q\in\mathcal{B}} q x_q$, where $\mathcal{B}$ is the specified bundle, $q$ is the ionic charge number, and $x_q$ is the local number-density fraction of charge state $q$ within that bundle, with $\sum_{q\in\mathcal{B}}x_q = 1$. Only charge states within the specified bundle contribute; different ion species are not combined. The spatial average uses volume weighting without additional ion-density weighting, so it is not generally equal to the ratio of total ionic charge to total ion number over the plasma. For a bundle containing one fixed charge state, the result equals that state's charge number. This first charge-number moment is distinct from the [bundle mean-square charge number](name:square_of_ion_charge_state_charge_number), which averages the squared ionic charge number rather than the charge number.

documentation after

This quantity is the [volume average](concept:volume_averaged) of the local [ion charge-state average charge number](name:ion_charge_state_average_charge_number) for a specified charge-state bundle of one ion species. The averaging region is the plasma volume unless otherwise specified. The local mean entering this average is $$ \overline{Z}_{\mathcal{B}} = \frac{\sum_{q\in\mathcal{B}} q\,n_q}{\sum_{q\in\mathcal{B}} n_q} $$ where $\overline{Z}_{\mathcal{B}}$ is the local bundle mean ionic charge number, $\mathcal{B}$ is the specified set of charge states, $q$ is the ionic charge number labeling a state, and $n_q$ is its local number density. This local mean is defined where the bundle has nonzero total number density. The concentration weighting includes only states within the specified bundle, excluding other species and charge states outside that bundle. Spatial averaging is volume-weighted rather than particle-density-weighted, so the result is not generally the mean obtained by pooling all bundle particles across the volume. For a single-state bundle, both the local and volume-averaged means equal that state's charge number. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_ion_density

description before

Number density of ions of a specified species, summed over all ionic charge states and averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface.

description after

Number density of ions of a specified species, summed over positive charge states and averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface, excluding neutral particles.

documentation before

Number density of ion The volume average of the local [ion density](name:ion_density) is defined by $$ \langle n_i\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_i\,dV $$ where $i$ labels the specified ion species, $n_i$ is its local ion number density, $\langle n_i\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged number density, $V$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is a volume element. The species density is summed over all ionic charge states and includes thermal and nonthermal ions; neutral particles are excluded. This is a single-species particle number density, not an all-species total, electric charge density, or mass density. It is a volume mean rather than a local value or a chord mean. Multiplying this average by the confined plasma volume gives the total number of ions of the specified species within that volume. The [volume-averaged argon density](name:volume_averaged_argon_density) is a species-specific instance of the same averaging definition.

documentation after

Number density of ion. Its relation to the local [ion density](name:ion_density) is $$ \langle n_i\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_i\,dV $$ where $n_i$ is the local number density of the selected ion species, $i$ labels that species, $\langle n_i\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged number density, $V$ is the nonzero confined plasma volume, and $dV$ is a volume element. The density is summed over all positive charge states of the selected species; neutral particles are excluded. Both thermal and nonthermal ions contribute. Each ion counts once, without electric-charge or mass weighting, so this quantity is neither charge density nor mass density. The corresponding [ion charge-state density](name:ion_charge_state_density) instead resolves a specified charge state. This quantity concerns one selected species, not an all-species total. Summing it over all ion species using the same averaging volume gives the volume-averaged total ion density. Fixed-species members, such as [volume-averaged argon density](name:volume_averaged_argon_density), specialize the species selection. Multiplying this density by the confined plasma volume gives the number of ions of the selected species within that volume. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_ion_temperature

description before

Temperature of a specified ion species from random translational motion, arithmetically averaged over the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface.

description after

Temperature of a specified ion species from random translational motion, averaged arithmetically throughout the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface.

documentation before

Temperature of a specified ion species averaged over the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface is the spatial mean of its local kinetic temperature. The local temperature represents the isotropic part of random translational energy and excludes directed bulk-flow energy. The volume average of the local [ion temperature](name:ion_temperature) is defined by $$ \langle T_i\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V T_i\,dV $$ where $\langle T_i\rangle_V$ is the volume-averaged temperature of the specified ion species, $T_i$ is its local temperature, $V$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface, and $dV$ is the differential physical-volume element. For the specified species, the local temperature is the density-weighted mean over all ionic charge states; [ion charge-state temperature](name:ion_charge_state_temperature) resolves an individual charge state. The subsequent spatial average is an arithmetic volume average, not a density-weighted particle average or a volume-integrated thermal energy. It does not combine different ion species, unlike [ion average temperature](name:ion_average_temperature). [Volume-averaged electron temperature](name:volume_averaged_electron_temperature) is the corresponding electron-population quantity. This ion-species volume average supplies the denominator in the [ratio of magnetic-axis ion temperature to volume-averaged ion temperature](name:ratio_of_ion_temperature_at_magnetic_axis_to_volume_averaged_ion_temperature), which expresses central temperature peaking.

documentation after

Temperature of a specified ion species averaged over the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface describes its thermal state across the confined plasma. The local temperature represents the isotropic part of random translational energy and excludes directed bulk-flow energy. For the local [ion temperature](name:ion_temperature) $T_i$, the corresponding volume-averaged temperature $\langle T_i\rangle_V$ satisfies $$ \langle T_i\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V T_i\,dV $$ where $V$ is the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface and $dV$ is the differential physical-volume element. At each location, the species temperature is the density-weighted mean over all charge states of that species. The spatial average is arithmetic, not weighted by ion number density. It is distinct from volume-integrated ion thermal energy and from the cross-species averaging represented by [ion average temperature](name:ion_average_temperature). The averaging domain excludes plasma outside the last closed magnetic flux surface. The definition also applies during plasma initiation whenever the enclosing surface exists; it is not restricted to that phase. This quantity applies to one specified ion species; [volume-averaged electron temperature](name:volume_averaged_electron_temperature) is the corresponding electron quantity. It supplies the denominator in the [ratio of magnetic-axis ion temperature to volume-averaged ion temperature](name:ratio_of_ion_temperature_at_magnetic_axis_to_volume_averaged_ion_temperature), which expresses central ion-temperature peaking. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_iron_density

description before

Volume-averaged iron density is the total number density of iron ions, summed over Fe1+ through Fe26+ and averaged over the confined plasma volume.

description after

Number density of iron ions summed over positive charge states and averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface, excluding neutral iron.

documentation before

Volume-averaged iron density is the total number density of iron ions, aggregated over all iron charge states from Fe$^{1+}$ through Fe$^{26+}$ and averaged throughout the confined plasma volume. It is the spatially averaged form of [iron_density](name:iron_density), not a charge-state-resolved quantity. The volume-averaged density is defined by $$ \overline{n}_{\mathrm{Fe}} = \frac{1}{V_{\mathrm{p}}} \int_{V_{\mathrm{p}}} \left(\sum_{z=1}^{26} n_{\mathrm{Fe}^{z+}}(\mathbf{r})\right)\,dV $$ where $\overline{n}_{\mathrm{Fe}}$ is the volume-averaged iron density in m$^{-3}$, $V_{\mathrm{p}}$ is the confined plasma volume in m$^{3}$, $n_{\mathrm{Fe}^{z+}}(\mathbf{r})$ is the local density of iron charge state $z$, $\mathbf{r}$ is position, and $dV$ is a volume element. Neutral iron is excluded, and the result does not preserve the distribution among charge states. It differs from [volume_averaged_ion_density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density) through its fixed iron-species scope and from [iron_density_at_magnetic_axis](name:iron_density_at_magnetic_axis) because it represents a volume average rather than a local axis value.

documentation after
