Number density of iron. Its relation to the local [iron density](name:iron_density) is $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{Fe}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{\mathrm{Fe}}\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Fe}}$ is the local iron-ion number density, $\langle n_{\mathrm{Fe}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged value, $V$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is a volume element. Both thermal and nonthermal iron ions contribute. Each ion contributes one count, without weighting by electric charge or mass. This is a single-species number density, not an all-species total, electric charge density, or mass density. This volume mean differs from a local value and from [line-averaged iron density](name:line_averaged_iron_density), which averages along a specified plasma chord. Multiplying this volume mean by $V$ gives the number of iron ions within the confined plasma volume. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

volume_averaged_lithium_density

description before

Volume-averaged total number density of lithium ions, summed over all charge states from Li+ through fully stripped Li3+, within the confined plasma volume.

description after

Number density of lithium ions summed over all ionic charge states, excluding neutral atoms, and averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface.

documentation before

This is the volume average of lithium ion number density, summed over all ionization stages from Li+ through fully stripped Li3+, throughout the confined plasma. Neutral lithium is excluded. The quantity provides a global measure of lithium impurity content rather than a local or boundary value. The volume average is defined as $$ \overline{n}_{Li} = \frac{1}{V_p}\int_{V_p} n_{Li}\,dV $$ where $n_{Li}$ is the local lithium-ion number density, $V_p$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is the physical volume element. Unlike the boundary value flux-surface-averaged lithium density at the plasma boundary, this quantity averages over the full confined volume. Its electron-normalized counterpart is the ratio of volume-averaged lithium density to volume-averaged electron density.

documentation after

Number density of lithium. The species-specific volume average is $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{Li}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{\mathrm{Li}}\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Li}}$ is the local lithium-ion number density, $\langle n_{\mathrm{Li}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged value, $V$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is a volume element. Each ion contributes one particle without weighting by electric charge or mass. This is the lithium-specific instance of [volume-averaged ion density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density), not the [volume-averaged total ion density](name:volume_averaged_total_ion_density) summed across species. It is a volume mean rather than a local value or a chord mean. Multiplying this average by the confined plasma volume gives the number of lithium ions within that volume. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

volume_averaged_parallel_electric_field_due_to_dreicer

description before

Plasma-volume mean of the parallel electric-field magnitude at which electric acceleration competes with collisional drag on bulk thermal electrons.

description after

Plasma-volume-averaged electric-field scale at which magnetic-field-parallel acceleration competes with Coulomb drag on bulk thermal electrons.

documentation before

The volume-averaged Dreicer field is the plasma-volume mean of the positive local field scale for accelerating bulk thermal electrons into runaway motion parallel to the magnetic field. It is the unweighted volume average of the [local Dreicer field](name:parallel_electric_field_due_to_dreicer): $$ \langle E_D\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V E_D\,dV $$ where $\langle E_D\rangle_V$ is the volume-averaged Dreicer field, $E_D$ is the local Dreicer field magnitude, $V$ is the plasma volume, and $dV$ is an infinitesimal volume element. The average covers the plasma volume and is not weighted by electron density. It averages a positive reference magnitude, not the signed applied parallel electric field. The Dreicer scale characterizes electric acceleration competing with collisional drag on thermal electrons; it is not a sharp threshold below which thermal-tail runaway generation vanishes. This quantity is distinct from the [volume-averaged runaway-electron critical field](name:volume_averaged_runaway_electron_critical_electric_field), which concerns sustained acceleration of relativistic electrons rather than acceleration out of the bulk thermal population.

documentation after

The plasma-volume-averaged Dreicer field characterizes primary runaway generation from the thermal electron population. It is the global average of the local [Dreicer-field magnitude](name:parallel_electric_field_due_to_dreicer), rather than the applied parallel electric field. Its defining relation is $$ \langle E_{\mathrm D}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V E_{\mathrm D}\,dV $$ where $E_{\mathrm D}$ is the local Dreicer-field magnitude, $V$ is the plasma volume, $dV$ is a volume element, and $\langle E_{\mathrm D}\rangle_V$ is the plasma-volume-averaged Dreicer field. The averaging covers the plasma volume with volume weighting only, not electron-density weighting. The result is a nonnegative field scale, not a signed electric-field component. It differs from the [volume-averaged runaway-electron critical field](name:volume_averaged_runaway_electron_critical_electric_field), which concerns sustained acceleration of already energetic electrons against collisional drag rather than acceleration out of the thermal population. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_parallel_runaway_electron_current_density

description before

Plasma-volume average of the signed conventional current-density component carried by runaway electrons along the local magnetic-field direction.

description after

Parallel current density carried by runaway electrons, expressed as a magnetic-field-weighted plasma-volume average in the reference-field convention.

documentation before

This quantity is the plasma-volume average of the local field-aligned conventional current density carried only by runaway electrons. It selects the magnetic-field-parallel contribution of the runaway-electron current before applying the same volume averaging represented by volume-averaged runaway-electron current density. $$ \left\langle j_{\parallel,\mathrm{RE}} \right\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V_p}\int_{V_p} \mathbf{j}_{\mathrm{RE}}(\mathbf{x}) \cdot \hat{\mathbf{b}}(\mathbf{x})\,dV $$ where $\mathbf{j}_{\mathrm{RE}}$ is the local conventional current-density vector of the runaway-electron population, $\hat{\mathbf{b}}$ is the local unit vector in the magnetic-field direction, $V_p$ is the selected plasma volume, and $dV$ is its volume element. Contributions from thermal electrons, ions, and non-runaway populations are excluded. Because this is conventional current, its direction is opposite to the motion of negatively charged runaway electrons. Sign convention: Positive when the local conventional runaway-electron current component points along the local magnetic-field direction.

documentation after

Parallel runaway-electron current density averaged over the plasma volume represents the signed conventional-current contribution of the runaway-electron population. It is the plasma-volume aggregate of [parallel runaway-electron current density](name:parallel_runaway_electron_current_density), using the same reference-field convention. Its defining relation is: $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $\overline{j}_{\parallel,\mathrm{RE}}$ is this volume-averaged parallel current density, $\mathbf{j}_{\mathrm{RE}}$ is the conventional current-density vector carried by runaway electrons, $\mathbf{B}$ is the magnetic-field vector, and $B_0$ is the nonzero reference magnetic-field value used for normalization. The brackets $\langle\cdot\rangle_V$ denote the volume-average operator over the plasma volume $V$. The magnetic-field weighting distinguishes this convention from an unweighted average of the local current projection onto the magnetic-field unit vector. The quantity includes only runaway-electron current, excluding thermal-electron and ion contributions. It is a global current density, not a local value, a single-flux-surface average, or a cross-section-integrated runaway current. Sign convention: Positive when the plasma-volume average of $\mathbf{j}_{\mathrm{RE}}\cdot\mathbf{B}$ has the same sign as $B_0$. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_ratio_of_hydrogen_density_to_electron_density

description before

Ratio of hydrogen (protium) ion density to electron density, averaged over the plasma volume at the time of peak electron density.

description after

Ratio of volume-averaged protium-ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density in the confined plasma, evaluated at the time of peak electron density.

documentation before

Ratio of local hydrogen (protium) ion number density to local electron number density, averaged over the plasma volume and evaluated at the time of peak electron density. It expresses hydrogen-ion abundance relative to free electrons. The volume average is defined by $$ \langle n_\mathrm{H}/n_e\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V \frac{n_\mathrm{H}}{n_e}\,dV $$ where $n_\mathrm{H}$ is the local hydrogen ion number density, $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density, $V$ is the plasma volume and denotes the integration region, and $dV$ is a volume element. The angle brackets denote the volume average. All densities and the integration volume refer to the time of peak electron density; this is not a time average. The numerator includes all ionic charge states of protium, comprising only singly ionized hydrogen. Neutral hydrogen, deuterium, and tritium are excluded from the numerator. The local ratio requires nonzero electron density wherever it is evaluated. This quantity is the volume average of the [hydrogen-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_hydrogen_density_to_electron_density), not, in general, the ratio of the two volume-averaged densities. The [lithium counterpart](name:volume_averaged_ratio_of_lithium_density_to_electron_density) applies the same spatial averaging and peak-electron-density evaluation convention to lithium ions.

documentation after

Ratio of volume-averaged hydrogen (protium) ion number density to volume-averaged free-electron number density, evaluated at the time of peak electron density. The composition ratio is $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $C_{\mathrm{H}/e}$ is this composition ratio, $n_{\mathrm H}$ is the local [protium-ion number density](name:hydrogen_density), $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density, and $t_*$ is the time of peak electron density. The numerator is the [volume-averaged hydrogen density](name:volume_averaged_hydrogen_density), and the denominator is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density). Both averages use the same nonzero confined plasma volume $V$ inside the last closed flux surface; the denominator must be positive. The numerator is summed over all positive charge states of protium, counting each ion once without charge or mass weighting. Neutral hydrogen and other hydrogen isotopes are excluded from the numerator. Both thermal and nonthermal particles contribute to their respective densities, while bound electrons are excluded from the denominator. This quantity uses the separately volume-averaged operands of the local [hydrogen-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_hydrogen_density_to_electron_density); it is not the unweighted volume average of that local ratio. Its electron denominator also distinguishes it from the [protium fraction among hydrogenic ions](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_hydrogen_density_to_volume_averaged_total_hydrogenic_density). $$a/b$$ The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_ratio_of_lithium_density_to_electron_density

description before

Ratio of lithium ion number density to free-electron number density, averaged over the plasma volume at the time of peak electron density.

description after

Ratio of charge-state-summed lithium-ion density to free-electron density, volume averaged over confined plasma at the time of peak electron density.

documentation before

Ratio of lithium ion number density to free-electron number density, averaged over the plasma volume, expresses the mean lithium ion abundance relative to free electrons. It is evaluated at the time of peak electron density. The local [lithium-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_lithium_density_to_electron_density) is $c_\mathrm{Li}=n_\mathrm{Li}/n_e$, where $n_\mathrm{Li}$ is the local lithium ion number density and $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density. Its volume average is defined by $$ \langle c_\mathrm{Li}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V c_\mathrm{Li}\,dV $$ where $\langle c_\mathrm{Li}\rangle_V$ is the volume-averaged ratio, $V$ denotes the plasma integration region and its volume, and $dV$ is a volume element. All densities and the integration volume refer to the same evaluation time. Lithium density is summed over all ionic charge states, from singly ionized through fully stripped lithium; neutral lithium is excluded. The electron density must be nonzero wherever the local ratio is evaluated. This quantity is the volume average of the local ratio, not the ratio of separately volume-averaged densities, and it is not a time average. The [hydrogen counterpart](name:volume_averaged_ratio_of_hydrogen_density_to_electron_density) applies the same volume averaging and peak-electron-density evaluation convention with hydrogen ion density in the numerator. $$a/b$$

documentation after

Ratio of lithium-ion number density to free-electron number density, formed locally and then volume averaged over the confined plasma inside the last closed flux surface. The quantity is evaluated at the time of peak electron density, rather than averaged over time. Its defining relation is $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{Li}}/n_e\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V \frac{n_{\mathrm{Li}}}{n_e}\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Li}}$ is the local lithium-ion number density, $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density, $V$ is the nonzero confined plasma volume at the evaluation time, $dV$ is a volume element, and the angle-bracketed expression is the volume-averaged lithium abundance relative to electrons. The electron density must be nonzero wherever the local ratio is evaluated. The lithium density is summed over all positive charge states, excluding neutral lithium. Both thermal and nonthermal particles contribute. Each lithium ion counts once without electric-charge or mass weighting, so the ratio expresses particle abundance rather than lithium's contribution to charge density. This quantity averages the local [lithium-to-electron density ratio](name:ratio_of_lithium_density_to_electron_density). It is not generally equal to the ratio of [volume-averaged lithium density](name:volume_averaged_lithium_density) to volume-averaged electron density, because averaging and division do not generally commute. The ratio is one quantity divided by another. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_runaway_electron_critical_electric_field

description before

Plasma-volume average of the critical parallel electric-field magnitude required for sustained runaway-electron acceleration against collisional drag.

description after

Plasma-volume-averaged parallel electric-field threshold above which sufficiently energetic electrons can sustain acceleration against relativistic collisional drag.

documentation before

This quantity is the unweighted plasma-volume average of the local threshold parallel electric-field magnitude for sustained runaway-electron acceleration against collisional drag. The volume average of the [runaway-electron critical electric field](name:critical_electric_field) is defined by $$ \langle E_c\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V E_c\,dV $$ where $E_c$ is the local critical parallel electric-field magnitude, $V$ is the plasma volume, $dV$ is a spatial volume element, and $\langle E_c\rangle_V$ is the resulting volume average. The average is weighted by spatial volume, not by electron density. It averages the threshold magnitude rather than the actual electric field and does not replace the local criterion for sustained runaway acceleration. This threshold concerns sustained acceleration of sufficiently energetic electrons. It is distinct from the [volume-averaged Dreicer field](name:volume_averaged_parallel_electric_field_due_to_dreicer), which characterizes electric acceleration relative to collisional drag on thermal electrons.

documentation after

The plasma-volume-averaged critical electric field characterizes the threshold for electric acceleration to overcome relativistic collisional drag on sufficiently energetic electrons. The averaging applies to the local [critical electric-field magnitude](name:critical_electric_field) throughout the plasma volume, not to the actual accelerating electric field. The local classical Connor–Hastie threshold in a fully ionized plasma is $$ E_c = \frac{n_e e^3 \ln\Lambda}{4\pi\varepsilon_0^2 m_e c^2} $$ where $E_c$ is the local critical electric-field magnitude, $n_e$ is the electron number density, $e$ is the elementary-charge magnitude, $\ln\Lambda$ is the Coulomb logarithm, $\varepsilon_0$ is the vacuum permittivity, $m_e$ is the electron rest mass, and $c$ is the speed of light. This classical threshold concerns collisional drag and does not include additional radiation-reaction losses. Its plasma-volume average is a global summary of local thresholds, not a criterion that guarantees runaway acceleration everywhere in the plasma. It differs from the [volume-averaged Dreicer field](name:volume_averaged_parallel_electric_field_due_to_dreicer), which characterizes electric acceleration competing with Coulomb drag on bulk thermal electrons rather than the relativistic drag limit. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_runaway_electron_density

description before

Mean number density of runaway electrons over the confined plasma volume, obtained by averaging the local suprathermal population that remains in the accelerating tail.

description after

Number density of runaway electrons averaged over the plasma volume, counting the suprathermal population for which electric-field acceleration can overcome collisional drag.

documentation before

Volume-averaged runaway electron density is the spatial mean of the local runaway-electron population over the confined plasma volume. Runaway electrons are suprathermal electrons whose momentum makes collisional slowing-down ineffective compared with continued electromagnetic acceleration, as described by [runaway_electron_density](name:runaway_electron_density). The volume average is defined by $$ \overline{n}_\mathrm{RE} = \frac{1}{V_\mathrm{p}} \int_{V_\mathrm{p}} n_\mathrm{RE}(\mathbf{r})\,\mathrm{d}V $$ where $\overline{n}_\mathrm{RE}$ is the volume-averaged runaway electron density, $n_\mathrm{RE}(\mathbf{r})$ is the local runaway electron density at position $\mathbf{r}$, $V_\mathrm{p}$ is the confined plasma volume, and $\mathrm{d}V$ is the physical volume element. This quantity averages the local density rather than reporting the total runaway-electron count; the latter is obtained by multiplying the average by $V_\mathrm{p}$. It includes the full confined plasma volume and excludes regions outside that volume.

documentation after

Number density of runaway electron. The runaway-electron inventory in the averaging volume satisfies $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $N_{e,\mathrm{RE}}$ is the total number of runaway electrons within the averaging volume, $V$ is that nonzero plasma volume, $n_{e,\mathrm{RE}}$ is the local [runaway-electron density](name:runaway_electron_density), and $\langle n_{e,\mathrm{RE}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged value. The averaging domain is the stated plasma volume, not only the region occupied by runaway electrons. All runaway electrons within that volume contribute, regardless of their generation mechanism; nonrunaway electrons are excluded. Each particle counts once, without electric-charge, kinetic-energy, or velocity weighting. For the same averaging volume, this quantity is the runaway-population contribution to the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density), which also includes nonrunaway free electrons. It differs from the [volume-averaged runaway-electron kinetic-energy density](name:volume_averaged_runaway_electron_kinetic_energy_density), which weights the runaway population by particle kinetic energy rather than counting particles. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_runaway_electron_kinetic_energy_density

description before

Mean relativistic kinetic energy per unit volume of the runaway electron population, obtained by averaging the local kinetic energy density over the confined plasma volume.

description after

Relativistic kinetic energy density of runaway electrons averaged over the full plasma volume, excluding rest-mass energy.

documentation before

The volume-averaged runaway electron kinetic energy density is a global scalar characterizing the mean suprathermal energy stored per unit volume in the runaway electron (RE) population. It is obtained by integrating the local relativistic kinetic energy density over the total confined plasma volume $V$ and dividing by $V$. The underlying local quantity is the relativistic kinetic energy moment of the RE distribution function: $$ u_\mathrm{RE}(\mathbf{r}) = \int f_\mathrm{RE}(\mathbf{r}, \mathbf{p})\,(\gamma - 1)\,m_e c^2 \, d^3p $$ where $f_\mathrm{RE}$ is the runaway electron phase-space distribution function, $\gamma = (1 + p^2 / m_e^2 c^2)^{1/2}$ is the Lorentz factor, $m_e$ is the electron rest mass, $c$ is the speed of light, and $\mathbf{p}$ is the relativistic momentum. The volume average is then $\langle u_\mathrm{RE} \rangle = V^{-1} \int_V u_\mathrm{RE}\,dV$. This quantity is evaluated from kinetic or reduced-fluid runaway electron models, particularly in disruption current-quench simulations where RE populations can reach energies of several to tens of MeV per particle. Multiplying by the plasma volume $V$ recovers the total stored relativistic energy given by [runaway_electron_kinetic_energy](name:runaway_electron_kinetic_energy). The local non-averaged counterpart is runaway_electron_kinetic_energy_density, and the corresponding global particle number density is [volume_averaged_runaway_electron_density](name:volume_averaged_runaway_electron_density).

documentation after

Number density of runaway electron. The corresponding total [runaway-electron kinetic energy](name:runaway_electron_kinetic_energy) satisfies: $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $W_{\mathrm{RE}}$ is the total runaway-electron kinetic energy within the plasma volume, $V$ is that volume, $w_{\mathrm{RE}}$ is the local [runaway-electron kinetic energy density](name:runaway_electron_kinetic_energy_density), and $\langle w_{\mathrm{RE}}\rangle_V$ is its plasma-volume average. The averaging domain is the entire plasma volume, including regions without runaway electrons, rather than only the volume occupied by the runaway-electron population. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_runaway_electron_pitch_angle

Volume-averaged mean pitch angle (v-parallel/|v|) of the runaway electron distribution

volume_averaged_runaway_electron_source_rate

description before

Volume-averaged production rate of runaway-electron number density over the confined plasma, including all active generation mechanisms and excluding runaway-electron losses.

description after

Volume-averaged production rate of runaway-electron number density over the confined plasma, including all active generation mechanisms and excluding runaway-electron losses.

documentation before

This quantity is the confined-plasma volume average of the local runaway-electron number-density production rate. It represents a production contribution to the runaway-electron balance, not a loss rate. The volume average is defined by $$ \langle S_{\rm RE} \rangle_V = \frac{1}{V_c} \int_{V_c} S_{\rm RE}(\mathbf{x})\,dV $$ where $S_{\rm RE}(\mathbf{x})$ is the local volumetric source rate, $V_c$ is the confined-plasma volume, $\mathbf{x}$ is position, and $dV$ is the volume element. The integrand is the total [runaway electron source rate](name:runaway_electron_source_rate), including all active generation mechanisms. Sink and loss terms are excluded. This quantity is distinct from the volume-averaged runaway-electron density, which describes the population rather than its production rate.

documentation after

This quantity is the confined-plasma volume average of the local runaway-electron number-density production rate. It represents a production contribution to the runaway-electron balance, not a loss rate. The volume average is defined by $$ \langle S_{\rm RE} \rangle_V = \frac{1}{V_c} \int_{V_c} S_{\rm RE}(\mathbf{x})\,dV $$ where $S_{\rm RE}(\mathbf{x})$ is the local volumetric source rate, $V_c$ is the confined-plasma volume, $\mathbf{x}$ is position, and $dV$ is the volume element. The integrand is the total [runaway electron source rate](name:runaway_electron_source_rate), including all active generation mechanisms. Sink and loss terms are excluded. This quantity is distinct from the volume-averaged runaway-electron density, which describes the population rather than its production rate.

volume_averaged_runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_compton_scattering

description before

Plasma-volume-averaged source rate of electrons entering the runaway population after receiving energy from gamma-ray photons through Compton scattering.

description after

Plasma-volume-averaged Compton source rate counts electrons entering the runaway population through energy transfer from gamma-ray photons, averaged over the plasma volume.

documentation before

Plasma-volume-averaged runaway-electron source rate due to Compton scattering is the mean volumetric production rate associated with energy transfer from gamma-ray photons to plasma electrons. It counts energetic recoil electrons that enter the runaway regime, where electric-field acceleration overcomes collisional drag. The volume average is defined by: $$ \langle S_{\mathrm C}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V S_{\mathrm C}\,dV $$ where $S_{\mathrm C}$ is the [local runaway-electron source rate due to Compton scattering](name:runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_compton_scattering), $\langle S_{\mathrm C}\rangle_V$ is its plasma-volume average, $V$ is the plasma volume and integration domain, and $dV$ is an infinitesimal volume element. The average covers the entire plasma and uses volume weighting only, not electron-density weighting. It remains a production rate per unit volume, distinct from the volume-integrated electron production rate. This contribution forms part of the [volume-averaged total runaway-electron source rate](name:volume_averaged_total_runaway_electron_source_rate), but excludes contributions assigned to other generation mechanisms. It is a generation term, not the net rate of change of runaway-electron density after transport and losses.

documentation after

Plasma-volume-averaged Compton source rate describes runaway-electron generation initiated when gamma-ray photons transfer sufficient energy to plasma electrons through Compton scattering. Applying the [volume average](concept:volume_averaged) over the plasma volume to the [local Compton source rate](name:runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_compton_scattering) gives $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $S_{\mathrm C}$ is the local Compton contribution to runaway-electron production, $n_{\mathrm{RE}}$ is the runaway-electron number density, $t$ is time, and $V$ is the plasma volume. Angle brackets denote the volume average, and the restriction $\mathrm C$ selects only the Compton generation contribution. Only electrons entering the runaway population are counted, not all Compton recoil electrons. The quantity remains a volumetric source rate, rather than a volume-integrated production rate or a net density-change rate including transport and losses. This contribution enters the [plasma-volume-averaged total runaway-electron source rate](name:volume_averaged_total_runaway_electron_source_rate) alongside the separately attributed generation mechanisms. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_dreicer

description before

Plasma-volume-averaged source rate of runaway electrons generated from the thermal tail by magnetic-field-parallel electric acceleration below the Dreicer field.

description after

Plasma-volume-averaged Dreicer source rate counts runaway electrons produced from the thermal distribution's suprathermal tail by parallel electric acceleration below the Dreicer field.

documentation before

Plasma-volume-averaged runaway-electron source rate due to Dreicer generation is the mean volumetric production rate from thermal-tail electrons accelerated into the runaway population by the magnetic-field-parallel electric field. The sub-critical electric fields considered here are below the [Dreicer field](name:parallel_electric_field_due_to_dreicer), which characterizes competition between electric acceleration and collisional drag on thermal electrons. The plasma-volume average is defined by $$ \langle S_{\mathrm D}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V S_{\mathrm D}\,dV $$ where $S_{\mathrm D}$ is the local [runaway-electron source rate due to Dreicer generation](name:runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_dreicer), $V$ is the plasma volume, $dV$ is a differential volume element, and $\langle S_{\mathrm D}\rangle_V$ is the resulting volume average. The average covers the full plasma volume with no additional density weighting. It remains a volumetric source rate; multiplication by the plasma volume gives the total number of runaway electrons produced per unit time through Dreicer generation. This quantity is the Dreicer contribution to the [volume-averaged total runaway-electron source rate](name:volume_averaged_total_runaway_electron_source_rate). It excludes avalanche, hot-tail, Compton, and other generation mechanisms, and it is not the net density-change rate after transport and losses.

documentation after

Plasma-volume-averaged Dreicer source rate counts electrons entering the runaway population through primary electric acceleration from the thermal distribution's suprathermal tail. The accelerating electric field is parallel to the magnetic field and has magnitude below the [Dreicer field](name:parallel_electric_field_due_to_dreicer). The local source entering this average is defined by: $$ S_{\mathrm D}=\left.\frac{\partial n_{\mathrm{RE}}}{\partial t}\right|_{\mathrm D} $$ where $S_{\mathrm D}$ is the local [runaway-electron source rate due to Dreicer generation](name:runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_dreicer), $n_{\mathrm{RE}}$ is runaway-electron number density, $t$ is time, and the subscript $\mathrm D$ restricts the derivative to Dreicer production. The averaging region is the plasma volume, without density or current weighting. The result remains a volumetric production rate, not a volume-integrated particle production rate or a net density derivative including transport and losses. This source contributes to the [volume-averaged total runaway-electron source rate](name:volume_averaged_total_runaway_electron_source_rate), but excludes avalanche multiplication, hot-tail generation, Compton scattering, and tritium-decay generation. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_hot_tail

description before

Plasma-volume-averaged hot-tail source rate of runaway electrons produced from a suprathermal tail that survives rapid cooling of the thermal electron population.

description after

Plasma-volume-averaged hot-tail source rate describes runaway-electron production from a suprathermal electron tail surviving rapid bulk-electron cooling.

documentation before

Plasma-volume-averaged hot-tail runaway-electron source rate is the volumetric production rate averaged over the plasma volume. It describes runaway generation from a suprathermal electron tail whose collisional relaxation is slower than the cooling of the thermal population. The unweighted volume average is defined by $$ \langle S_{\mathrm{ht}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V S_{\mathrm{ht}}\,dV $$ where $S_{\mathrm{ht}}$ is the local [hot-tail runaway-electron source rate](name:runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_hot_tail), $V$ is the plasma volume over which the integral is taken, $dV$ is the differential volume element, and $\langle S_{\mathrm{ht}}\rangle_V$ is the resulting plasma-volume average. Only hot-tail production is included; Dreicer generation, avalanche multiplication, and other generation mechanisms are excluded. This quantity is an average production rate per unit volume, not a volume-integrated electron production rate or a net density-change rate including losses. It is the hot-tail contribution to the [plasma-volume-averaged total runaway-electron source rate](name:volume_averaged_total_runaway_electron_source_rate).

documentation after

Plasma-volume-averaged hot-tail runaway-electron source rate quantifies generation from a high-energy electron tail that persists during rapid cooling of the bulk electrons. These tail electrons relax collisionally more slowly than the bulk cools, allowing some to enter the runaway population through electric-field acceleration. The local source entering this average is defined by: $$ S_{\mathrm{ht}} = \left.\frac{\partial n_{\mathrm{RE}}}{\partial t}\right|_{\mathrm{ht}} $$ where $S_{\mathrm{ht}}$ is the local [hot-tail runaway-electron source rate](name:runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_hot_tail), $n_{\mathrm{RE}}$ is the [runaway-electron number density](name:runaway_electron_density), $t$ is time, and the subscript $\mathrm{ht}$ selects production by the hot-tail mechanism rather than the complete density evolution. The quantity applies to the entire plasma volume without density weighting. It is an averaged volumetric source, not the volume-integrated electron production rate. It supplies the hot-tail contribution to the [volume-averaged total runaway-electron source rate](name:volume_averaged_total_runaway_electron_source_rate); other generation mechanisms, transport, and loss terms are excluded. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_time_derivative_of_electron_density

description before

Rate of change of the electron number density averaged over the confined plasma volume, with positive values indicating increasing mean density.

description after

Volume-averaged rate of local free-electron density change inside the last closed flux surface, including thermal and nonthermal electrons.

documentation before

This quantity is the temporal rate of change of the electron number density after volume averaging over the confined plasma volume. Positive values indicate increasing mean electron density, while negative values indicate decreasing mean electron density. For a fixed averaging volume, the defining relation is $$ \frac{d\langle n_e\rangle_V}{dt} = \frac{1}{V}\int_V \frac{\partial n_e}{\partial t}\,dV $$ where $n_e$ is the local electron number density, $V$ is the confined plasma volume, and $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density). The volume-average operation is applied to the electron density before time differentiation. This distinguishes the quantity from the more general [time derivative of electron density](name:time_derivative_of_electron_density), which does not specify volume averaging.

documentation after

Number density of electron. For a fixed averaging volume, the relation between this quantity and the local [electron density](name:electron_density) is: $$\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}$$ Here, $n_e$ is the local free-electron number density, $t$ is time, $V$ is the nonzero confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface, $dV$ is a volume element, and $\langle n_e\rangle_V$ is the [volume-averaged electron density](name:volume_averaged_electron_density). The local time derivative is evaluated at each fixed spatial position before the [volume average](concept:volume_averaged). Each electron contributes one particle, without charge or mass weighting. When the averaging volume changes with time, differentiating its mean also includes changes to the integration region and to $V$; in that case, the time derivative of the mean is not in general equal to the volume average of the local time derivative. This quantity is volume-averaged, unlike the line-averaged [time derivative of electron density](name:time_derivative_of_electron_density). Sign convention: Positive when the volume-averaged free-electron number density increases with time. The time derivative is the rate of change of a quantity with time at a fixed point or label. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_total_ion_density

description before

Volume-averaged total ion number density over the confined plasma volume, summing all ion species and charge states and including thermal and non-thermal ion populations.

description after

Number density of ions summed over all species and positive charge states, including thermal and nonthermal ions but excluding neutrals, averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface.

documentation before

Volume-averaged total ion density is the aggregate ion number density averaged over the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface. It includes all ion species, resolved charge states, and thermal and non-thermal ion populations; it excludes electrons and neutral particles. The volume average is defined by $$ \overline{n}_{i,\mathrm{total}} = \frac{1}{V_p}\int_{V_p} n_{i,\mathrm{total}}(\mathbf{x})\,dV $$ where $\overline{n}_{i,\mathrm{total}}$ is the volume-averaged density, $n_{i,\mathrm{total}}(\mathbf{x})$ is the local [total ion density](name:total_ion_number_density) in m$^{-3}$, $V_p$ is the confined plasma volume in m$^3$, $\mathbf{x}$ is position, and $dV$ is the volume element. This is a density average, not the total ion inventory. Unlike [volume-averaged ion density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density), it aggregates all ion species rather than one selected species.

documentation after

Number density of ion. Its relationship to the [volume-averaged density of a selected ion species](name:volume_averaged_ion_density) is $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $\langle n_{i,\mathrm{tot}}\rangle_V$ is the volume-averaged total ion number density, $s$ labels an ion species, $n_s$ is that species' local number density summed over positive charge states, and $\langle n_s\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged number density. The subscript $i$ denotes ions, and $V$ is the same nonzero confined plasma volume for every term. All positive charge states contribute; the [ion charge-state density](name:ion_charge_state_density) instead resolves one species and charge state. Neutral particles and electrons are excluded. Both thermal and nonthermal ions contribute, unlike the thermal-only population counted by [total thermal ion density](name:total_thermal_ion_density). Each ion counts once without electric-charge or mass weighting, so this quantity is neither charge density nor mass density. Multiplying this density by the averaging volume gives the total number of ions within that volume. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_total_ion_temperature

description before

Density-weighted effective temperature of the combined thermal ion population, with all included species and charge states contributing by number density and averaged over the confined plasma volume.

description after

Temperature of thermal ions from random motion, density-weighted locally over all species and charge states and volume-averaged inside the last closed magnetic flux surface.

documentation before

The volume-averaged total ion temperature is the volume average of the local density-weighted effective temperature of the combined thermal ion population, including all included thermal ion species and charge states. It represents random thermal motion and excludes directed bulk-flow energy. The volume average is defined by $$ \overline{T}_{i,\mathrm{tot}} = \frac{\int_V T_{i,\mathrm{tot}}(\mathbf{r})\,dV}{\int_V dV} $$ where $V$ is the confined plasma volume, $dV$ is its differential volume element, $\mathbf{r}$ is position, and $T_{i,\mathrm{tot}}(\mathbf{r})$ is the local quantity defined by [total_ion_temperature](name:total_ion_temperature). Species and charge-state density weighting is performed locally before the volume averaging. Unlike [line_averaged_total_ion_temperature](name:line_averaged_total_ion_temperature), this quantity averages over volume rather than along a plasma chord.

documentation after

Temperature of the combined thermal ion population averaged over the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed magnetic flux surface describes its thermal state across the confined plasma. The local temperature represents random translational motion and excludes directed bulk-flow energy. The local quantity being averaged is the [total ion temperature](name:total_ion_temperature), defined by $$ T_{i,\mathrm{tot}} = \frac{\sum_{s,q} n_{s,q}T_{s,q}}{\sum_{s,q} n_{s,q}} $$ where $T_{i,\mathrm{tot}}$ is the local effective temperature of the combined thermal ion population, $s$ labels ion species, $q$ labels ionic charge states, $n_{s,q}$ is the corresponding number density, and $T_{s,q}$ is the corresponding local temperature. Both sums include all thermal ion species and charge states in the population. The number-density weighting combines populations locally; it is not an additional spatial weight. Unlike [volume-averaged ion temperature](name:volume_averaged_ion_temperature), which applies to one specified ion species, this quantity combines all included ion species. It is a temperature rather than a volume-integrated thermal energy, and its averaging domain excludes plasma outside the last closed magnetic flux surface. The [line-averaged total ion temperature](name:line_averaged_total_ion_temperature) uses the same local population aggregate but averages along a specified plasma chord rather than throughout the enclosed plasma volume. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_total_runaway_electron_source_rate

description before

Plasma-volume-averaged volumetric production rate of runaway electrons, summed over Dreicer, avalanche, hot-tail, Compton, and tritium-decay generation mechanisms.

description after

Plasma-volume-averaged total runaway-electron source rate counts electrons entering the runaway population through all active generation mechanisms.

documentation before

Plasma-volume-averaged volumetric production rate of runaway electrons is the spatial average, over the plasma volume, of the local total rate at which electrons enter the runaway population. The total rate includes generation by Dreicer, avalanche, hot-tail, Compton, and tritium-decay mechanisms. The volume average is defined by $$ \left\langle S_{\mathrm{RE,total}}\right\rangle_V = \frac{1}{|\Omega_p|}\int_{\Omega_p} S_{\mathrm{RE,total}}(\mathbf{x})\,dV $$ where $\left\langle S_{\mathrm{RE,total}}\right\rangle_V$ is the volume-averaged total runaway electron source rate, $S_{\mathrm{RE,total}}(\mathbf{x})$ is the total runaway electron source rate at position $\mathbf{x}$, $\Omega_p$ is the plasma-volume domain, $|\Omega_p|$ is the volume measure of that domain, and $dV$ is the volume element. This quantity applies to the runaway-electron population within the plasma volume and uses an unweighted volume average of the total local volumetric production rate. The mechanism sum is part of the quantity definition; the total includes component production rates such as the [Dreicer runaway electron source rate](name:runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_dreicer) and the [hot-tail runaway electron source rate](name:runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_hot_tail), but is not restricted to either component. It is the plasma-volume-averaged counterpart of the total runaway electron source rate. It is a production-rate density averaged over volume, not the absolute number of runaway electrons and not a current-density measure; in particular it is distinct from the [volume-averaged parallel runaway electron current density](name:volume_averaged_parallel_runaway_electron_current_density), which describes charge transport by the runaway population.

documentation after

Plasma-volume-averaged total runaway-electron source rate quantifies the generation contribution to the change in runaway-electron number density. The averaging region is the plasma volume, and the total includes all active runaway-generation mechanisms. The quantity being averaged is the [total runaway-electron source rate](name:total_runaway_electron_source_rate), with mechanism aggregation defined by $$ S_{\mathrm{tot}} = \sum_k S_k $$ where $S_{\mathrm{tot}}$ is the local total volumetric runaway-electron source rate, $S_k$ is the local volumetric contribution from generation mechanism $k$, and $k$ ranges over all active mechanisms. These include Dreicer generation, avalanche multiplication, hot-tail generation, Compton scattering, and tritium decay. The result remains a volumetric production rate, distinct from the volume-integrated particle production rate. It describes generation rather than the full density evolution when transport or runaway-electron losses are present. The [volume-averaged hot-tail source rate](name:volume_averaged_runaway_electron_source_rate_due_to_hot_tail) is one mechanism-specific contribution to this total. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

volume_averaged_total_xenon_fraction

description before

Volume-averaged density of xenon summed over all charge states divided by volume-averaged electron density.

description after

Volume-averaged density of xenon summed over all charge states divided by volume-averaged electron density.

documentation before

documentation after

volume_averaged_tritium_density

description before

Volume-averaged number density of tritium ions over the confined plasma volume, obtained from the local charge-state-summed fuel-ion density.

description after

Number density of tritium ions summed over positive charge states, excluding neutral atoms, averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface.

documentation before

Volume-averaged tritium ion number density quantifies the mean concentration of hydrogen-3 fuel ions throughout the confined plasma volume. It is the spatial average of the local [tritium_density](name:tritium_density), rather than a chord average such as [line_averaged_tritium_density](name:line_averaged_tritium_density). $$ \overline{n}_T = \frac{1}{V_c}\int_{V_c} n_T\,dV $$ where $\overline{n}_T$ is the volume-averaged tritium ion number density, $n_T$ is the local tritium ion number density, $V_c$ is the confined plasma volume bounded by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is the volume element. The tritium ion density is summed over all ionic charge states and excludes neutral tritium. This quantity provides the tritium-density basis for volume-averaged isotopic ratios and is distinct from the local boundary value [tritium_density_at_plasma_boundary](name:tritium_density_at_plasma_boundary).

documentation after

Number density of tritium. The average determines the tritium-ion inventory through $$\langle f\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V f\,dV$$ where $\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{T}}$ is the total number of tritium ions within the averaging volume, $V$ is the confined plasma volume, $n_{\mathrm{T}}$ is the local [tritium density](name:tritium_density), and $\langle n_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged value. This is a single-species particle number density, not an all-species total, electric charge density, or mass density. It is a volume mean rather than a local value or a chord mean. It is the tritium-specific instance of [volume-averaged ion density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density). The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

volume_averaged_tungsten_density

description before

Volume-averaged number density of positively charged tungsten ions within the plasma enclosed by the last closed flux surface, summed over all ionization states.

description after

Number density of tungsten ions in all positive charge states, excluding neutral atoms, averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface.

documentation before

Volume-averaged tungsten-ion density is the spatial mean of the local charge-state-summed density tungsten-ion density over the plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface. It includes all positive tungsten ionization states and excludes neutral tungsten. The volume average is defined by $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{W}} \rangle_V = \frac{1}{V_{\mathrm{LCFS}}}\int_{V_{\mathrm{LCFS}}} n_{\mathrm{W}}(\mathbf{r})\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm{W}}(\mathbf{r})$ is the local tungsten-ion density, $V_{\mathrm{LCFS}}$ is the enclosed plasma volume, $\mathbf{r}$ denotes position, and $dV$ is the volume element. The local density is the sum of the densities of all tungsten charge states from W$^{1+}$ through W$^{74+}$. This quantity is distinct from the boundary flux-surface-averaged tungsten density and from the impurity-abundance [ratio of volume-averaged tungsten density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_tungsten_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density), which is normalized to electron density.

documentation after

Number density of tungsten. Its relation to the local [tungsten density](name:tungsten_density) is $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{W}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{\mathrm{W}}\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm{W}}$ is the local tungsten-ion number density, $\langle n_{\mathrm{W}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged number density, $V$ is the nonzero confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is a volume element. Both thermal and nonthermal tungsten ions contribute. Each ion contributes one particle, without electric-charge or mass weighting. This is a single-species number density, not an all-species total, charge density, or mass density. Its volume weighting distinguishes it from [line-averaged tungsten density](name:line_averaged_tungsten_density), which averages along a specified chord. This quantity is the tungsten-specific instance of [volume-averaged ion density](name:volume_averaged_ion_density). Multiplying it by the confined plasma volume gives the number of tungsten ions within that volume. It is also the numerator of the [ratio of volume-averaged tungsten density to volume-averaged electron density](name:ratio_of_volume_averaged_tungsten_density_to_volume_averaged_electron_density). The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.

volume_averaged_xenon_density

description before

Number density of xenon ions averaged over the full plasma volume, including all charged xenon ionization states and excluding neutral xenon.

description after

Number density of xenon ions summed over all charge states, excluding neutral atoms, and averaged over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface.

documentation before

The volume-averaged xenon ion number density is the plasma-wide mean of the local [xenon_density](name:xenon_density), which sums xenon ions from singly ionized through fully stripped xenon and excludes neutral atoms. $$ \overline{n}_{\mathrm{Xe}} = \frac{1}{V_p}\int_{V_p} n_{\mathrm{Xe}}(\mathbf{r})\,dV $$ where $\overline{n}_{\mathrm{Xe}}$ is the volume-averaged xenon ion density, $n_{\mathrm{Xe}}(\mathbf{r})$ is the local xenon ion density at position $\mathbf{r}$, $V_p$ is the full plasma volume, and $dV$ is the physical volume element. The averaging is over the plasma volume rather than along a measurement chord or at a single locus. The resulting global impurity-content measure can be compared with electron content through the ratio of volume-averaged xenon density to volume-averaged electron density.

documentation after

Number density of xenon. Its relation to the local [xenon density](name:xenon_density) is $$ \langle n_{\mathrm{Xe}}\rangle_V = \frac{1}{V}\int_V n_{\mathrm{Xe}}\,dV $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Xe}}$ is the local xenon-ion number density, $\langle n_{\mathrm{Xe}}\rangle_V$ is its volume-averaged value, $V$ is the confined plasma volume enclosed by the last closed flux surface, and $dV$ is a volume element. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded. Both thermal and nonthermal xenon ions contribute. Each ion contributes one particle without weighting by electric charge or mass. This is a xenon-only particle number density, not an all-species total, electric charge density, or mass density. It is a volume mean rather than a local value or a chord mean. Multiplying this average by the confined plasma volume gives the total number of xenon ions within that volume. The [volume-averaged argon density](name:volume_averaged_argon_density) applies the same averaging definition to argon instead of xenon. The volume average is the integral of a quantity over a stated volume divided by that volume.

xenon_density

description before

Number density of xenon ions at a given point in the plasma, summed over all ionization states from singly ionized through fully stripped xenon, excluding neutral xenon atoms.

description after

Number density of xenon ions across all positive charge states, excluding neutral xenon, expressing their concentration per physical plasma volume.

documentation before

Xenon is a high-$Z$ noble-gas impurity (atomic number $Z_\mathrm{Xe} = 54$) introduced into fusion plasmas for radiative-mantle experiments, impurity-transport studies, and disruption mitigation. The xenon ion number density $n_\mathrm{Xe}$ quantifies the local concentration of all charged xenon species and enters the plasma effective charge through $Z_\mathrm{eff} = \sum_i Z_i^2 n_i / n_e$, where $n_i$ is the partial density of ion species $i$ and $n_e$ is the electron density. The total xenon ion density is the sum over all ionic charge states: $$ n_\mathrm{Xe} = \sum_{z=1}^{54} n_{\mathrm{Xe}^{z+}} $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Xe}^{z+}}$ is the partial number density of xenon ions carrying charge $ze$. The charge-state distribution is determined by coronal equilibrium or collisional-radiative modeling at the local electron temperature and density. The xenon density profile is inferred from vacuum-ultraviolet or soft-X-ray line emission via spectroscopy and tomographic inversion, or from charge-exchange recombination spectroscopy using neutral-beam injection. Spatially resolved forms include [xenon_density_at_magnetic_axis](name:xenon_density_at_magnetic_axis) at the innermost flux surface and xenon_density_at_pedestal in the edge transport barrier, while [volume_averaged_xenon_density](name:volume_averaged_xenon_density) provides the global impurity content figure of merit. The dimensionless impurity fraction relative to electron density is given by xenon_fraction.

documentation after

Number density of xenon. The corresponding local density is defined by $$ n_{\mathrm{Xe}} = \int f_{\mathrm{Xe}}(\mathbf{v})\,d^3v $$ where $n_{\mathrm{Xe}}$ is the local xenon-ion number density, $f_{\mathrm{Xe}}$ is the velocity-space number distribution of the counted xenon-ion population, $\mathbf{v}$ is ion velocity, and $d^3v$ is the velocity-space volume element. The integral covers all velocities. No particular location or spatial averaging operation is specified. [Xenon density at the plasma boundary](name:xenon_density_at_plasma_boundary) selects a local boundary value. [Line-averaged xenon density](name:line_averaged_xenon_density) averages this concentration along a specified plasma chord, whereas [volume-averaged xenon density](name:volume_averaged_xenon_density) averages it over the confined plasma volume inside the last closed flux surface. These averages remain number densities, distinct from line-integrated density or a total ion count. The density counts ions summed over all charge states; neutral atoms are excluded.
